Verified state: v2's `ensureDateChain` (`workspace-client.ts:1743-1776`) already authors `presentAsMain: true` on every new chain node — the only chain-creation path in this repo — and both live workspaces are clean (987 date nodes, all bitted; the second workspace has none). Deliverables anyway, per owner: **`scripts/migrate-date-pages-main.mts`** — scans every workspace's derived DB for active date-shape ids (`…00bb-YYYY…` year / `…00aa-YYYYMM…` month / `…00dd-YYYYMMDD…` day, the deterministic `dates.ts` shapes) with `present_as_main = 0` and appends `object.update {presentAsMain: true}` envelopes through the relay `/batch` ingest (immutable log untouched, every replica converges; the `migrate-mention-texts.mts` pattern verbatim). Ran against the live server: `done: 0/0 date nodes fixed` — idempotent no-op confirmed. Both containers healthy; `scripts/screenshots/verify-min.mjs` **VERIFY-PASS** (clean boot, footer "Sync: idle", UI search hit, zero console errors). ### 34.39 Editor atom pill — v1 selected look (owner request 2026-10-03 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner request ("i want v1 look … implement the full editor alignment"): the selected node-link pill in the block editor must look like v1's — reaching it with the caret (or clicking it in edit mode) selects it **with an outline**, and clicking toggles selection rather than navigating. Functionally v2 was already at parity — §34.22 E1/E2 shipped the full v1 gesture set (first click selects with caret placement blocked, second click places the caret before/after by half, arrows select when the caret reaches a boundary, dblclick/Enter open, Backspace/Delete atomic, any other key clears the flash) — so the delta was purely visual: v2's selected state was a `--color-primary-container` background fill, v1's is primary text on an 8% primary tint inside a focus ring.

**Archive mining (standing rule):** v1's `frontend/src/styles/inline-link.css:60-68` @ `v1-archive` — `.inline-link-wrapper--selected .inline-link-inner`: `color: var(--color-primary)`, primary underline color, `background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-primary) 8%, transparent)`, `--shape-extra-small` radius, `padding: 0 var(--spacing-micro)` paired with `margin: 0 calc(var(--spacing-micro) * -1)` (the tint and ring extend a micro beyond the text without shifting the line's layout), `box-shadow: var(--shadow-focus-ring-primary)`. Ported near-verbatim; the only adaptation is structural — v1's style targeted an inner span inside a wrapper, v2's pill is a single `span.nt-atom` (the underline lives on `border-bottom` rather than v1's `text-decoration`, so selection recolors that border). The pad pair was carried over on day one and dropped the same day — see the fix below.

**Shipped:** `apps/web/src/ui/app.css` `.nt-atom--selected` (+ its `:hover` guard) — primary text, 8% primary tint, `--shape-extra-small` radius, primary focus-ring outline; the dashed underline hides on selection so the ring reads as one clean shape. Base and hover chrome untouched: the edit pill keeps mirroring the read-mode link look (the §34.22 "visually identical to read mode" invariant), and the selection state has no read-mode counterpart. Token-only per the UI law — every value resolves to `apps/web/src/ui/variables.css` (`--color-primary`, `--shape-extra-small`, `--shadow-focus-ring-primary`; the 8% mix uses the same `color-mix` pattern the token file itself uses). No TypeScript/test changes — selection state, gestures, and the `nt-atom--selected` class contract are unchanged. Two same-day owner-report follow-ups:

1. **Layout-jump fix** ("on highlight via caret, the node link jumps line", with a screenshot of a date pill fragmenting "2026/10/0|4" across lines): the pad pair carried over from v1 (`padding: 0 var(--spacing-micro)` + negative margin) made the selected pill +4px wider; on a tight line the delta found the `/` break opportunity inside the pill text and re-wrapped it exactly on selection. The selected state is now layout-neutral — no padding/margin (ring, tint, radius, colors affect paint only), so selection can never change wrapping.
2. **Underline hidden** ("hide the underline when a node link is selected"): `border-bottom-color: transparent` on the selected state (v1 recolored its `text-decoration` instead — the owner prefers it gone; owner call wins over archive fidelity).

**Verification:** `pnpm test` green repo-wide (one mid-session gate run showed 4 transient failures in `workspace-client.test.ts`'s icon tests while the parallel templates session was saving files mid-run — immediate re-run clean at 483/483 web, not reproducible); live probe `scripts/screenshots/pill-select-probe.mjs` against the deployed build — **PILL-PROBE-PASS** (ring present, primary text color via rgb-normalized compare, underline transparent, first click selects without navigating, ArrowRight clears the selection, row-fill click keeps focus with the caret after the pill); the probe's scratch fixture is trashed on exit. `verify-min.mjs` VERIFY-PASS after each deploy.

**Deploy addendum (owner "commit, push and deploy", 2026-10-03):** three host-built `notees-web:latest` deploys in this slice (v1 look → wrap fix → underline-hidden, the last also carrying §34.40's row fill), each `docker compose up -d` per the §34.38 follow-up (the TAG pins are gone from the stack env, so compose tracks the local build). The first deploy also carried the parallel session's uncommitted templates T3 work, per that session's own addendum precedent (green under build/test at deploy time). No git tag pushed — ghcr CI published nothing; the owner's read-only docker login untouched.

**Docs (same pass):** `docs/ux.md` mention row — the pill's selected look named (primary text on a faint primary tint inside a focus-ring outline). No SCHEMA.md delta (chrome-only, no wire/model touch); no AGENTS.md delta.

### 34.41 Editor rows span side to side (owner request 2026-10-03 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner request: "block rows should span side to side in their container, not adapt to the block content."

**Archive mining (standing rule):** v1's `.custom-inline-editor` (`frontend/src/features/editor/custom/components/CustomInlineEditor.css` @ `v1-archive`) is `flex: 1` — the editable fills the block's content column. v2's `.nt-block-text` was `display: inline-block`, hugging its text: in edit mode, clicking the empty right side of a row landed outside the contentEditable → blur → the block dropped out of edit instead of placing the caret. (The row chrome itself already spanned — `.nt-block-content` is `flex: 1`; only the editable element hugged.)

**Shipped:** `apps/web/src/ui/app.css` — `.nt-block-text { display: block }` plus `.nt-editor-root { display: block }` (the wrapper was `inline`, which would split anonymous boxes around its now-block child). Clicking anywhere on an editing row now places the caret at the nearest position, matching the archived editor's contract. The live probe asserts it: the editor's width equals the content column's width, a click at the right edge keeps focus, and the caret lands after the pill.

**Verification:** `pnpm test` green (web 483); the row-fill assertions ride `pill-select-probe.mjs` (**PILL-PROBE-PASS** on the deployed build).

### 34.40 Effective icons — display-time defaults (owner request 2026-10-03 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner request: "implement a default icon (display time, not stored) in effectiveIcon for classes that have no icon set. Same for main nodes/pages, use a page icon for example." The effective-icon chain (own icon → first class's chain icon, class order, extends-walked) previously ended in `null`, so iconless classes/pages rendered no glyph anywhere.

**Shipped:**

- **Domain** — new `packages/domain/src/icons.ts`: `DEFAULT_CLASS_ICON` (`mdi-shape-outline`), `DEFAULT_PAGE_ICON` (`mdi-file-document-outline` — the same glyph choices the command palette already used as its ad-hoc fallbacks), and `defaultIconFor(node)`, the render-state-aware tail of every effective-icon chain: class nodes → class glyph, document-chrome nodes → page glyph, **inline blocks → null** (their chrome is the bullet dot — a default there would erase the page/block distinction the render cascade exists to make).
- **Store (pre-existing bug the feature forced to surface)** — `upsertClassNode` (`appliers.ts:711`) routed create-time `icon`/`color` through its LWW-gated UPDATE, which can never fire for the creating envelope's own HLC (the in-code comment already knew this about `content` — content rode the INSERT directly, icon/color did not). So `class.create` with an icon wrote the glyph to the `class` registry row but left the **node row** `icon NULL` — and every effective-icon read (node rows) missed it. Every seeded system class (all carry `SYSTEM_CLASS_ICONS`) was iconless in node-row-derived surfaces. Fix: the INSERT now carries `icon`/`color` exactly like `content`. Pure projection fix — no payload change, no fixture gate.
- **Web core** — `WorkspaceClient.effectiveClassIcon` = raw extends-chain walk (`classIconInChain`, extracted private) ?? `DEFAULT_CLASS_ICON`; `effectiveNodeIcon` = own icon ?? first class's *raw* chain icon ?? `defaultIconFor(node)` — the class default deliberately does NOT leak into nodes (an iconless classed page renders the page glyph, not the class glyph; class identity already shows in the class pills). Signature widened with the three render facts (`isClass`/`presentAsMain`/`parentId`); the worker proxy (`worker-client.ts`) and dispatch (`worker-core.ts`) forward them.
- **Web UI** — `iconFor.nodeIcon` (the UI twin of the resolver: PageView header, Sidebar rows, Outline/Cards/Table items, BlockRow bullets) applies the same `defaultIconFor` tail, so all surfaces agree. `ClassPills` (pill + sortable popup row) switched from raw `cls.icon` to `client.effectiveClassIcon(classId)` — the class glyph shows even when none is authored. ClassView's icon-picker button keeps its `mdi-dots-grid` neutral placeholder (it signals "no icon set" for editing, not display). Link/mention picker rows (`NodeResultItem`/`NodePill`) keep raw own-icon display — chooser rows where a default per row is noise, not identity.
- **Tests** — domain: `defaultIconFor` cascade + constant shape (5 tests). Web (`workspace-client.test.ts`, node env, in-process client): class default + not-stored assertion, own-icon precedence, extends inheritance + multi-hop chain, node chain in class order, page defaults across both document-chrome branches, no class-default leak into iconless classed pages, block → null, class node → class default, `nodeIcon` helper parity (8 tests). 12/12 file-green.

**Verification:** store 167/167 both backends; web 483/483 across 51 files; domain 30/30. `pnpm -r build` + `pnpm -r typecheck` green at the entry.

**Docs (same pass):** `docs/ux.md` §Views — the effective-icon chain + display-time-default paragraph. No SCHEMA.md delta (the `icon` field semantics are unchanged; defaults are read-side, nothing normative moves). No AGENTS.md delta (no wire/op/model touch).

**Projection-healing note (existing live data):** class node rows created before the `upsertClassNode` fix keep `icon NULL` in existing derived DBs (browser worker DBs sync incrementally and do not re-apply old envelopes). Effect until healed: those classes show the new *default* glyph instead of their authored one. Healing paths: the server projection replays its relay log at boot (`workspace-store.ts` hydration — the deploy restarts it), and any fresh/healed client re-fetches the log through the fixed applier. Cosmetic lag, converges by replay; no data migration needed (the log was always correct).

**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.34 blocks register (untouched — no block-content delta; block bullets still dot by the `nodeIcon` null tail) · B4 row `:1581` ("icon fallback chain defines no per-class look" — still true: defaults are generic, per-class bound defaults remain B4's separate proposal) · `:1559` class-influence line (updated behavior, same data-only invariant — nothing persisted) · command palette defaults (now redundant but harmless duplicates of the domain constants — palette items construct their own rows; left as-is) · lockstep law (AGENTS.md parked decisions): **no new op, no wire change → no three-way gate**; sibling clients' own effective-icon code should mirror the defaults (their repos, non-blocking observation) · SCHEMA.md owed-work register (no entry needed — no normative change).

### 34.42 Data color preset palette — refresh, new presets, class-view unification (owner request 2026-10-03 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner request ("suggest a better color presets palette, they look bad. and add presets for missing colors, like yellow, light blue/teal, etc", screenshot of the class view header swatch row). Two problems: **(1)** ClassView carried its own hardcoded 7-hex palette (`ClassView.tsx` `CLASS_COLORS` — raw `#b42318`…`#475467`, the muted row in the screenshot), diverged from the shared data-level preset system (`ui/components/ui/colorPresets.ts` + `variables.css` `--color-preset-*`) that every other picker uses, and showed no yellow/teal; **(2)** the shared values themselves were muddy. No archive mining owed — v1 had no equivalent preset table (its colors were freeform).

**Shipped (web):**

- **`variables.css`** — the 8 `--color-preset-*` values re-tuned brighter with perceptually even lightness and full hue spacing (single set serves light warm-paper AND dark themes — presets are deliberately NOT theme-overridden): red `#e34d45`, orange `#ed822b`, yellow `#f3b816`, green `#30a66f`, teal `#27a59c`, blue `#4072e7`, purple `#9662da`, pink `#de4996`. Two new tokens: `--color-preset-sky` `#20a9e9` (the requested light blue) and `--color-preset-gray` `#8c857d` (warm neutral, replaces the legacy class row's slate).
- **`colorPresets.ts`** — `PRESET_CSS_VARS` / `PRESET_COLOR_ENTRIES` / `PRESET_VAR_NAMES` gain sky + gray: 10 presets in hue order (red, orange, yellow, green, teal, sky, blue, purple, pink, gray). Every picker mapping the shared entries (node picker palette, ColorButton picker, pill context-menu swatch row) picks the additions up automatically.
- **`ClassView.tsx`** — the class header swatch row renders `PRESET_COLOR_ENTRIES` and stores `var(--color-preset-*)` references like every other picker; the ad-hoc raw-hex `CLASS_COLORS` is deleted. `LEGACY_CLASS_COLOR_TO_VAR` maps the 7 old raw hexes to their successor presets so existing colored classes keep the right swatch highlighted; clicking re-stores the var reference. Legacy raw-hex data still renders (`resolveCssColor` passes plain hex through; contrast math unchanged).
- **`ColorButton.css`** — picker grid `repeat(8, 1fr)` → `repeat(5, 1fr)` (tidy 2×5 for ten presets).
- **`nodeColors.ts`** — order comment updated. **`pickers.test.tsx`** — swatch-count assertion 9 → 11 (no-color + 10 presets).

**Client follow-up (owner "implement in gtk and flutter too??"):** presentation-layer alignment, not a wire change — stored colors remain opaque strings, so no op/fixture/three-way protocol gate per the lockstep law.

- **Flutter** (`notees-clients/notees-flutter`) — `lib/core/utils/color_presets.dart` `entries` replaced with the web hex values verbatim (the muted mobile-only palette `#c55a55`… was drift) + `Sky`/`Gray` added; labels double as the `var(--color-preset-<name>)` lookup key in `tryResolve`, so the web's "Light blue" tooltip is labeled `Sky` there. All mobile pickers (node edit modal, quick capture sheet) map `ColorPresets.entries` → ten swatches automatically. Verified: `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 451/451.
- **GTK** (`notees-clients/notees-gtk`) — **no change**: the GTK client round-trips node/class `color` as an opaque store string and has no color rendering or picker UI, so old and new preset values (including `var(--color-preset-sky)`) flow through untouched.

### 34.43 Color wire grammar — token | #hex | null-clear, three-way lockstep, live-log migration (owner directive 2026-10-03 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner directive ("proceed with the best implementation everywhere, no matter the cost. migrate as needed"), following the §34.42 palette refresh and the schema question it raised. The first v3 color encoding stored CSS variable references (`var(--color-preset-red)`) — a web-ism on the wire that drifted between clients (mobile stored resolved hexes from its own muted table, the class view stored raw hexes from a third palette). The §34.43 grammar replaces it: node/class `color` is ONE string field carrying a **preset token** (`red orange yellow green teal sky blue purple pink gray` — set normative in the protocol, order a client concern), a custom **`#RRGGBB`** hex, or **`null` to clear**. `object.update` gains the clear (the UI's "No color" was a protocol no-op until now — `ClassPills.tsx` carried the admission comment); `class.update`'s documented "null clears" is now schema-legal too. Concrete hex never rides the wire — it lives client-side (web `variables.css`, Flutter `ColorPresets`), so themes remap without touching data. **Archive mining:** none owed — v1 had no preset table (freeform colors only).

**Shipped (this repo):**

- **Protocol** — new `src/colors.ts` (`COLOR_PRESET_TOKENS`, `colorValueSchema`, `isColorValue`); `object.update`/`class.create`/`class.update` `color` now `colorValueSchema.nullish()`; the retired `var(--color-preset-*)` encoding is **rejected outright** (fixture test proves it); op-catalog descriptions updated. **13th canonical fixture `object-color.json`** (token → hex → null-clear on object.update; class.create token → class.update clear; id block 500-511 — the f-block collided with existing fixtures, caught by the store's shared-replay gate) + gate list 12→13.
- **Store** — appliers already pushed `null` through (`if (p.color !== undefined)`); only an `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` widen on `upsertClassNode` (`color?: string | null | undefined`) + two semantic tests (fixture-driven: node token/hex/clear; class token on BOTH node + registry rows then clear on both).
- **Web** — `colorPresets.ts` rework: entries emit **tokens**; render bridges `cssColorFor` (token → `var(--color-preset-*)` for style props), `resolveCssColor` (token → concrete hex via `PRESET_HEX` for contrast/canvas), `canonicalColor` (retired var() folds to its token — render tolerance until a client re-syncs); every render boundary converted (ClassView swatches + legacy raw-hex highlight map now maps to tokens, ColorPickerRow, ColorButton, ClassPills pill + **no-color now writes null**, MetadataSection tag pills, InlineTokens underlines, NodeIcon JSON colors, PageCard accent); `UpdateObjectInput.color: string | null`. New `test/color-presets.test.ts` — **PRESET_HEX ↔ variables.css parity guard** (fails on drift; escapes the jsdom fs shim via `process.getBuiltinModule`) + grammar tests. `pickers.test.tsx` stores `"red"` now.
- **Server** — `updateBodySchema.color` mirrored the old freeform string (would have 422'd every clear) → `colorValueSchema.nullish()`; PATCH re-validates through `objectUpdatePayload` (already strict).
- **CLI** — `--color` help documents the grammar; `--color none` sends the clear; e2e test: token lands verbatim, none clears, `var(--color-preset-red)` rejected server-side (422 → domain exit).
- **Migration `scripts/migrate-color-tokens.mts`** (migrate-node-type pattern: read-only dry-run, backup+quick_check before write, strict re-validation of every rewritten payload, snapshots dropped, derived DBs removed, restore_epoch bumped, idempotent marker `color_token_migration_v1`; protocol_version stays 3 — envelope shape unchanged). Rewrites `var(--color-preset-<x>)` → `<x>` in the three color ops' payloads AND inside JSON icon strings — plus a **legacy-hex → token table** (pre-§34.42 web palette, retired ClassView raw hexes, drifted Flutter muted hexes; the mobile cream default #f9f5e8 correctly stays custom). **Live dry-run: 19 legacy hexes → tokens across 4 workspaces, 1 custom passthrough, 0 unmapped, 0 var() refs** (the owner colored from mobile/class-view, never from the web pickers).

**Lockstep (the "everywhere" of the directive) — no new op, but a payload-schema change, so the three-way gate ran:**

- **GTK** (`notees-clients/notees-gtk`, uncommitted → committed+pushed+tagged **`v2.0.0-m5`**): `core/protocol/colors.py` parity module + pydantic `ColorValue`; builders take `color` via the file's `_UNSET` sentinel (None → explicit wire clear); **presence-vs-null fix** in `_apply_object_update`/`_apply_class_update`/`_upsert_class_node` (`"color" in payload`, TS `!== undefined` parity); vendored 13th fixture + replay test (intermediates + both class rows); +73 tests (**515 passed, 3 skipped**; ruff + mypy clean). **Latent parity bug found & fixed beyond the brief**: GTK's `_upsert_class_node` INSERT didn't carry create-time icon/color — the exact §34.40 TS bug, still present here; aligned with `appliers.ts`.
- **Flutter** (`notees-clients/notees-flutter`, → committed+pushed+tagged **`v2.0.0-m15`**): `domain/models/relay/colors.dart` (pure-Dart grammar port); `_color()` validator on all three ops (rejects the retired encoding/garbage; null-clear satisfies the at-least-one-field rule); `_undefined` sentinels on builders AND on the offline outbox (JSON round-trips `"color": null` — proven by flush tests); `ColorPresets.entries` gains tokens (labels unchanged: "Sky" = web's "Light blue", and the var-name lookup keeps resolving); pickers write tokens; `tryResolve` gains a token branch (the brief's assumption that label-lookup covered tokens was wrong — it only ran in the css-var branch); **`_applyClassUpdate` description clobber fixed** (unconditional write → presence-based, TS parity); color clear plumbed end-to-end (`NodeRepository.updateNode` sentinel → `update_color` null → wire). +28 tests (**479 passed**; analyze clean).
- **Observed, recorded, not fixed (out of scope):** a pre-existing GTK WS-timing test flutters intermittently on the pristine baseline too (`test_ws_client.py::TestLifecycle::test_hello_dispatches_and_updates_state`, passes in isolation); Flutter's mobile class-edit path still routes via `object.update` for class fields (converges on the node table; pre-existing); Flutter quick-capture create-time color never reaches the wire (v2 `object.create` carries no color slot — the TS CLI composes create+update for this).

**Verification:** main repo `pnpm -r build` + `pnpm test` green at the entry (protocol 129, domain 30, export 162, store 171 both backends, sync 13, query 153, server 116, **web 489**, cli 64). GTK 515/3-skipped; Flutter 479 — all suites green, both client repos uncommitted-until-this-entry's-commit.

**Docs (same pass):** SCHEMA.md new normative "Data color grammar" section; `docs/usage.md` CLI `--color` grammar; AGENTS.md lockstep line bumped to m5/m15.

**Deploy addendum (owner "commit push and deploy everything"):** committed+pushed all three repos (main `3f8613dc`; GTK `c00fbf5` + tag **`v2.0.0-m5`**; Flutter `848ed75` + tag **`v2.0.0-m15`** — CI publishes from the tags), then `docker compose stop` → `migrate-color-tokens.mts --apply` (**19 payload rewrites**, backup `relay.db.bak-20261003-171912` quick_check-verified, 18 snapshots dropped, 4 derived DBs removed, restore_epoch bumped ×4) → host-built `:latest` images → `docker compose up -d` → smoke **VERIFY-PASS**. The pre-migration server accepted tokens (its schema was any ≤32-char string), so the rewrite was safe under the old build. No main-repo git tag pushed — ghcr CI published nothing.

**Post-migration observations (recorded, no action needed):** (1) the server boot replay (183k-envelope workspace, derived DBs wiped) saturated the event loop ~19 minutes before healthchecks passed — expected one-time cost; (2) every client full-resyncs on next open (restore_epoch bump + snapshots deleted): a fresh browser measured **~5.3 min** to "Sync: idle", so `verify-min.mjs`'s 240 s window timed out twice — a 900 s-window scratch copy passed (VERIFY-PASS: UI search hit, no raw JSON, footer clean); if that window recurs in future migrations, raise the script's timeout permanently; (3) the replay surfaced one **pre-existing** sync error — an old `object.move` envelope parenting class `12eb6ec4…` (move-guard rejects it loud, by design) — historical log data untouched by this migration, visible only because the wipe forced a full replay.

**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.42 (palette values + presets — the grammar's token set and hexes inherit it; PRESET_HEX parity guard now prevents re-drift) · §34.40 (the GTK INSERT bug it fixed in TS — same bug fixed in GTK here) · §34.38 (CLI create+update composition pattern reused for --color) · lockstep law (AGENTS.md parked decisions): payload-schema change → the three-way gate SHIPPED same day (m5/m15) · SCHEMA.md owed-work register (no entry needed — the normative section IS the record).

### 34.44 Class view redesign — a class page is a page (owner directive 2026-10-03 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner directive ("the class view looks super ugly compared to the page view… a normal page view with some extra, class-relevant sections, no class pills but class extends instead"), designed against Capacities (type page = database of objects; settings in panels) and Tana (supertag page = table of tagged nodes; config behind a wrench panel) and recorded at `.plans/design/05-class-view-redesign.md` (rev 2, the owner's corrections folded: class pages keep an editable block body; extends — not class pills — in the corner; class-only picker; Description dropped since title-is-content makes it a literal duplicate; "super nice" UIs for property definitions and templates; ClassPills → NodePills rename).

**What shipped (commits `8430ac73` + `7134fbaf`):**

- **PageView composition slots** — `forClass`, `rootClassName`, `corner`, `iconButton`, `headerActions`, `notice`, `sections`, `systemSections`; all default to the plain-page chrome, so page rendering is untouched. The page read accepts a class node only under `forClass` (`getPage` excludes classes by design).
- **ClassView is a thin wrapper** over PageView: same document chrome, same editable outliner body (classes are containers — the old read-only Blocks section left; the body IS the block tree), Find/Replace, tags row, kebab, empty-page affordance.
- **Corner: ExtendsRow** — the class's parents as NodePills (colored pills, × removes, "+" opens the class-only NodeSelector, self excluded); writes go through `class.setExtends` (replace semantics — the store's loud CycleError surfaces as the transient banner via the `notice` slot). No drag-sort (extends order is deterministic).
- **Header** — the curated ClassIconButton replaces the page icon picker (slot); the 10-swatch color strip became one ColorButton dot with the full picker (§34.43 grammar, none-option clears; the header swatches now ride the §34.42 palette).
- **Sections** — ClassedNodesSection (the instances: existing ViewToolbar + editable table, a column per binding; **expanded by default** — the class page's centerpiece per the Capacities/Tana lesson); PropertyDefinitionsSection (the schema editor — one line per binding: drag-grip reorder writing sequences, per-type mdi glyph, only-set chips (filter/default/precision), Required/Readonly/Hide-when-empty icon toggles, "Configure" expands the full editor (rename, read-only type, default, checkbox flags, date precision/qualified); "Add property" opens a search/create popup over existing schemas; **expanded when empty, collapsed once bound** — parity with the page's "Properties N"); TemplatesSection (assigned templates as cards — icon + name, hover × unbind, click opens; the §34.25 template-family picker unchanged).
- **System sections** — new ExtendedBySection (subclasses, transitive; badge count; a backlink-class read like Linked references) + the standard SystemSections reused verbatim (Child pages expanded by default, per the page view; Linked/Unlinked references). Empty sections hide themselves.
- **NodePills** — ClassPills renamed (three consumers now: page corner, block-row column, extends row) with a parameterizable write path (`actions` add/remove/reorder + removeLabel/addLabel/sortable/excludePickerNodeId); default behavior unchanged for the existing two sites.
- **Dropped** — the Description panel (title-is-content), the Extends/Extended-by/Templates/Property-bindings admin panels (`.nt-class-panel*` CSS retired), the color swatch strip, the read-only Blocks section. The class's generic Properties table suppresses the has-template row (the Templates section is its friendly face).

**Decisions taken (recorded per the owner rule):**

1. **Target-class filter is read-only** in the expanded editor — the wire's `propertySchema.update` carries no `targetClassFilter` (create-only field). Editing it needs a protocol addition (three-way lockstep) — out of this slice; the collapsed chip and expanded pills display it.
2. **Precision is a text chip on the collapsed row** (not a click-cycler as the proposal sketched) — the cycling select lives in the expanded editor; less chrome, no hidden write path.
3. **Classed nodes has no eager count badge** — members aren't a materialized count, and the section defaults to expanded so the badge would be redundant.
4. **Class pages keep the page view's Tags row and Properties section** (page parity); only the has-template row is suppressed (see above).
5. **`getPage` stays class-exclusive** — PageView gains a separate class-aware read under `forClass` rather than widening the `getPage` contract other callers rely on.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; full gate green — `pnpm -r --workspace-concurrency=1 build` + `pnpm test` (protocol 129, store 171 both backends, sync 13, query 153, domain 30, export 162, server 116, **web 490**, cli 64). Class-view suite rewritten for the new composition (extends picker + cycle banner, classed-nodes expanded-by-default, property-definitions editor flows, Child pages in system sections).

**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.25 T3 (the templates slot's bind/unbind UI moved into TemplatesSection — the has-template property semantics and the ONE-class-filtered-picker amendment unchanged) · §34.42/§34.43 (the class color dot consumes the new palette + grammar; the legacy-hex mapping retired with the strip) · §34.23 render-state cascade (ClassView remains the `is_class` branch; the cascade spec untouched) · §34.19/§34.34 registers (no block-content delta) · SCHEMA.md owed-work register (no entry needed — no normative change; the wire is untouched) · lockstep law (AGENTS.md parked decisions): **no new op, no wire change → no three-way gate**; GTK/Flutter class views are separate code, unaffected.

### 34.45 Properties + search correctness batch — PB2/PB3/PB4/PG4/PC1/PC2 + M2/M3/M5 (2026-10-03 — SHIPPED same day)

The two registers' store/web correctness slices executed together (both are "no wire change, unblocked" per their cost frames — §34.32 sequencing step 1's PB1–PB4/PG4/PC1/PC2 minus PG6, and §34.30 sequencing M2→M3→M5). **No new op types, no payload changes — zero protocol/lockstep gate** (applier semantics changed; GTK/Flutter keep the old derived state until they port, with no data loss — the log is untouched and a wipe+replay heals).

**PB2 — text-property carrier lifecycle** (`packages/store/src/appliers.ts`, `property-values.ts` new, `apps/web/src/core/workspace-client.ts`, `apps/web/src/ui/views/TableView.tsx`):
- `property.set` enforces one-shape-per-type fail-loud at the applier, keyed off `property_schema.type`: **text = scalar string or `{nodeId}` carrier ref**; **date/object = node reference** (legacy bare uuid normalizes to `{nodeId}`; a date-looking string is rejected — dates are nodes); **date_range = `{start,end}` of refs, either side open**. Unknown schema ids store unchecked (`property.set` has no schema FK — server-tested behavior preserved). `null` means "no value" and bypasses the check.
- `property.unset` of a node-backed text value **deletes the carrier**: trash + retention, consistent with node deletion, guarded three ways — the removed value resolves to a node ref, the target is an active non-class **child of the owner**, and **no other `property_value` row references it** (both stored shapes checked). Scalar text (citekey-style) carries no carrier; object-typed unsets never delete their target.
- **"Promote to block"** surfaces at the client level as `WorkspaceClient.promotePropertyCarrier(objectId, schemaId, idx)` — detaches the value and revives the carrier as an ordinary body child, composed from the two existing ops (`property.unset`, whose applier trashes the orphaned carrier, then `object.restore`, a safe no-op when a remaining reference kept it alive). Display state only: no move op, no tree write.
- The three coexisting text shapes converge: `{nodeId}` canonical, plain strings = scalar text (the seeded citekey/isbn/publisher family stays string-typed — server API tests pin this), legacy bare uuids read-lenient and normalize on write.

**PB3 — body-exclusion depth bug** (`apps/web/src/core/workspace-client.ts`): `getBlockTree` computed the carrier-exclusion set once per page; now computed **per subtree root** (`propertyCarrierIdsOf(id)` at every level), and an excluded carrier's subtree is pruned with it.

**PB4 — documented gap-tolerance**: normative SCHEMA.md statement — multi-value idx slots are positional LWW, `unset` never re-packs, readers must treat idx as an opaque slot key (real fix stays PG5 element identity).

**PG4 — extends-aware binding resolution** (`packages/store/src/effective.ts`): winners are (class, ancestor) candidates discovered by BFS over `class_extends` — own binding (distance 0) first, then shortest extends-path, ties by class-assignment HLC then class id; `boundBy` names the ancestor whose row supplies default + metadata. Subclass nodes inherit ancestor bindings (the citations promise: runtime `source` subclasses get `authors`/`attachments`). The `class_hierarchy` closure carries no distance, so the walk uses the edge table (register said "walk class_hierarchy" — deviation recorded; no distance column exists). With no extends edges the sort key reduces exactly to the shipped first-class-applied-wins.

**PC1 — render contract**: normative SCHEMA.md statement in "Class properties" — readonly dims editors, required = empty-highlight + lint *suggestion*, hideWhenEmpty hides valueless rows; **client-honor by design law, zero store enforcement** (no new ops, per the register). The web chrome half shipped in §34.44's PropertyDefinitionsSection.

**PC2 — typed/validated defaults**: `class.property.set` fails loud on a `defaultValue` mismatching the schema type (node-typed schemas accept only JSON null — a default link is meaningless); the effective read drops a stored default that drifted out of match (schema delete+recreate under one id) instead of deriving garbage.

**M2 — FTS ranking** (`packages/store/src/search.ts`, `store.ts`): `Store.search` keeps its signature; ordering is now relevance + recency. **Deviation from the register, verified**: its "bare `rank` works FTS4+FTS5" claim is false for the shipped sql.js wasm ("no such column: rank") — FTS5 orders by the hidden `rank` column in SQL; FTS4 fetches `matchinfo('x')` and scores hit counts in JS. Both tiebreak `updated_at` DESC then node id. **The module is a property of the database, not the backend** (the cross-backend snapshot restore parks an FTS4 index inside better-sqlite3 and the repair path keeps it — caught by the existing restore test), so it is detected from `sqlite_master` and cached per connection (WeakMap; restore swaps the connection). Title-weight column still pending per the register (DDL, rides the rebuild path, sequenced last).

**M3 — snippet helper**: `searchSnippet(db, nodeId, query, {maxTokens, ellipsis})` + `Store.getSearchSnippet` return the excerpt around the densest query-term cluster with char-accurate match spans. **Deviation, verified**: computed in JS over the derived plaintext instead of SQL `snippet()` — the stock sql.js FTS4 `snippet()` emits its column index into the output and misplaces markers (probe evidence), and `offsets()` units differ per module; one JS implementation is byte-identical on both and needs no MATCH query.

**M5 — property values in the FTS index** (`packages/store/src/content.ts`, `appliers.ts`): `extractSearchPlaintext(db, raw, nodeId?)` (additive optional param) folds text-ish property values into the row — text carrier content (one level), scalar strings (url/email), select option labels, numbers as strings; date/object refs, ranges, booleans stay structural. `property.set`/`property.unset` reindex the owner; `reindexAllSearch` rebuilds the same rows (restore-repair included).

**Verification:** store build clean (tsup + DTS); store 255 tests green on BOTH adapters (new `test/property-values.test.ts` 26 + `test/search-ranking.test.ts` 22); web typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` clean; web 529 tests green (properties-panel gained PB2/PB3/promote coverage; view-modes' text-cell test updated to the carrier contract; TableView text cells now write carriers — create-on-first-edit, update-the-carrier after); server 141 tests green (search route untouched signature-wise; citekey/isbn string writes + unknown-schema writes still pass).

**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.32 (PB2/PB3/PB4/PG4/PC1/PC2 rows ticked above; PG6 remains — its scalar/multi/target-existence checks are the next batch; PB1 remains — owner rule pending; PC8's doc-lag batch remains, one item of which — SCHEMA.md:9's metadata tick — this batch touches adjacent text but does not tick) · §34.30 (M2/M3/M5 rows ticked; M1/M4/M6–M12 untouched; B1's stale-comment row rides M4 as registered) · SCHEMA.md (extends owed item :20 ticked at the read model — the `class_extends.ord` column stays deferred per the owner decision; FTS plaintext :41 ticked with the M5 enumeration; "Class properties" gained the PG4/PC1/PC2 normative statements; "Node-backed text properties" gained the write-shape contract, the unset-carrier guards, the promote composition, and the PB4 gap-tolerance) · architecture.md §11 item 1 (PG4 headline — marked done) · §34.38 restore (`object.restore` semantics unchanged; promote composes it) · §34.25 templates (clone engine copies authored `{nodeId}` refs unchanged) · §34.34 BB-rows (untouched) · lockstep law: no new op, no wire change → no three-way gate.


### 34.46 Properties SURFACE batch — PG7 + PG12 + PG14 (wired half) + PG15 + PG17 (2026-10-04)

The §34.32 register's surface slices, minus the owner-gated rows (PB1/PG1/PG3/PG6/PG10/PG13/PG16 untouched; PG2/PG9/PG11 stay registered for their own passes). **No new op types, no payload changes — zero protocol/lockstep gate** (HTTP routes, client reads, web chrome, export projections only).

**PG7 — server routes + CLI verbs.**
- Routes (`apps/server/src/routes-objects.ts`, all additive, all through the one envelope pipeline): `PATCH /api/property-schemas/:id` (propertySchema.update: name/options/datePrecision/dateQualified patch; empty body 422; unknown id 404 — the applier's UPDATE is quiet on unknown ids, the API is not), `DELETE /api/property-schemas/:id` (soft-delete; second delete 404s; the schema views gained `datePrecision`/`dateQualified` — the PATCH response exposed their absence), `POST /api/classes/:id/properties` (class.property.set; body carries `propertySchemaId` + the patch, mirroring the object-properties route; returns the decoded binding row; a wrong-typed defaultValue fails 422 at the applier per PC2), `DELETE /api/classes/:id/properties/:propertySchemaId` (class.property.unset; authored values survive — tested). All four registered in the OpenAPI route table (`x-required-scope: objects.write`, idempotency honored — the hooks are header-driven and automatic).
- CLI (`apps/cli/src/cli.ts`, `uuid.ts`, `shell.ts`): new top-level `notees property` group — `list`, `get`, `create` (`--type/--multi/--option/--targetClass`; the schema id is a fresh UUIDv7 from a small local RFC 9562 generator in `uuid.ts` — the CLI carries no uuidv7 dependency and identity is UUIDv7 by law; option ids derive deterministically), `rename`, `delete` (preview-first `--yes` rail; soft-delete, values survive, recreate-under-same-uuid reactivates per the register's blessed path), `bind`/`unbind` (tri-state `--flag`/`--no-flag` pairs, `--default` parses JSON). The schema-ref contract is unchanged: unknown NAMES fail usage (exit 2), unknown UUIDs reach the server 404 (exit 1). Shell gains `unsetProperty` + the schema/binding helpers (`propertySchemas`, `propertySchema`, `createPropertySchema`, `updatePropertySchema`, `deletePropertySchema`, `setClassProperty`, `unsetClassProperty`).
- Docs: `docs/usage.md` (new "Property schemas" CLI section, shell helper list, quick-reference table rows for the four routes).

**PG12 — PropertyView + PropertyReferencesSection.**
- Store (`packages/store/src/store.ts`): additive read `propertyValueCarriers(schemaId)` — active nodes with authored slots (values + qualifier metadata), trashed nodes excluded, `classMembers`-order determinism; both-backend tests.
- Clients: `WorkspaceClient.getPropertyReferences` + the worker path (`worker-client` cachedRead, `worker-core` invoke case).
- Web (`apps/web/src/ui/components/PropertyView.tsx` + CSS, entry in `MetadataSection.tsx`): the inspector modal (schema metadata line, target-class pills, options pills, bound classes, references table over the NodeCollection machinery — Name + the schema's value column via `propertiesOf`) — deliberately NOT the v1 full PropertyView (v2 schemas are registry rows). Entry point per the register: the property settings modal's new "Open property" button; the property-label click still opens the settings modal. The §34.30 register's shell wave, riding the store-side M2/M3/M5 that shipped in §34.45. **No new op types, no payload changes — zero protocol/lockstep gate** (derived-state reads, HTTP query params, web chrome only). Store `dist` rebuilt (public API gained `searchPage`; `buildMatchQuery` semantics extended); query `dist` rebuilt (`buildMatchExpression` kept in lockstep with the store port).

**M1 — SearchBox mounted** (`apps/web/src/ui/SearchBox.tsx`, `ui/components/Sidebar.tsx`, `ui/App.tsx`): the field sits in the sidebar under the workspace switcher; the results panel overlays the nav (position-absolute, token-only CSS in `app.css`), renders the M3 snippet per plain-text hit (highlighted `<mark>` spans, `client.getSearchSnippet`), and pages with **Load more**. `cacheVersion` (the App's `pagesVersion`) is a new optional Sidebar prop forwarded to SearchBox. The sidebar magnify button still opens the palette — the register's either/or resolved as both (field = search, palette = command/launcher).

**M4 — palette Content group + M6 sections/registry + §34.28 #12 date affordances** (`apps/web/src/ui/components/CommandPalette.tsx` + CSS): full rewrite of the item pipeline. Synchronous section registry in Capacities order — **Recent** (device-local `notees.recents`, empty query only, live `notees:recents` broadcast; no sync per §34.29 #8) → **Date Pages** (the parsed-date suggestion via `parseDate` + deterministic `chainNodeIds`, create-on-pick through `ensureDateChain`; fuzzy over date pages with formatted display + raw `YYYYMMDD` keywords) → **Pages** → **Classes** → **Content** → **Commands** (the action registry: New page, query-scoped typed `Create page "…"` → `handleNewPage(title?)`, Toggle theme, Sign out). Content is debounced (150ms, generation-guarded, re-runs on `cacheVersion`) over `client.searchPage`, with M3 snippets and containing-page labels on block hits. The **`is_daily:`** prefix scopes the palette to the Date Pages section. Cross-section dedupe by node id (a date page lands in Date Pages, not Pages; a title match stays out of Content). Empty query no longer dumps every page/class (gated — fuzzy `""` matches everything). B2's input half: the raw `<input>` is the `SearchField` kit primitive now; loading ("Searching content…") and empty states added. **Deviation (registered on the B2 row):** result rows keep the palette-skinned markup (a grouped list-row composite, closer to NodeResultItem than to `Button`) — extracting a kit list-row primitive rides a future polish pass rather than restyling the palette here. **jsdom guard:** `scrollIntoView?.()` (the effect never ran in jsdom before — no test rendered the palette).

**M7/C3 — mention candidates + class refine** (`apps/web/src/ui/components/pickers/NodeSelector.tsx`): candidates already rode M2-ranked `client.search` (C3's reroute is thus confirmed live; the `outliner-context.ts` citation stays corrected per §34.34 BB2). New: a leading **`class:<name>`** prefix refines any non-classes mode to that class's members — greedy longest-display-name resolution (spaced names unambiguous), unknown names leave the query untouched; the create row answers the REST of the query (the prefix never becomes the title) and `defaultCreateNew` carries the refined class (`effectiveClassIds` = caller `classFilters` ∪ refine). Empty effective query lists the refined class's members (the picker's natural candidate set).

**M8 — block mode + containing-page labels** (`NodeSelector.tsx`): `searchMode="blocks"` restricts candidates to `rendersAsInlineBlock` nodes (the future `((` picker's set — capability ships without a production caller by design, like SearchBox pre-M1); every block hit now renders a containing-page breadcrumb (nearest document-chrome ancestor's path, built from the existing `buildParentPath`). Also consumes: the LinkEditModal block picker gains the labels for free.

**C1 — quoted phrases in `buildMatchQuery`** (`packages/store/src/search.ts`, port-synced in `packages/query/src/compiler.ts`): `"…"` segments compile to exact FTS phrases ANDed with the unquoted remainder's prefix tokens; unterminated quotes degrade to plain text; empty quotes vanish. Probe-verified against the shipped sql.js FTS4 AND better-sqlite3 FTS5 before implementation (phrase syntax is the intersection; FTS5's `"term"*` phrase-PREFIX stays off the table — FTS4 silently no-matches it). FTS4 matchinfo scoring now sizes the blob by AND-clause count (one FTS phrase each), not raw term count. Store `searchPage` shares the builder.

**C5 — cursor pagination**: `Store.searchPage(query, {limit, cursor})` (offset cursor over the deterministic relevance/recency/id order; `nextCursor` null at the end; a garbage cursor throws — the API validates the shape first, so it 422s before reaching the store). `/api/search` gains the opaque `cursor` param + returns `nextCursor` (additive; the `isClass`/`presentAsMain` filters compose — the cursor walks the full ranked stream). Web clients: `searchPage` on both (WorkspaceClient direct over the store page; WorkerClient raw RPC + worker-core `searchPage` case — deliberately NOT a cachedRead: the cache key would include the cursor, so a load-more could never reuse page one). SearchBox pages through it; the sync first page offers Load more on exactly-50 hits (one possibly-empty trailing page — documented). `search` gains an optional `limit` param plumb (client + worker) — additive, default unchanged.

**C6 — name→id resolver**: `GET /api/resolve?name=…` (`routes-objects.ts` + OpenAPI row) — case-insensitive exact display-name match over the top-100 ranked FTS candidates (blocks included; follows search semantics), 404 `not_found` when absent. Web clients expose `resolveNodeByName` (worker RPC case included); SearchBox's DSL `linked:` resolver consumes the local one (replacing its old search-then-exact-match helper). **Deviation:** rewiring `cli.ts` off its `/api/search` prefetch kludge is parked — cli.ts is owned by another wave; the endpoint it should consume now exists.

### 34.49 Server-side snapshot self-heal — the missing-snapshot root cause and fix (owner directive: fix the live bug — SHIPPED 2026-10-04)

The §34.48 addendum's owed question, answered: **why did snapshot recreation never fire after the §34.43 migration dropped all 18?** Snapshots existed on the relay only via two paths — client `PUT /snapshot/data` (the SyncEngine's `maybeUploadSnapshot`, best-effort with a bare catch: a failed upload must not fail sync) and operator `POST /compact`. The server itself — despite hydrating a fully-replayed derived store on every boot — never snapshot it, and no scheduler existed. Investigation against the live DB (0 snapshot rows across all 4 workspaces, 12 h post-migration) plus a local reproduction at live scale (147k-envelope workspace on the real server build):

- The client path **works at small scale** (2k-envelope repro: first sync uploads, second client restores) and **completes at 147k scale in Node** — but the produced blob was **119.7 MiB against the relay's 128 MiB `bodyLimit`**: a PUT past the limit is rejected `413 Request body is too large` and the engine's catch swallows it **silently, forever** (the in-memory `uploadedSnapshotHlc` gate resets per session, so every sync retries and every retry 413s). Linear scaling to the live 183k envelopes overshoots the cliff. Compounding factors: the 60 s transport timeout on a ~2-min upload, and tab lifetime — the browser pays ~5.3 min of catch-up first and may close before a multi-hundred-MB upload finishes. Net: client-only snapshot production is structurally unreliable at this workspace's size, so the wipe left the relay snapshot-less indefinitely — exactly the SCHEMA.md owed item first observed after the Revision 11 deploy (2026-10-02) and re-flagged by the §34.43 migration.
- **Fix (design law kept: snapshots are an optimization, never authority — additive, no wire change, read paths untouched):** the server's own store is the natural snapshotter. `WorkspaceManager.hydrate` now calls `ensureSnapshotAfterReplay` after the replay converges: when the workspace's log has **no snapshot at all** and holds at least `SNAPSHOT_REBUILD_MIN_ENVELOPES` (1,000 — below one catch-up page a restore buys nothing over a seq-0 replay, and the client path covers those), it persists `store.snapshot()` via the existing `relay.saveSnapshot` (blob under `snapshots/<id>.db` + meta row, `hlc = maxHlc`, `up_to_seq = latestSeq`). Best-effort (a failed write never fails hydration, mirroring the client's maybeUploadSnapshot), synchronous inside hydrate where the expensive replay already happens, and **once per wipe at most** (fires only when `latestSnapshot` is null), so no thrash and no row accumulation beyond the one the wipe removed. The bytes are the same whole-DB dumps client snapshots always were: the client's existing restore path handles them (including the FTS5→FTS4 cross-backend repair — already store-tested), and the server's own boot fast path restores them on the next wipe.
- **Files:** `apps/server/src/workspace-store.ts` (the trigger + exported threshold constant), `apps/server/test/workspace-snapshot-rebuild.test.ts` (new — 5 tests), `apps/server/test/developer-api.test.ts` (pre-existing red at HEAD, unrelated: `/api/meta` version still asserted `^2.0.0-m` after the committed 3.0.0 bump — now asserts `SERVER_VERSION`).
- **Verification:** server suite **154/154** (149 pre-existing + 5 new; multi-boot creation, no-thrash across a third boot, small-log skip, existing-snapshot untouched, and an end-to-end fresh `SyncEngine` client over a real socket restoring the server-produced snapshot + catching up the tail); sync suite **13/13** (its snapshot round-trip coverage already exercises the restore contract; the snapshot format itself lives in `packages/store`, whose cross-backend restore tests are unchanged and green). Live-scale repro numbers above; `>128 MiB` PUT confirmed `413`.
- **Operational note (next deploy):** after `docker compose up -d` on this fix, the first request touching each workspace (any client write or object read — hydration is lazy) creates the snapshot within seconds (the derived store already exists; no replay, just one ~100 MB serialize+write); `GET /api/relay/v2/snapshot` then reports `hasSnapshot:true` and fresh clients restore + delta-catch-up instead of the 5.3-min full replay. The 3 small workspaces stay snapshot-less by design (under the 1,000-envelope threshold; they replay in ~1 page anyway). **Residual (registered, not this slice):** (1) the 128 MiB `bodyLimit` cliff still blocks oversized *client* uploads in steady state — ~~harmless while a server snapshot exists, but a freshness ceiling as logs grow; a future compact/refresh policy (and/or chunked uploads / raised limit) is the durable answer~~ **HEALED 2026-10-04 (§34.69): the snapshot PUT rides a route-local 512 MiB cap and the client's 413 is reported once per session instead of swallowed;** (2) the replay-performance half of the SCHEMA.md owed item (batch/transaction-wrap the server replay, yield the event loop so healthz stays live) stands; (3) the 60 s upload timeout and non-413 failure silence keep client-upload failures otherwise invisible — acceptable by design, worth remembering when debugging sync.

**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.43 (migration that dropped the snapshots — its "recreation never happened" observation is this entry's bug report) · §34.48 addendum (owed question — resolved here, pointer left in place) · SCHEMA.md owed-work register (the "verify the server creates a fresh snapshot after a rebuild" half of the Replay/snapshot-bootstrap item is ticked DONE here; the replay batching/event-loop half remains owed) · `.plans/dev/architecture.md` §3 (server-hydration paragraph extended with the self-heal) · lockstep law: no wire change, no new op → no three-way gate.

### 34.51 §34.19 standalone register rows — editor chrome & keymap wave (2026-10-04)

The §34.19 rows no detail section owns, in one wire-neutral slice (no new ops, no payload changes → no GTK/Flutter lockstep gate). All seven rows land behind the existing client surfaces; the register rows above carry the ✅/🟡 status, this entry is the work record.

**1. Back/Forward in-app history** — `historyNavKeyHandler` (exported from `App.tsx`, the `openTodayKeyHandler` pattern): Alt+← / Alt+→ preventDefault the browser's native history jump and call `window.history.back()/forward()`, so the EXISTING popstate effect drives selection/nav from the same stack deep links already push. Guarded from form fields (input/textarea/select/contenteditable); Ctrl/Meta/Shift variants ignored (Ctrl+Alt+arrows are the fold chords). Topbar buttons stay owed (§34.19 :1149).

**2. Block multi-selection** — session-local selection state in `OutlinerContext` (`useOutlinerValue` gains the set + anchor, opt-in via a new `selection` option; PageView enables it for the main body — embedded feeds and class composition don't). Gestures: shift+click extends a document-order range from the anchor, Ctrl/Cmd+click toggles, press-and-drag over rows ("arches") extends from the press anchor (the trailing click is swallowed via a one-shot flag so it never enters edit mode), Escape / outside-mousedown clear. `useBlockSelectionSurface` owns the document listeners; rows get `nt-block--selected`. The floating `SelectionBar` (`ui/components/SelectionBar.tsx`) offers the group ops through the existing per-block client writes (sequential, honest per-block failures): bulk class assign/unassign (unassign refuses non-removable classes up front), bulk tag, bulk move-to-page (`moveObject` per block), bulk delete via the reusable danger `ConfirmationModal` (latched so it outlives a selection clear). **Deliberately NOT implemented (recorded, not approximated):** box/marquee select, Shift+arrows keyboard extension, Ctrl+A/C/X/V over a clipboard internal format — no client API exists for those; they ride the future selection-store work.

**3. Ghost trailing block** — a page body whose last child is non-empty renders the muted "Click to add a block" ghost row (outline mode, non-embedded; hidden when the last child is empty or in prose/cards). Display-only until the click, which creates a real empty block (`object.create`, the only op) and focuses it via the outliner focus request.

**4. Keymap entries** — Alt+Shift+↑/↓ reorder the edited block among its siblings (one `object.move` per press, caret stays; the v1 MOVE chords). Fold: Ctrl+. toggles the FOCUSED block (discovered from `document.activeElement` — no focus ledger), Ctrl+Alt+← folds / Ctrl+Alt+→ unfolds (one-directional); childless blocks no-op. Alt+←/→ went to Back/Forward (§1), resolving the register's same-chord collision; Ctrl+Alt+arrows verified free in Chrome/Firefox/Safari (OS display-driver screen rotation excepted, out of page reach). Ctrl/Cmd+N (new page, created + opened through `openPage` so history tracks it), Ctrl/Cmd+, (settings), Ctrl/Cmd+\ (sidebar) ship via the exported `keymapChordHandler`; all form-field-guarded. Ctrl+N remains browser-reserved on desktop builds (fires only where the browser yields); Ctrl+Alt+P stays reserved-unbound and Ctrl+F stays browser-native per the §34.30 C4 / §34.48 rulings.

**5. Suggestion popup multi-select + filter prefixes** — `NodeSelector` gains `multiSelect`/`onApplyMulti`: rows render the kit Checkbox (pointer-transparent; the whole row toggles), picked rows ride the top, the footer carries the picked count + Apply + hints; Enter keeps the fast single-assign path (pick + commit in one gesture — the pre-multi contract, so the capture flows keep working), Ctrl/Cmd+Enter commits the accumulated set, clicking a picked row un-picks. The editor's `#`/`+` captures opt in (`commitNodePicks` assigns the whole set and strips the trigger once); `@` stays single-pick. Filter prefixes: `daily:` (bare = day-precision pages; combined with text it filters the name inside the pool), `is_daily:`/`is_page:`/`is_class:` booleans — tokens strip from the search text before FTS/name matching AND the create row (a filter never becomes a page title); a bare filter lists its pool instead of waiting for input. All pickers inherit the prefixes (they live in the shared machinery).

**6. Broken-link "create page with UUID"** — LinkEditModal's broken-target state (mention target resolves to no node) renders the heal row; the click runs the caller-id create path (`createObject({ id: targetId, presentAsMain: true })`) and closes — the mention token is untouched, so the link heals in place instead of being retargeted. Create failures toast.

**7. Non-removable-class rules** — `ui/components/classRemoval.ts`: the class anchor + year/month/day journal classes refuse ×-removal everywhere the UI offers it — NodePills pill × and overflow-popup row (which covers the page classes row AND the block-row classes column), the classed-nodes row ×, the node context menu's "Remove from this node", and the selection bar's bulk unassign. The × becomes a permanent lock (`pill--non-removable`), the click surfaces the honest toast via the notification store, and no write is issued. UI-level rule by design; the server/CLI REST surface stays an ungated admin path (same as v1).

The pre-existing `capture.test.tsx` #/+ specs pass unchanged (the Enter fast path preserves their contract).

### 34.50 Remote-poison quarantine — the stuck-replay fix (owner bug report 2026-10-04 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner report: "I get this in the web UI: object.move: node 12eb6ec4… is a class; classes are always roots and cannot have a parent." Investigation: exactly ONE poison envelope in the live log — seq 52497, a v1-era `object.move` parenting the "Reunión de Producto" class (pre-Revision-11 data; the §34.23/§34.43 migrations left it). `Store.applyMany` applied remote batches in one transaction, so the throw rolled back the whole page, the cursor never advanced, and **every fresh client replay since Revision 11 was stuck at July 22, 2026 (seq 52497)** with a repeating sync error — the §34.43 smoke's one console error was this, and its checks never noticed the stuck cursor. (288 `object.create` on class ids replay fine — upsert-tolerated, not poison.)

Fix (owner directive era: implement all pending): remote replay **quarantines** move-guard violations instead of bricking — `quarantined_envelope` table (schema v9, additive), the batch records the poison id as processed (cursor advances), and the sync engine surfaces one aggregated onError report. Local authoring (`Store.apply`, and `applyMany` by default) stays fail-loud; validation errors (`PropertyValueShapeError` et al.) still throw from batches — quarantine is scoped to `MoveGuardError` under the engine's explicit `quarantineMoveGuards` option.

Log repair (registered decision): the poison envelope is NOT deleted — quarantine makes replay convergent and the owner's law keeps the log immutable; deletion stays available (backup + restore_epoch pattern) if the per-fresh-sync error report ever becomes noise.

Verified: store 299/299 both adapters (new quarantine suite), sync 14/14 (fresh-device convergence past a historical poison, error surfaced once). Register cross-check: §34.45's PG6 fail-loud contract preserved (validation errors throw); the §34.43 smoke's known console error now appears once per fresh replay and converges.

### 34.53 §34.19 whiteboard full toolset — palette, formatting, multi-select, align/distribute, minimap, keyboard (2026-10-04 — SHIPPED same day)

The §34.19 `:1181` row ("Whiteboard full toolset") ships its toolset wave over the existing canvas: all geometry stays in the `whiteboard` token (content grammar — no new wire op, no fixture gate, no lockstep), cards stay child blocks (the model law), and the embedded/fullscreen contract (`embedded` prop, capped height, scroll priority) is unchanged for the presentation deck's consumption.

**Palette** (`apps/web/src/ui/whiteboard/tools.ts`, kit `Button`s in the canvas toolbar): select/move, card, sticky note, rect, ellipse, line, arrow, freehand stroke, text, connector. Draw tools drag to draw (a bare click places a default-size shape at the point); place tools put one item and return to select — every tool is one-shot, and **Esc** exits an armed tool (also cancels an in-flight gesture). The **connector** is an arrow whose endpoints snap to card/shape anchors (edge midpoints + centers, 14-world-unit radius) at creation time; the plain arrow tool does not snap.

**Formatting surfaces**: the toolbar grows a contextual section with a selection — `ColorButton` picker (presets + custom hex input + remove) writing the **§34.43 grammar**: shape/stroke colors land in the layout JSON (`color` field, tolerant-parse validated), a *card's* color is the **node's** `color` field (the grammar's home — sticky notes are colored cards, seeded `yellow`); a stroke-width `SelectionButton` (S/M/L = 2/4/8 world units) writes `strokeWidth`/`width`. **Multi-select**: shift+click accumulates, background drag box-selects (marquee with live highlight + additive shift), dragging any selected card moves the whole selection; **Delete/Backspace** removes the mixed selection (geometry out of the token, card blocks out of the graph). **Alignment/distribution** (≥2 / ≥3 selected) operate on cards + shapes in one write. **Grid/snap**: the dot grid moved onto the transformed world layer (world-anchored, zoom-honest) with a Snap toggle (24-unit step for placement/draw/drag). **Minimap** (fullscreen only): full-bounds overview, viewport rectangle, click/drag to navigate. **Zoom cluster**: −/+/Fit/1:1 + live percentage; wheel zoom and middle-drag pan unchanged (left-drag is now marquee — the pan migration is recorded below). **Keyboard** (surface-focused, inert inside card text/label inputs): Delete/Backspace, arrows nudge 1 world unit (Shift = grid step), Esc.

**Architecture**: pure helpers in `apps/web/src/ui/whiteboard/geometry.ts` (bounds, marquee hit-test, snap, align/distribute, anchors — all React-free, directly tested); `WhiteboardMinimap.tsx` (view-state only, never writes); the layout schema in `whiteboard-layout.ts` extended (shape kinds `line`/`text`, `color`/`strokeWidth`, stroke `color`/`width`/`highlight`) under the existing migration-tolerance rule — the strict token schema (`layout: record`) is untouched. **Write coalescing hardened**: `commitLayout` now chains its base AST while a canvas write is still applying (rapid same-tick gestures — nudges, quick recolors — no longer lose the first write to a stale host read) and updates the view ref synchronously.

**Found + fixed en route (kit, load-bearing beyond this wave):** `ColorButton`'s picker had `onClickCapture={stopPropagation}` on the picker dialog — React's capture walk aborted there, so **every swatch/hex/remove click inside any color picker was dead** (the app-wide symptom stayed latent: §34.43's live note says the owner colored from mobile/class-view, never the web pickers; no existing test exercised the picker's interior). The capture handler now stops mousedown only (the blur/overlay guard that mattered); swatch clicks live. Verified by the new formatting tests.

**Deviations from the v1 row, registered:** (1) background left-drag is now the marquee (the select tool's box-select) — fullscreen pan moves to the **middle button**, the minimap, and the zoom cluster; the shipped "background drag pans" test was updated to middle-drag; (2) the connector snaps endpoints **at creation** but stays plain geometry afterwards — it does not track moving cards (v1's sticky-ended connectors would need anchor persistence in the layout; recorded, not built); (3) the row's "15 shapes" scope ships as the task's enumerated set (rect/ellipse/line/arrow + text) — triangle/star/etc. remain owed; (4) pen/highlighter/eraser **modes** are not a tool group: the stroke tool + width tiers cover widths, the `highlight` flag exists in the layout schema (round-trip tested) but no UI ships it, and the eraser is Delete; (5) the context menu stays owed — alignment/formatting live in the toolbar's contextual section instead; (6) card colors render as the card background only (title bar tints via `color-mix`; no luminance-driven foreground override).

**Verification:** `apps/web` typecheck clean for this wave's files (the `remap-reunion` tsc reds are a concurrent untracked sibling wave's files, not this change). Whiteboard/embed suites green (whiteboard-canvas 17, whiteboard-toolset 24 new, embeds 6); **full web suite 81 files / 768 tests green**. No SCHEMA.md owed-item falsified (the normative "Whiteboard modeling" section gained the toolset-fields line; card color = node color recorded); docs in the same pass — `docs/ux.md` (Whiteboards Today + the views table), `docs/usage.md` (new Whiteboards section under Tables), this row + entry.

**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.19 `:1181` (this row) · §34.26 (`PresentationOverlay` remains the future fullscreen host; the deck's embedded usage untouched — `deck-view.test.tsx` green) · §34.43 (the formatting surfaces consume the grammar; the ColorButton fix is recorded there-adjacent in this entry, not a new register row) · §34.34 B5 (whiteboard layout-as-token precedent cited there stands).

### 34.54 🔴 Protocol batch part 1 — `workspace.feature.set` + `code_block` + `hr` + `embed_ref.view`: the three-client lockstep TS reference (2026-10-04 — TS reference SHIPPED same day; **LOCKSTEP-PENDING: no writer emits until the GTK/Flutter releases**)

Part 1 of the §34.34/§34.35 protocol batch (owner-authorized lockstep; client releases follow separately). The hard constraint governs everything: until the GTK/Flutter releases ship, NOTHING may write these ops/tokens to a live log — older clients fail loud on unknown opTypes/token shapes. So the TS reference ships COMPLETE (op schemas, strict grammar, store applier + schema v10, domain map, read helpers, renderers, fixtures, tests) while every AUTHORING surface stays inert: the Features-tab toggles render live state but are disabled (`FEATURE_TOGGLE_WRITES_ENABLED = false`), and the `/code` + `/hr` slash registry rows sit commented with a LOCKSTEP-PENDING marker in TriggerPopup.

**§34.35 — `workspace.feature.set {feature, enabled}` (the Features system):**

- **Protocol** — the op lands in op-types.ts + the op catalog; `feature` is fixed vocabulary (`WORKSPACE_FEATURES`: tasks / journals / readItLater / library / people / collections — feature ids, not UUIDs), strict schema rejects unknown ids. Canonical fixtures: **`workspace-feature-set.json`** (a same-slot race: laptop enable @…010000 vs phone disable @…010100 — the disable wins; journals off; readItLater off→on) and **`class-delete-managed.json`** (seeded task class + member, a `class.delete` on the managed class, a re-enable).
- **Store (schema v10, additive)** — `workspace_feature (workspace_id, feature, enabled, hlc…)`; the v9→v10 migration is the additive CREATE-IF-NOT-EXISTS pattern (empty table = all enabled, F2 — zero migration for existing workspaces). The applier is row-LWW by envelope (hlc, actor); the winning toggle derives the **membership-preserving archival** of the feature's managed classes (class registry `active` + the class node's `is_active` — what pickers/search/hubs already filter; `class_member_set` NEVER touched, F3-hide-surfaces-keep-data). The flips are pure active-bit projections: **no causality/timestamp writes** — racing toggles must converge byte-identical under either delivery order (verified by the store suite's forward/backward dumpDb tests).
- **Found + fixed en route (convergence, load-bearing):** two order-dependence traps in the original sketch. (1) The task-family seed-ensure cannot be gated on the toggle WINNING the LWW race — a losing enable must still author the family or the two delivery orders of a racing pair diverge on row existence; the ensure now rides every enable payload and normalizes the archival bit to the CURRENT row state. (2) Any `updated_at` bump on the flips diverges by delivery order (the flip lands under a different envelope per order) — removed; the toggle's HLC on the workspace_feature row is the only causality record.
- **F4 routing** — `class.delete` on a feature-managed class is applied as a feature-disable (LWW row + flips); the lossy plain delete (membership tombstoning) never runs on managed classes. Non-managed classes keep plain semantics (test-pinned).
- **The `tasks` enable authors the family** (constraint 5 — closes the §34.22 `:1622` row): the applier materializes the `task` class + the six property schemas + bindings at the fixed ids, INSERT-or-ignore (a client-authored family with random option ids is never clobbered; first writer wins). Deterministic option ids had to be minted — `TASK_STATUS_OPTION_UUIDS`/`TASK_PRIORITY_OPTION_UUIDS` continue the `…0004-…` select-option namespace (statuses …008–…00d, priorities …00e–…011; appended, never reused).
- **Read surface + web** — `Store.isFeatureEnabled/listFeatureRows/featureInstanceCount` (+ the F2 absent-row default) and the same three on both web clients (WorkerClient RPC-mirrored through worker-core). The **Workspace Settings Features tab** (third tab) renders the six toggles live — BooleanToggle primitive, per-feature description, the F3 instance count ("N existing objects…"), disabled with the honest "clients catching up" note. Feature chrome gating (tasks hub / journals section hides) rides the lockstep wave — the derived flags already hide the classes themselves everywhere.
- **Domain** — `features.ts`: the feature map (label/description/managed classes), the always-on list, `managedClassIds` / `featureForManagedClass` / `isAlwaysOnSystemClass`. Recorded deviation: `meeting` joins the always-on list (the §34.36 plain-seed ruling predates the toggle system — it never entered the toggle set).

**§34.34 B3 — `code_block`:** grammar `{type, language?, text}` (language = strict lowercase hint tag; text verbatim, no nested tokens); a **promotion survivor** alongside whiteboard/query (`stringifyContentAst`/`isTextOnlyContent`) — a code page is a real surface. Web renderer: read-only mono `<pre>` + language badge (token-only CSS). The editing surface (a CodeTextarea branch in the editor) is designed with the token but unshipped — no authoring gesture until lockstep. Fixture **`code-block.json`** pins the survivor through a real promote op. Mermaid: no bundling ruling, no new deps — rides a later wave.

**§34.34 B5 — `hr`:** grammar `{type}`; renderer `<hr>` (token-only CSS); deliberately NOT a promotion survivor (no prose — a rule in a page title is meaningless; fixture `hr.json` pins the flatten). The group render-flag half of B5 stays owed: §34.19 `:1128` block selection shipped its first half (Shift/Ctrl+click, drag select, action bar — §34.50) but the boxed-group flag is a wire item needing its own op + owner ruling — recorded, not approximated.

**§34.34 B8 — `embed_ref.view`:** additive optional strict field `"embed" | "small_card" | "wide_card"` (absent = the full transclusion — default behavior untouched; unknown values rejected). The web renderer routes card views to a new `EmbedCardView` (bounded identity card: icon + name, wide adds the one-line excerpt, click navigates via openNode — cards never transclude, so no cycle-guard interplay); projections without the injected renderer fall back to the full embed. Fixture **`embed-ref-view.json`**. The authoring gesture (a view picker on embeds) rides the lockstep wave.

**Export interim:** `buildBlocks` maps `code_block` → one inline-code paragraph span and `hr` → a paragraph flush (the IR has no fenced/thematic kinds yet) — honest lossless-enough interim, no undefined spans; a proper `code`/`hr` ExportBlock kind + the five serializers ride the post-lockstep export slice.

### 34.55 Features reshape — the toggles ARE the core class families (OWNER DIRECTIVE 2026-10-04, same-day TS reference update; LOCKSTEP-PENDING unchanged)

Owner directive (2026-10-04): the Features tab is for the **core class families** — each toggle is a seeded system-class family with built-in product logic that grows as needed. The part-1 feature set (§34.54: tasks/journals/readItLater/library/people/collections) is replaced by the five families: **tasks=task, events=event, meetings=meeting, sources=source, persons=person** (seeded titles verified against the §34.36.2/§34.36.3 catalog: `event` …0001-000000000040 is the calendar family root, `meeting` …039 extends it, `birthday` …041 extends it).

- **Protocol** — `WORKSPACE_FEATURES` in op-types.ts reshaped to the five; the retired ids (journals/readItLater/library/people/collections) are rejected outright by the strict schema (the op payloads in stored part-1-era logs would fail — recorded: no such log exists, the op shipped inert and no writer ever emitted it). `workspace-feature-set.json` reshaped: the tasks same-slot race stands, plus a single `events` disable and a `sources` off→on pair.
- **Domain** — `features.ts` rewritten: the five-family map (baseClass/label/powers — Tasks → "Tasks hub + checkbox gestures", Events → "The calendar day/month surfaces", Meetings → "Meeting quick-create + meeting logic", Sources → "The source family + citation import/export", Persons → "The people graph + contact fields"); `familyClassNames`/`managedClassIds` resolve the family set through `systemClassAncestors` (events → event+birthday+meeting; sources → the 10-strong family); `gatingFeaturesForClass` = own feature + managed ancestors (meeting ← meetings AND events; birthday ← events only); **F4 routing is base-only** (the owner's exact mapping — the five family bases route; extends-CHILDREN like `book`/`birthday` keep plain delete semantics; `meeting` IS a base — it routes, and the Flutter v3.0.0 port's note about a doc/test conflict traced to the §34.55 commit message's imprecise parenthetical, not the code: `featureForManagedClass` and the domain test agree); always-on list keeps the base system incl. journals/assets + the dropped features' classes (highlight/weblink/collection/agent/organization — plain vocabulary now).
- **Store** — the applier's blind family-wide flip is replaced by **per-class re-derivation** (`deriveFamilyClassBits`): each family class's archival bit = the AND of its gating features' current rows — re-enabling events does not un-archive a meetings-off meeting (test-pinned). Convergence invariants unchanged: pure active-bit projections, no causality/timestamp writes, the ensure rides every enable payload, forward/backward dumpDb equality holds.
- **Web** — the Features tab rows reshape per the directive: the class's effective icon (tinted by its effective color when set), its live title, the powers line, and the **ToggleSwitch** primitive (still inert — `FEATURE_TOGGLE_WRITES_ENABLED` stays false; lockstep still pending). Chrome gates read live via the new `featureGates.ts` helper (`isClassFamilyEnabled`): the **sidebar Tasks hub nav entry** hides while `tasks` is off, and the **calendar quick-create chips** (CalendarView + the settings Quick-Create editor) filter per the family walk — an `events`-off hides the meeting AND birthday chips with it. Reads re-run on the existing notification/cacheVersion paths; absent row = ON, so the gates are no-ops until a toggle lands.
- **Dropped features, recorded (owner directive):** journals/readItLater/library/collections/people are gone from the toggle set — the journals chain (year/month/day) and assets stay always-on base system; highlight/weblink/collection/agent/organization return to plain always-on class vocabulary. §34.35's F1 row updated.
