### 34.57 🔴 Protocol batch part 2 — PG5 element identity + PC4 binding `active` + PC6 date-node qualifiers: the three-client lockstep TS reference (2026-10-04 — TS reference SHIPPED same day; **LOCKSTEP-PENDING: no writer emits until the GTK/Flutter releases**)

Part 2 of the §34.48 protocol batch (owner-authorized; part 1 = §34.54). Same hard constraint: until the GTK (`v2.0.0-m6`+) / Flutter (`v2.0.0-m16`+) releases ship parsers, NOTHING may write the new payload keys to a live log — older clients fail loud on unknown opTypes/payload keys. The TS reference ships COMPLETE (strict schemas, schema v11, appliers, read models, query compiler, fixtures, both-adapter tests); every authoring surface stays inert (the PC4 Enabled toggle renders live state DISABLED — `BINDING_ACTIVE_WRITES_ENABLED = false`; the web property write paths are unchanged and keep emitting the legacy shapes they always did). *(Numbering note: the brief named this record §34.56; a concurrent covers-v2 wave landed its own §34.56 while this batch was in flight — this record takes the next free number. The covers commit `8cc0151c` also swept this batch's already-in-progress web/server working-tree edits into its tree; they were verified coherent and are cross-referenced, not reverted.)*

**PG5 — per-element value UUID + OR-Set multi-values** (`packages/protocol/src/op-types.ts`, `packages/store/src/{schema,appliers,property-values,effective,edges,content,store}.ts`, `packages/query/src/compiler.ts`):
- **Wire:** `property.set`/`property.unset` gain an OPTIONAL strict `elementId` (UUID). The `property_value` row id **IS** the element id: writer-minted UUIDv7 for element adds; the deterministic composite `node:schema:idx` for single-value slots and legacy positional writes (the pre-PG5 id generation — replayed stored logs apply byte-identical). `idx` becomes a per-element order hint (writers allocate; readers order by `(idx, element id)`; the PB4 gap-tolerance stands).
- **OR-Set:** adds of distinct elements never conflict; a remove tombstones the element (`property_value_element_tombstone`, strictly-greater full `(hlc, actor)` upsert — the `class.unassign` convention). **Add-wins generalized:** the membership comparator is **HLC-only** — on equal `(physical, logical)` the add wins regardless of actor (the classIds `>=` convention lifted to the two-table projection; the value/metadata overwrite per element keeps the full `(hlc, actor)` tuple like the pre-PG5 slot LWW). A re-issued add with a not-strictly-older HLC revives the element. **Single-value slots stay LWW at the element** (one deterministic element per slot — the existing `property-set-lww.json` fixture replays unchanged).
- **Positional legacy path** (payload without `elementId`): unchanged slot LWW + slot tombstones, now keyed by the deterministic row id; a positional `unset` suppresses the whole SLOT positionally (newer element adds at that idx survive) — old clients keep operating positionally for years.
- **Projection:** schema **v11** (additive pattern): `property_value` is REBUILT (the `UNIQUE(node, schema, idx)` retires — same-idx elements are legal; the v8 node-rebuild precedent), plus the element tombstone table. The **visible set** (live rows minus slot tombstones minus element tombstones) is derived ONCE in `visiblePropertyValueRows` (property-values.ts) and every read consults it: the effective model (which also keys its merge per ELEMENT — the old `(schema, idx)` merge key clobbered coexisting elements, caught by the new suite), the edge index, the FTS plaintext (M5), the store reads (`propertyValueCarriers`, `scalarPropertyValues`), the API projections (each property entry carries `elementId` — additive), and the query compiler (one shared `visibleValuePredicate` SQL fragment). The permanent-delete cleanup drops the subtree's element tombstones.
- **Deviations from the register, documented:** (1) "additive columns + tombstone table" landed as the tombstone table + the `class_property.active` column (PC4) + the `property_value` rebuild — no new `property_value` column is needed because the row id is the element id; (2) id generation (the register-deferred call): the §34.45 content-addressed convention — composite for positional, writer UUIDv7 for elements; (3) payload shape (register-deferred): optional `elementId`, absent = legacy positional carrier — fail-loud-on-missing would break the 183k-envelope replay gate (live logs carry pre-PG5 multi writes), so legacy adds normalize to the deterministic positional element instead (the bare-uuid precedent).

**PC4 — `class_property.active`** (`op-types.ts`, `schema.ts`, `appliers.ts`, `effective.ts`, web `PropertyDefinitionsSection.tsx` + `workspace-client.ts`, server binding route): the binding row gains `active INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` (additive; absent = active, so existing rows converge with zero migration); `class.property.set` gains `active?: boolean` (omitted = keep — the patch convention). Row LWW unchanged. The effective read filters candidates to `active = 1` — an inactive binding stops contributing defaults AND metadata (required/readonly/hideWhenEmpty/sequence, extends-inherited rows included) while the ROW survives (unlike `class.property.unset`) and authored values read as unbound (`boundBy` null). The query compiler's derived-defaults CTEs gained `cp.active = 1`. Web affordance per the brief: the PropertyDefinitions expanded row gains an **Enabled ToggleSwitch rendering live state, disabled with the honest lockstep note** (`BINDING_ACTIVE_WRITES_ENABLED` flips with the lockstep wave); inactive rows dim with a "disabled" chip. "(Own row wins over seeds)" — the flag rides the workspace's own binding row LWW; the family ensures are existence-gated INSERT-or-ignore, so a user flip is never re-clobbered by a seed re-emit.

**PC6 — date-node-backed qualifiers** (`property-values.ts`, `appliers.ts`, web `MetadataSection.tsx`, `packages/export` serializers): the reserved qualifier keys canonicalize as **date-node refs through the chain** (`{ "nodeId": <chain node> }` — the ensureDateChain pattern; refs stay existence-lenient until the chain exists, the text-carrier precedent). **Normalize-on-write:** the applier rewrites a well-formed `YYYY-MM-DD` string in `metadata.startDate`/`endDate` to the deterministic day-node id — ONLY on dateQualified schema rows, only those two keys, pure value rewriting (no graph side effects, no existence assertion). **Read-leniency:** every reader accepts both shapes — the panel's qualifier control formats refs from the deterministic id (`parseDateNodeId`, no lookup), export gained `qualifierDisplayOf` (the four serializers rendered `String(entry)` — a ref would have been `[object Object]` the moment normalization fired). The panel still WRITES the legacy string until the lockstep wave switches it (normalization makes the stored shape canonical in the meantime — a derived-state behavior, no old client fails loud, which is why PC6 rides the batch's gate rather than a hard one). Scheduled BEFORE qualifier data accrues per the register — the panel has shipped the string write for one day; the move now costs a normalize instead of a migration.

**Fixture gate 18→21.** New canonical fixtures (vendored-fixture pattern; per-file id ranges …07xx, envelope v3, ascending HLCs): **`property-value-elements.json`** (same-idx concurrent element adds coexist and order by element id; element remove + newer re-add revival; legacy positional add back-compat), **`class-property-active.json`** (bind with default → disable wins the row LWW race over an interleaved lower-HLC enable → authored value while inactive reads unbound → re-enable), **`property-date-qualifier.json`** (chain-created refs for both qualifier ends at idx 0; legacy ISO string at idx 1 that normalizes on write to the deterministic day-node id).

**Verification:** the full gate green at completion: **protocol 242, domain 39, store 365 BOTH adapters (the new `property-elements.test.ts` 44: PG5 OR-Set semantics incl. same-HLC add-wins under both delivery orders, edge/FTS/population consistency, carrier-trash on element unset, PC4 default/metadata suppression incl. extends-inherited rows + concurrent-flip both-orders, PC6 normalize/read-leniency; plus the corpus convergence tests — causal fixtures forward-replay byte-identical across BOTH adapters, concurrent same-HLC pairs byte-identical under EITHER order), sync 14, query 171 (incl. the new executor-level PG5/PC4 condition specs), export 166, server 157, web 794, cli 68**. Web typecheck clean. Attribution note: three web specs (`capture` ×2, `block-selection` ×1) were red from the concurrent covers-v2 seed change (the `+` picker / `listClasses` assumptions) and are repaired here minimally (pick the created class by id/name; filter the class registry by name) so the gate is green — flagged for the covers wave. The known `query-builder-guard` full-suite load flake stayed green on the final run.

**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.32 (PG5/PC4/PC6 rows ticked above; PG8's query-compiler property holes remain that row's own — the metadata-predicate/range arms stay deferred; this batch only kept the EXISTING arms consistent with the new visible set) · §34.48 (batch line updated — part 2 shipped, the group render-flag + collapse flag remain) · §34.54 (part-1 record stands; its "Remaining for part 2" list is this record) · §34.56 (the covers wave's record — the numbering collision + the swept-web-edits note is recorded in this entry's header; no work overlaps) · SCHEMA.md owed register (no open item falsified; the "m2m tombstones" item is this batch's) · §34.45 (the bare-uuid/numeric-string normalize-on-write precedent this batch mirrors for qualifiers; the unset-carrier guards ride both positional and element removes) · §34.51 (PG6's validation surface — PC6 qualifiers deliberately skip existence assertions; `image` still unchecked) · lockstep law: **fixtures gate 18→21, all inert until GTK m6+ / Flutter m16+**; GTK/Flutter porting follow-up = the elementId/active parsers + the qualifier ref reader + activating the web writers (the `BINDING_ACTIVE_WRITES_ENABLED` flip, the panel's element-id addressing on multi add/unset, the qualifier picker write).

### 34.59 Plugin manifest schema + server registry — SCHEMA+REGISTRY SHIPPED, RUNTIME PARKED (owner-authorized 2026-10-04)

Owner directive (same session as the prefs ruling): implement the plugins SCHEMA — the manifest grammar + the server registry ONLY; a plugin RUNTIME is explicitly PARKED (this record is the parking stake). The manifest schema + registry are the inert foundation the future runtime (AG7 — capability broker, subprocess host) consumes; nothing executes, loads, spawns, imports, exports, or subscribes anything anywhere in this slice.

*(Numbering note: the concurrent prefs wave's in-flight OpenAPI text and the covers/export waves' in-flight test titles cross-reference "§34.59" for their own slices; the owner brief assigned §34.59 to this record. Precedent §34.57: the waves that land second renumber their cross-refs — the prefs/covers/export records' numbers are theirs to assign.)*

- **Protocol (`packages/protocol/src/plugin-manifest.ts`, barrel-exported; no wire change — no fixture gate, no lockstep).** `pluginManifestSchema` is a versioned strict zod grammar: `manifestVersion: z.literal(1)` (breaking changes bump the literal via a protocol-batch decision), `id` = lowercase reverse-domain OR UUID (identity is never the display name), `version` = strict semver 2.0.0, `name`/`description`/`author`, `capabilities` (strict, all optional, DECLARED-only): `exportFormats [{id, label, mime}]` · `importers [{id, label, mime, mode: "file"|"text"}]` · `commands [{id, label}]` · `widgets [{id, label}]` (the least-designed member — ids/labels only until the runtime design fixes the widget contract), `entrypoint` (RESERVED — relative path, traversal rejected, stored only, never executed), `permissions` (RESERVED — validated enum: the §26 scope set + the §19 plugin-era additions `events.subscribe` + `network`; a parked runtime enforces nothing). Helpers: `parsePluginManifest`/`safeParsePluginManifest` (fail loud), `summarizeCapabilities` (the management-surface one-liner). Deviation recorded: the owner brief sketched `permissions: ["read:graph","write:ops"…]` as an example shape — the shipped vocabulary is the §26+§19 set instead, because plugins are "API clients with an embedded token" (§19) and a second permission dialect would double the story; the example's `read:graph`/`write:ops` names do not exist in any register.
- **Server (`apps/server/src/routes-plugins.ts` + `context.ts` + `app.ts` + `openapi.ts`).** `PluginRegistry` on the shared relay.db: `plugin (id, version, name, description, author, manifest JSON, enabled, installed_at, PK (id, version))` — the additive CREATE-IF-NOT-EXISTS migration; the composite PK makes install idempotency a natural key. Routes under /api, owner/admin-scoped via `requireAdmin` (the operator key = owner; otherwise an administrator account; a scoped API key additionally needs the `admin` scope — **the first enforcement of that reserved name**, recorded in the OpenAPI `x-api-key-scopes` mapping): `GET /plugins` (list), `POST /plugins` (install — body IS the manifest, strict-validated 422; idempotent on id+version → 200 `alreadyInstalled`; same id at a different version → 409, versioned updates ride the parked runtime), `DELETE /plugins/:id` (all versions), `POST /plugins/:id/enabled` ({enabled} → all versions of the id). All four rows in the OpenAPI route table (new "Plugins" tag; the `:id` parameter description gained a per-surface override since plugin ids are not UUIDv7 — the first non-uuid `:id` route). THE REGISTRY IS INERT DATA — the routes' doc comments say so; the load-bearing test proves no envelope ever reaches the op log (registry rows are server state, not log state — the prefs/shares ruling) and that rows survive a restart against the existing relay.db.
- **Web (`apps/web/src/ui/components/modals/PluginsSettingsTab.tsx` + `pluginsApi.ts`; Workspace Settings gains the Plugins tab — additive fourth tab).** Lists installed manifests (name, version, id, capability summary, enable ToggleSwitch, InlineConfirmButton uninstall) and an "Install manifest" CodeTextarea paste-JSON affordance that validates CLIENT-side first (JSON parse, then the protocol schema — issues listed with paths, nothing hits the network on invalid input) and surfaces server errors (e.g. the 409 version conflict) in an alert. Kit primitives only (Button, CodeTextarea, EmptyState, InlineConfirmButton, ToggleSwitch); the toggle rows pass a unique `id` (the shared default made duplicate labelledby ids — a pre-existing kit quirk the Features tab shares).
- **Capability set shipped (minimal + honest):** exportFormats, importers, commands, widgets — declarations only; the runtime (parked) owns consumption. No importers/exporters/commands/widgets actually run.
- **The exact runtime-parked boundary:** SHIPPED = manifest grammar (zod, normative in SCHEMA.md), registry table + 4 admin routes, OpenAPI rows + coverage, web management tab, docs. PARKED (AG7, M3 program — own plan when scheduled): the capability broker / permission proxy, the subprocess JSON-RPC host, manifest CONSUMPTION of any kind (an installed manifest changes zero behavior — the enable bit is read by nothing), versioned updates, event subscriptions/webhooks (AG6), trust prompts, the `api_version` negotiation, any loader that would resolve `entrypoint`. Until AG7 lands, `/api/plugins*` is a validated shelf.
- **Verification (final gate, this slice's files)** — protocol 258 (16 new manifest specs: valid minimal/full/UUID-id, every rejection — version literal, id shapes, semver, strict keys top+capability level, importer mode, empty labels, unknown permissions, entrypoint traversal, capabilities-required — plus the summary helper), server 187 all green (9 new: 401 matrix, full lifecycle, idempotent install + 409 version conflict, 422 rejections storing nothing, 404/422 toggle corners, the no-envelope-in-log proof, restart persistence, admin/non-admin gate, scoped-key admin-scope enforcement incl. the non-admin-minting-an-admin-key negative; a workspace-snapshot-rebuild timeout blip under host load was re-run clean), web plugins-settings 7 new (list + summary + toggle state, toggle POST + refresh, inline-confirm uninstall, non-JSON/schema-invalid paste never POSTs, valid install POST + refresh + paste clear, server 409 surfaced) + every WorkspaceSettingsModal describe in settings-modals green. Server typecheck clean; web typecheck clean for this slice (one red in `SharePageModal.tsx` is the concurrent shares wave's uncommitted file — flagged, not fixed). ### 34.60 §34.24 EXPORT RESIDUE — CSV view export + selection-scoped export + JSON archive (owner-authorized 2026-10-04 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner directive: implement the three parked §34.24 formats/surfaces — CSV view export, selection-scoped export, the JSON Tier-1 archive. *(Numbering note: the owner brief said "§34.59 sub-record", but the concurrent plugins wave landed §34.59 first — and the favorites/shares waves §34.61/§34.62 — before this record settled; per the §34.57 collision precedent the later wave renumbers. The §34.24 parked register + gap-register rows 13/14 cross-reference §34.60.)*

- **CSV view export** — `packages/export/src/csv.ts`: the dependency-free RFC-4180 serializer behind the package's options-bag discipline (`CsvExportOptions { bom (default ON — Excel UTF-8 detection), lineEnding (default CRLF) }`, `resolveCsvExportOptions`, `escapeCsvCell` — quote on `,`/`"`/CR/LF, double internal quotes, `renderCsv(header, rows)`). Web: the TableView toolbar's **Export CSV** (`apps/web/src/ui/views/TableView.tsx`) downloads the CURRENT view — visible columns × the FULL sorted result set (the 100-row window is display-only, never an export cut; hidden columns stay out) — with cells rendered exactly as on screen (names, class labels, locale created date, `propertyDisplayText`; raw ids never leak). Blob `text/csv;charset=utf-8`, filename `<exportFileName|table-export>.csv` (new optional `NodeCollectionProps.exportFileName`). One implementation — every table surface (classed nodes, query results, tasks) gets it.
- **Selection-scoped export** — the table's session row-selection gains **Export selected…** (count + button in the toolbar): it opens the existing `ExportPageModal` with `nodeUuids` = the checked ids — the modal's batch path IS the id filter (no new engine concept). Verified end-to-end: the batch zip carries exactly the two selected roots' files + a 2-entry manifest. Deviation recorded (superseded §34.69): cards/kanban had NO collection selection when this shipped (the §34.21 machinery was table-only) — **the follow-up slice SHIPPED same day (§34.69): flat cards and kanban gained per-card checkboxes + the same Export selected… affordance, and the table's CSV gained the optional selected-rows scope (Export selected CSV)**.
- **JSON archive** — `packages/export/src/json-archive.ts`: `buildJsonArchive(nodes, ctx, {now?})` + `renderJsonArchive` — the §34.12 Tier-1 projection as ONE versioned envelope: `{format: "notees-json-archive", version: 1, generatedAt, nodes[]}`; each node carries `id`, the Revision-11 booleans, `parentId`, a content-derived `displayName` (redundant by design), the **verbatim** `contentAst`/`classIds`/authored property values, position-ordered direct-`children` ids (both render zones; a slice cut above a child still records the id), and `edges[]` mined from the stream + values (`mention`/`embed`/`typedLink`/`class` chip/`asset`/`property` node-refs incl. arrays + date ranges — targets outside the slice record their id; classIds MEMBERSHIP is not an edge — the field itself carries it). Duplicate node ids collapse to the first occurrence. **CLI** — `notees export json --ids <uuid…> | --linked-to <uuid> | --class <id|title> [--depth N|fixpoint] [--output <file>]` (stdout default; the markdown bundle's exact selector/closure machinery via the new `apps/cli/src/json-export.ts`; one position-ordered children fetch per included node for the `children` metadata). **Web** — a sixth modal card (web-registry `WebExportFormatId` = `ExportFormatId | "json"`; the card is assembled web-side from package constants — the JSON archive is NODE-SET-shaped, so like `bundleMarkdown`/`csv.ts` it stays outside the IR-based `EXPORT_FORMATS` registry) delivered by `exportSubtreeJsonArchive`: ONE archive document over the union of the roots' slices (root + every inline-body descendant + child-page subtrees — blocks are verbatim records, NOT folded into a parent file like markdown; batch included — deliberately NOT a per-root zip). Filename `<first-root-slug>.json`; only the "Include child pages" option reaches the engine.
- **Registry-shape decision (recorded):** CSV + JSON archive do NOT join `EXPORT_FORMATS` — that registry's serializers consume the resolved per-subtree `ExportDocument` IR, while CSV input is view-shaped (visible columns × result rows) and the archive input is the verbatim node set (the IR folds/renames tokens — not backup-grade). Both follow the package's options-bag + purity disciplines and export from the index alongside the registry.

### 34.61 Cross-device favorites + recents — server-side per-user UI state (OWNER RULING 2026-10-04 on §34.29 #8 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner ruling: implement cross-device favorites/recents as **server-side per-user UI state**. The design law "device state is never an op" stands — favorites/recents are UI preferences, so they live in the sync server's per-user prefs store, NOT the operation log. Every client's derived DB semantics stay untouched; there is no envelope, no op type, no fixture gate, no client lockstep (GTK/Flutter have nothing to parse). This record IS the ruling's registration (SCHEMA.md normative section + the §34.29 #8 row ticked above).

- **Server (`apps/server/src/routes-prefs.ts` + `auth.ts` + `app.ts` + `openapi.ts`).** `user_prefs (user_id PK REFERENCES "user" ON DELETE CASCADE, favorites JSON, recents JSON, updated_at)` — the additive CREATE-IF-NOT-EXISTS migration in `AuthStorage`'s DDL (relay.db, beside the account). Routes in the account plugin group: `GET /api/me/prefs` (the account's lists + updatedAt; empty default when never written) and `PUT /api/me/prefs` (merge patch — each present list replaces its column wholesale; the client owns ordering, last-write-wins per list). Auth: `requireUser` — an account session OR a per-user API key (its owner); the operator key is not a user and gets 401. Validation is fail-loud zod strict: uuid-shaped node ids, order-preserving dedupe BEFORE the caps (favorites ≤ 500, recents ≤ 50), ≥ 1 list required. Both rows in the OpenAPI route table (Account tag) — the coverage gate passes. *(Numbering note: in flight this slice cross-referenced §34.59/§34.60; both were taken by concurrent waves' records that landed first — per the §34.57 precedent the second wave renumbers, so the shipped cross-refs say §34.61.)*
- **Web core (`apps/web/src/core/workspace-client.ts`, `worker-client.ts`).** `getPrefs()` / `patchPrefs(patch)` on BOTH client classes (the `AnyClient` seam stays honest): plain REST on the in-process path; RPC `getPrefs`/`patchPrefs` on the worker proxy, dispatched in `worker-core.ts`, with `store-worker.ts` forwarding the init message's serverUrl/apiKey into `WorkspaceClient.wrap` (the per-user prefs calls need the REST config in the worker — `fetchOperationsFor`'s sibling). **Offline semantics (chosen, simple + honest):** reads return the device-local cached copy (`notees.favorites`/`notees.recents`, the pre-existing keys — the palette's Recent section and every legacy reader keep working off the same source), tagged `source: "local"`; writes update the device cache immediately and PUT when a server answers — no durable queue, the next successful call syncs. Realms without localStorage (the worker) back the cache in memory, session-honestly.
- **Web UI (`apps/web/src/ui/components/nodePrefs.ts` + `Sidebar.tsx` + `FavoriteStar.tsx` + `PageView.tsx`).** One module-level store + `useNodePrefs` hook backs every surface: the sidebar's Favorites/Recents sections, the page-header star (`FavoriteStar` renders in PageView's default `headerActions` slot — title-row toolbar, class views keep their own), and the node menu / row-menu toggles. Boot: one GET, then the server copy is written through to the device keys + the legacy `notees:favorites`/`notees:recents` broadcasts — **the palette's Recent section reads the same source with zero changes**. Reconciliation is a merge, never a clobber: device-only recents prepend (they postdate this device's last successful push), device-only favorites append; a non-trivial merge pushes back so the server converges. Legacy writers (the node context menu's toggle, `recordRecent`/`removeRecent`) keep their device-local contract — the store listens to their broadcasts and syncs upward, so `App.openPage`'s recents write migrated to the server without App.tsx changing at all. A client lacking the prefs seam (hand-rolled test doubles) degrades to device-local lists instead of crashing the shell.
- **Surfaces recap:** star toggle on pages via (a) the node context menu (pre-existing item, now synced through the store), (b) the new page-header star, (c) the sidebar row star; the Favorites section lists starred nodes (click opens); Recents records every open (`recordRecent`, cap 12 → 50 to match the server) and syncs online. No `App.tsx` edit — the Sidebar owns its sections, the store owns the transport.
- **Verification** — server +8 new prefs specs (empty default, 401 matrix incl. operator-key rejection + API-key acceptance, round-trip + per-list patch, dedupe-before-cap, over-cap/unknown-key/empty-body 422s, per-user scoping, first-write upsert) + the OpenAPI coverage gate green; web +18 (node-prefs 16: core getPrefs/patchPrefs server+offline×2, merge helpers, store load-merge/nothing-clobbered, legacy-broadcast upward sync, toggle push, offline-push warn; worker-protocol 2: RPC round-trip memory fallback + REST config with the init credential) · the PageView-mounted star's prefs GET required one assertion fix in `asset-attachments.test.tsx` (count only `/api/assets` calls — the stub records every fetch). the sibling-flagged `settings-modals`/`protocol-batch` Sidebar-entry-point reds healed by the seam-degradation guard. Known concurrent-tree noise (not this slice's): the shares/plugins/export waves' uncommitted files (a web tsc red in share-modal.test.tsx, their spec ownership); the full-suite numbers land in the gate run.
- **Docs same pass** — SCHEMA.md (normative "Per-user UI state" section — the ruling + the store contract) · docs/ux.md (the today-table row) · docs/usage.md (sidebar item 2 + the palette Recent line) · this record + the §34.29 #8 row + decisions line + §34.30 M6's cross-ref. AGENTS.md unchanged (no invariant it describes changed).

### 34.62 Shares — READ-ONLY public page links (OWNER ruling 2026-10-04 — SHIPPED same day)

The M3 register's "shares" item (§34.19 P2 row, updated to 🟡 by this record), owner ruling received 2026-10-04: **read-only public share tokens.** A user shares a page → the server mints an unguessable token; anyone with the link gets a read-only view of that page's content. No write path, no account needed for the viewer. Share state is **server-side coordination (like prefs), NOT operation-log state** — no new op type, no wire change, no fixtures, no GTK/Flutter lockstep owed (the write-collaboration share variant — per-user shares, a Shares Inbox, edit over a link — stays parked as an M3 conversation; this record ships the public-read slice only). *(Numbering note: the owner brief named this "§34.59 sub-record"; §34.59 plugins and §34.60 export-residue landed first, §34.61 prefs took the next slot while this record was first written, and this wave's first append was lost in a concurrent stage — per the §34.57 collision precedent the later wave renumbers: this is §34.62, and every cross-reference this wave wrote carries it. The tree-wide "§34.61" sweep that retargeted the PLUGINS cross-refs is the prefs wave's to reconcile — the plugins record remains §34.59.)*

- **Server** — `apps/server/src/shares.ts` (`ShareStorage`, the additive `share_tokens` migration in relay.db beside the account tables: token/workspace_id/node_id/created_by/created_at/expires_at/revoked_at; the token is 24 random bytes base64url, stored as-is — a relay.db leak already exposes the workspace's whole derived content, so hashing buys nothing the threat model cares about, recorded in the OpenAPI description) and `apps/server/src/routes-shares.ts` (management surface + the public route) + `apps/server/src/share-render.ts` (the public serializer). Management: `POST /api/shares {nodeId, expiresAt?}` → 201 `{share: {token, urlPath, …}}`, `GET /api/shares?nodeId=`, `DELETE /api/shares/:token` — owner/admin gate (`requireShareAdmin`: the operator key, the admin flag, or owner membership on the default workspace; the same role idiom as workspace rename/delete). Minting validates: node exists + active (404), classes rejected (422 — classes are workspaces/databases, not documents), `expiresAt` must be future. The public surface is a ROOT-level `GET /s/:token` — **unauthenticated and GET-only BY DESIGN** (unguessable tokens; POST/PATCH/DELETE fall into the 404 wall): the answer is one self-contained HTML document projected by the **export package's HTML serializer** over the `ExportDocument` IR (title + block tree as nested lists + properties; escaping-correct, cycle-guarded, no JavaScript, no external resource — the reuse the brief allowed, no parallel serializer written), carrying `cache-control: no-store`, `x-content-type-options: nosniff`, `referrer-policy: no-referrer`, and a strict `content-security-policy` (default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'). **Threat note (documented in the OpenAPI entries + docs/usage.md):** possession of the URL is the capability; NO directory listing — unknown, revoked, expired tokens and trashed pages answer the *same* 404 (no enumeration oracle); revocation takes effect on the next request; expiry dies on its own. App wiring: `context.ts` owns `ctx.shares` (closes with the rest), `app.ts` registers the management plugin under /api (per-route auth, like the account surface — outside the scope-enforced object group) and the public route at the root.
- **OpenAPI** — four entries registered (`POST/GET /api/shares`, `DELETE /api/shares/:token`, `GET /s/:token` — the latter `public`), each carrying the threat-note description; the route-coverage gate passes both directions. The params builder gained the format override for non-uuid path params (share tokens are opaque base64url — the §34.59-plugins `params.id` precedent generalized). New "Shares" tag.
- **Web** — two NEW files, `apps/web/src/ui/components/modals/shareApi.ts` (fetch helpers mirroring workspaceApi's shape) + `SharePageModal.tsx` (kit primitives only: Modal/Button/Spinner/EmptyState/InlineConfirmButton; lists the page's shares on open, creates on demand, copies the composed URL through the shared clipboard seam with a transient "Copied", revokes via the two-step inline confirm; revoked rows stay as dimmed history). Menu wiring follows the `onExport` idiom with an optional `ShareTarget {serverUrl, credential}` prop: `NodeContextMenu` gains `onShare` (the item shows for pages with a target — never classes), `NodeMenuButton` + `PageView` render the modal from their latch state, `NodeView`/`App` thread `shareTarget` (absent offline — no server to mint against — and in peek cards).
- **Deviations recorded** — (1) record number §34.62, not the brief's §34.59 (collision precedent §34.57 — three waves raced the same brief line; plugins/export/prefs landed first). (2) The modal intentionally has NO expiry picker — the server honors `expiresAt` (agents/CLI), the UI keeps the one honest default "links live until revoked" (minimal per brief). (3) Classes are not shareable (422) — the public render is a document view. (4) Shares are default-workspace-scoped, matching the object API's v1 authorization. (5) The share VIEW renders `embed_ref` targets as the visible `[[uuid]]` convention (no `nodeOf` inlining — the single-file export default); asset tokens render as placeholders (no bytes in the public view).
- **Docs same pass** — `docs/usage.md` (new "Sharing a page" section: create/visitor/revoke + the honest threat model; the web-app "What works today" gains item 11; the API quick-reference gains the three management rows + the public view row; the accounts paragraph's "multi-user hardening" line updated to "read-only shares ship; roles/write-collab/registration stay M3") · `docs/ux.md` (new "Sharing — read-only public links" section: the link is the whole contract, the viewer gets a document-not-the-app, the small manager, the parked variant) · AGENTS.md (`apps/server` line gains coordination state + the public share view) · this record + the §34.19 P2 "Shares" row → 🟡 read-only slice shipped / write-collab parked. **SCHEMA.md intentionally untouched** — no wire, no op, no model change; shares are coordination state.
- **Verification** — server: `apps/server` 187 tests green (13 new in `test/shares.test.ts`: mint shape/createdBy, list + nodeId filter, immediate revocation + history listing, future/past expiry, fail-loud mints (unknown node 404 / class 422 / malformed 422), 401 gates on all three routes, 403 stranger + 403 plain member vs owner/admin/operator pass, the public render (content present + escaped, NO script/form, outline lists, the four security headers), GET-only 404 wall, indistinguishable 404s (unknown/revoked/expired), trash kills the share) + `tsc --noEmit` clean. Web: 7 new `test/share-modal.test.tsx` specs (list-on-open + URL composition, create + refresh, revoke through the inline confirm + dimmed history, copy through the clipboard seam, empty state, the menu item's page/class/onShare-absent gates) green; the share files typecheck clean. **Known concurrent-tree noise (not this slice's):** the prefs wave's `test/node-prefs.test.tsx` tsc reds (their file) and their `nodePrefs` server-prefs fetch on PageView mount adding a second fetch that reds their `asset-attachments` spec counter (flagged in §34.61's record too); the plugins cross-ref sweep noted above.
- **Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.19 P2 (this record ships the row — updated) · §34.29 #8 (the "server-side UI state, not op-log" ruling — prefs and shares are the two instances of the category) · §34.59 (plugins) + §34.60 (export residue) + §34.61 (prefs) — the numbering collisions, resolved per the §34.57 precedent · §34.33 AG4 (the route-coverage gate that forced the OpenAPI entries) · SCHEMA.md (deliberately untouched — no model/wire change) · GTK/Flutter: no wire change → no lockstep; the share view is plain HTML, so the mobile clients open links in a browser unchanged.
### 34.63 Recurrence — compute-on-read RRULE-lite on date values (OWNER ruling 2026-10-04 on §34.28 #6 — SHIPPED same day)

The §34.28 #6 register row demanded a spec decision first — owner ruling received 2026-10-04: **compute-on-read** (the register's 🟢 option). A recurring event is **ONE node** whose date value carries the rule as additive metadata (`metadata.repeat`, the startDate/endDate precedent) — **no new op, no wire change, no fixtures, no GTK/Flutter lockstep**. Occurrences are **virtual** (design law: no hidden objects): nothing is materialized per occurrence, editing the event edits the series, and a client that doesn't know the key reads an ordinary date value.

- **Grammar** (`packages/domain/src/recurrence.ts`, fail-loud parse/format): canonical string `<freq>` or `<freq>/<interval>`; freq ∈ `daily` | `weekly` | `weekdays` | `monthly` | `yearly`; interval 2..999 (1 is the default, never written; `/1` normalizes away); `weekdays` (Mon–Fri) rejects an interval. Parse throws on garbage with the vocabulary in the message. **Expansion** `occurrenceIsosOf(series, fromIso, toIso, cap = 366)`: the value's own day is the anchor (never stored in the rule — one source of truth); inclusive window; the anchor is occurrence #0; monthly skips months lacking the day-of-month (Jan 31 → no February), yearly does the same (Feb 29 skips non-leap years) — RRULE-style; windows wider than the cap TRUNCATE (callers size the window — the calendar reads ±40 days around the visible month). A present-but-corrupt rule throws — corrupt metadata never reads as "does not repeat".
- **Web reads** (`calendarViewUtils.ts` / `calendarRows.ts` / `CalendarView.tsx`): `recurringRowsOf` scans the document-chrome nodes' authored date values for a rule (the only materialized hook — occurrences have none), one series per node; `datedRowsForDay` unions the day node's materialized backlink set with the recurring events occurring that day (deduped by id — the anchor day already backlinks); the day view's Dated section + week agenda render the union; `partitionOpenTasks` places a repeating task in Scheduled on every occurrence day and **never** in Overdue (missed occurrences roll forward); the month grid + week strip dot every occurrence day via a ±40-day expanded iso set composed with the §34.28 #11 backlink dot. Recurring rows render ONE honest row with a ⟳ marker (icon + label + title) — never phantom rows.
- **Authoring**: `pickers/RepeatPicker.tsx` (kit Dropdown underneath; None/Daily/Weekly/Weekdays/Monthly/Yearly; interval-bearing stored values render their honest label and any pick overwrites) — wired three ways: the Calendar quick-create bar (device state; the created value's metadata), the metadata panel's date pills (`DatePropertyRow` — per-pill icon picker; the date re-pick now preserves metadata, so the rule survives moving the event), and the shared `DateSlotControl`'s optional repeat slot (unused by the table/range call sites — available for day-precision event cells).
- **Docs same pass**: SCHEMA.md new normative "Recurrence" section (grammar + compute-on-read ruling + expansion semantics + scope limits) with a bullet from "Dates" · `docs/usage.md` (Calendar: the quick-create ⟳ control + a "Repeating events" bullet) · `docs/ux.md` (the calendar paragraph gains the recurrence bullet; the "Today" line's "not shipped: recurrence" flips to shipped with the parked completion-history note). AGENTS.md needs no change (nothing it documents shifted). The §34.28 #6 register row and the §34.19 TasksPopup row are updated in place.
- **Verification** — domain: `packages/domain` 61 tests green (22 new in `test/recurrence.test.ts`: grammar round-trips, garbage fail-loud incl. `weekdays/2`/interval-0/1000/leading-zero normalization, month-boundary/leap-year/interval/cap/far-window expansions, reversed-window + bad-cap + bad-anchor throws), dist rebuilt. Web: typecheck clean; the FULL web suite green at **850** — 8 new specs (`test/recurrence.test.tsx` 7: virtual-occurrence listing + marker, repeating-task partition, occurrence dots, quick-create write + anchor day, panel picker write + re-pick preservation, non-recurring stays plain, corrupt-rule fail-loud read; plus 1 new `partitionOpenTasks` occurrence case in `calendar-view-utils.test.ts`); the pre-existing calendar suites (52 tests) all green unchanged.
- **Deviations registered** — (1) The brief's `occurrenceIsosOf(rule, fromIso, toIso, cap?)` signature takes the rule + anchor as one `RecurrenceSeries` object `{ rule, anchorIso }` — the anchor is the value's own day and must never be duplicated inside the stored grammar (single-source-of-truth law); the domain section documents the shape. ### 34.64 Global undo/redo — the session-local op-inverse journal (owner-authorized 2026-10-04 — SHIPPED same day)

The register's "client-side op-inverse journal" program (§34.19 P0 row — the "biggest single UX hole"; listed as a separate program in §34.48), owner-authorized 2026-10-04. **Design law honored: the op log stays the only authority — undo is a pure CLIENT convenience that composes EXISTING ops; no new op type, no wire change, no fixtures, no GTK/Flutter lockstep.** A session-local (in-memory, per client = per tab) journal records every locally-authored envelope the client successfully applies through its outbox path, with its INVERSE intent captured at apply time (the pre-op snapshot lives in the journal entry). Undo/redo compose the inverses into fresh envelopes through the normal write path, so an undone edit syncs like any edit.

- **Core (`apps/web/src/core/undo-journal.ts`, new).** `invertEnvelope(envelope, capture)` — the full inversion matrix over the existing catalog: object.update restores exactly the touched fields from the captured snapshot; object.create → object.delete (soft; the OR-Set add carrier behind assign class/tag inverts per added membership as class.unassign/tag.unassign); object.delete → object.restore (PERMANENT deletes are data loss — not journaled); object.restore → soft delete; object.move → prior parent + the prior sibling anchor (afterId, else beforeId; roots carry none — root order is name-sorted); property.set → set with the captured prior row (element id carried) or unset when the slot was empty (a PG5 element add → element-addressed remove); property.unset → set restoring the captured row; class.unassign/tag.unassign → the object.create add carrier; class.reorder → the prior effective order; class.property.set → restores touched binding fields (first bind → unset); class.property.unset → restores the full captured row; class.setExtends → the prior parent set; class.create → class.delete (**class.delete itself is NOT journaled** — the applier tombstones memberships); class/propertySchema update/delete inverses (full-row captures); asset.attach/detach and collection.member.* complements (membership captured — redundant flips are no-ops, not journaled); workspace.feature.set → the captured prior toggle. Structural no-ops detected at capture time (restore-on-active, unassign-of-unassigned, …) are skipped, not journaled. The `UndoJournal` class: bounded stacks (200 entries both sides), contiguous same-node contentAst-only updates inside a 1500 ms window coalesce into ONE entry (paragraph-grained typing-undo; the coalesced entry keeps the ORIGINAL inverse and the LATEST apply op, so redo lands the final text), `batch()` groups a multi-envelope gesture into one entry (inverses reversed — promote-to-block's unset+restore undo together), labels "Undo <verb>"/"Redo <verb>" derived from the op type with gesture refinements ("edit text", "assign class", "promote"…). Both stacks store {applySpecs, inverseSpecs} and BOTH pop directions apply the popped entry's inverseSpecs — redo reverses the reversal (the redo entry's inverseSpecs ARE the original gesture's ops, freshly captured when the undo applied); the undo/redo loops relabel the opposite-side entry with the ORIGINAL verb, so the chrome reads "Redo delete", never "Redo restore".
- **WorkspaceClient integration (`workspace-client.ts`).** One private write seam `enqueueLocal(envelope, mode, verb?)`: capture-before-apply, apply via the engine (outbox + durable local log exactly as before), journal-after-success — a throwing apply never reaches the outbox, so it never reaches the journal. Every existing write method funnels through it; `promotePropertyCarrier` batches its two envelopes into one entry. `undoState()` (availability + labels), `undo()`, `redo()` — the inverses are built with fresh HLCs through `buildEnvelope` and applied through the SAME seam, journaled to the opposite stack (the standard model: undoing is journaled on the redo side, never re-journaled on the undo side). A failed undo/redo apply restores the popped entry (unspent) and rethrows. The bootstrap backlog replay deliberately bypasses the journal (replays are not new gestures), and `bootstrapWorkspace` clears it (workspace switch = fresh session journal). `OutlinerClient` (outliner-context) gains the three methods so view-level surfaces reach the journal without shell plumbing.
- **Worker plumbing.** The journal lives worker-side (the worker owns the store and the outbox); `worker-core.ts` gains `undoState`/`undo`/`redo` RPC cases and `WorkerClient` gains the async proxies — the `AnyClient` union stays honest, both classes async.
- **Web wiring (`App.tsx`, `CommandPalette.tsx`).** `undoRedoKeyHandler` (exported for tests): Ctrl/Cmd+Z undo, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z (or Ctrl/Cmd+Y) redo — the **editor-interaction rule** (documented in code + docs/ux.md): the guard yields to ALL text fields and contenteditables, so while the outliner editor owns the focus its native text-scoped undo keeps the keystroke and the global journal stays out of the way; every other focus (read mode, block selected, structural gestures, property edits, deletes, moves, renames) runs the journal. preventDefault fires only when the journal can act. The App keeps the journal state in a coalesced macrotask refresh off every client notification (plus a sync ref for the chord handler). The palette's action registry gains **Undo/Redo rows that appear only when available, carrying the journal labels** ("Undo edit text") — the palette is the Edit-menu surface this pass.
- **Multi-tab honesty** (code notes + docs): per-tab session-local journal — undo never claims cross-tab or cross-device; another device's ops apply to the view but never enter the journal; a reload starts empty; the unpushed backlog replays unjournaled.
- **Wire warts documented in the module header** (restores pick the closest honest value, noted rather than approximated silently): object.update/class.update cannot express a prior-NULL icon/description (the payload grammar has no clear) — such updates are NOT journaled; class.property.set cannot re-express a SQL-NULL default (restored as explicit JSON null) and the applier coerces payload null → 0/false for the nullable flags (a NULL prior restores as false — the "cleared" value the UI itself can only write); propertySchema.update's NULL datePrecision restores as the explicit "day" default.
- **Verification** — new `test/undo-journal.test.ts` (47): the full inversion matrix per op family over a fake capture source, journal mechanics (stacks, redo-clearing, bounds, coalescing window + node scoping + non-text exclusion, batch grouping + reversed inverses, take/restore, labels, clear), and 17 WorkspaceClient integration specs over sql.js + MemoryRelay (create/update/delete/restore/move-with-position/property set+unset/assign+unassign class+tag/setExtends/binding set+unset round trips, coalesced typing redo landing the final text, permanent-delete + class.delete skip lists, multi-tab honesty, undo riding the outbox into a second client). New `test/undo-keymap.test.tsx` (8): the editor-interaction rule (contentEditable + form fields keep the keystroke, empty journal no-ops, chord variants) and the palette rows (labels, availability gating, the wired redo click running the real journal). `search-palette.test.tsx`'s harness gained the new props. **Gate: full web suite green — 91 files / 918 tests, exit 0** (`tsc --noEmit` clean for this slice's files; the residual protocol-batch red belongs to the concurrent embed wave's in-flight file). **Known concurrent-tree noise (not this slice's):** `test/query-block.test.tsx`'s "aggregated AST renders the aggregate grid" is a PRE-EXISTING timing flake — it counts workspace class nodes while `PageView`'s unawaited cover-family self-heal (`ensureCoverFamily`, §34.27 L2) authors 3 system classes (asset/source/cover) asynchronously; it fails with and without this slice's changes (verified by stash-revert runs), passes in isolation, and did not fire in the gate run. Flagged for the owners of that test/self-heal.
- **Docs same pass** — `docs/ux.md` new "Undo and redo — the session journal" section (the model, per-tab honesty, the bounded/coalesced journal, the coverage list, the two not-undoable deletes, the keystroke rule) · `docs/usage.md` (the web-app "What works today" gains the Undo & redo item + the global-chords line stays accurate) · this record + the §34.19 P0 row below + the §34.48 separate-programs line. AGENTS.md `apps/web` line gains the journal sentence; `.plans/dev/architecture.md`'s web paragraph gains the core file.
- **Deviations registered** — (1) Record number §34.64 (the brief's "§34.6x next free" — §34.63 recurrence landed first; per the §34.57 precedent the later wave numbers itself). (2) The brief's "Edit menu/palette" surfaces shipped as the palette action registry only — no app-wide Edit menu exists in the shell; topbar Undo/Redo BUTTONS stay with the §34.19 "Topbar right" row (still 🟡). (3) The v1 history-menu-with-jump-to half of the §34.19 row is NOT ported — the journal is a bounded in-memory stack, not a browsable history; recorded as owed in the row. (4) No editor-buffer integration: the editor's text undo stays browser-native contentEditable behavior (the editor has no internal undo buffer to delegate from — the honest simple rule the brief allowed). (5) Wire warts above — three restore shapes the payload grammar cannot express exactly; each is skipped or restored-to-the-applier-sanctioned equivalent and documented in the module header.

### 34.66 PG1 schema-at-capture + the visible rough-edges batch (owner-authorized 2026-10-04 — SHIPPED same day; numbered §34.66 — the class-page naming + default-mirror sweep committed mid-wave claiming §34.65, 221001a6)

Two owner-authorized slices, one pass, both pure web chrome over the shipped wire — **no new op types, no payload changes, zero protocol/lockstep gate** (PG1 composes the existing `propertySchema.create`; the embed picker rides the shipped `embed_ref.view`; the code surface writes the shipped `code_block` token). Sibling waves owned `core/App.tsx`/undo this round — the only App.tsx hunks here are the two additive integration lines the FAB + drawer dismissal physically require (one import + one mount + the `drawerDismissHandler` effect), landed clear of the undo wave's edits.

**SLICE 1 — PG1 schema-at-capture (§34.32 PG1, the Tana flagship gesture).**
- **The verb popover gains the bound-verb path** (`VerbPopover.tsx`): the typed verb live-matches the workspace's property schemas — an exact (case-insensitive) name hit offers **"Bind to property …"** (`onBind` writes the mark with `verb: { propertySchemaId }`); a name nobody has used offers **"Create property '…' and bind"** — the caller's async flow authors `propertySchema.create` typed `object`, `multi: true`, `targetClassFilter: []` and binds the mark to the fresh id in the same gesture; pending + error states surface in the row. The plain **Link** submit keeps the free-string verb byte-identical (the existing path, untouched).
- **BlockTextEditor** wires it: `writeVerbMark` (shared splice), `createAndBindVerb`, the `schemas`/`onBind`/`onCreateAndBind` props. **Right-clicking an existing typed-link word opens the LinkEditModal's new verb mode** (prose-span hit test, mirroring the mention flow): the verb field pre-fills (bound verbs resolve the schema name), the locator edits, the same create-and-bind row lives under the field (the broken-link/create-flow sibling), and Save rewrites the verb — free-text when unmatched-without-bind, bound when the name matches, created-and-bound via the row.
- **Bound verbs render like the designed path:** `InlineTokens` gains `resolveVerb` (the mark's tooltip shows the schema NAME, not the raw id; `data-verb-bound` marks it), wired through BlockRow, EmbedView (incl. recursion), and DeckView. **Edges verified:** the mark's schema id projects into the derived edge index (`edges.ts` carries verb = propertySchemaId — confirmed by the round-trip spec). **Honest boundary:** a bound verb is a mark on a word (target resolution is the owner-deferred M2 design), so per-node backlinks don't list it and the QueryAST has no typed-link-verb arm — property-VALUE queries over the same schema are the designed filter path once values exist; registering the gap rather than approximating a query-arm change outside this wave's files. **The backlinks half of the gap CLOSED 2026-10-04 (§34.69):** the designed targeted path — a property value over the bound schema — already projects verb = propertySchemaId into the edge index, and the linked-references read + both backlink surfaces now carry the verb and render the schema's name (a quiet pill per row); the query AST arm and any mark-target resolution stay M2.
- Tests: `verb-create-bind.test.tsx` (7): the create-and-bind round trip (schema shape, mark id, edge projection, read-mode name), the exact-hit bind (no schema authored), the free-string path untouched (no schema authored), the right-click → modal → save rewrite, and 3 modal-row specs (create row writes the bound shape, match note saves bound, miss saves free-text).

Mixed content keeps the ordinary prose editor (the surface is the pure-code block only). Tests: `code-block-editing.test.tsx` (4).

BlockRow/EmbedView (recursion rebinds the host to the carrying block)/DeckView thread the write context; read-only projections without it (the public share render) show no switcher. ### 34.67 Popup dismissal — the shared seam + the audit-fix batch (owner bug report 2026-10-04: "most, if not all, of the popups don't close when pressing Esc or clicking outside" — SHIPPED same day)

Pure web chrome — **no wire change, no fixtures, no lockstep**. One shared kit hook now carries the platform convention (Esc closes, outside-click closes, inside presses never dismiss); every popup that lacked honest dismissal got the layer; the ones that already had it were verified and left alone.

**The seam — `usePopupDismissal`** (`apps/web/src/ui/components/ui/usePopupDismissal.ts`, barrel-exported from the kit `index.ts`; documented as the §34.67 convention). One implementation: (1) **Escape** at document level closes while open, *unless the keydown originated inside the popup* — in-popup Escape belongs to the popup's own children (they may consume it for sub-states, stopPropagation discipline; popups whose children don't handle Escape wire `onKeyDown` at the popup root to close); (2) **pointer-down outside** the popup — and outside any declared `anchorRefs` (the trigger button — clicking it toggles instead of dismissing) — closes; (3) both listeners install on open and clean up on close/unmount; close callbacks ride refs so re-renders never re-subscribe. `anchorRefs` exempts the outside-pointer check only — Escape on an anchor still closes.

**The audit (every popup surface in `apps/web/src`, broken → fixed vs already-working):**
- **Already working (verified, untouched):** the kit `Modal` (Esc via the overlay-surface stack + backdrop click — every Modal-based dialog inherits it: QuickCreate, SharePage, Confirmation, settings/plugins/export/gallery/picker modals) · kit `ContextMenu` (stack Esc + outside-click — all context menus: ViewTabs, WhiteboardCanvas, TableView, MetadataSection, NodeContextMenu, NodeLinkContextMenu) · kit `Dropdown` (incl. `RepeatPicker`) · kit `ButtonWithPanel` · kit `ui/CalendarPopup` (topbar) · kit `ImageModal` · `PresentationOverlay` (stack Esc) · `NodeSelector` in every mode (own Esc + outside-click — the anchored consumers inherit it: @/#/+ suggestion popups, extends picker, template picker, breadcrumb parent picker, cover flows, share flows) · `TriggerPopup` (slash — the editor forwards Esc; own outside-click) · `TemplateListPopup`/`DatePickerPopup` (Esc in their autofocused inputs + outside-click — hardened below) · `CommandPalette` (backdrop + Esc) · `WorkspaceSwitcher` · `FindReplaceWidget` · `LinkEditModal` (Esc/backdrop).
- **Broken → fixed (10 surfaces):** `ColorButton`'s picker popover (Esc missing — the whiteboard/table/property color dots) · `IconPickerPopup` (Esc missing) · `pickers/CalendarPopup` (Esc missing) · `DatePickerPopup` + `TemplateListPopup` (Esc only reachable while the filter input held focus — a day/month/year or row click moved focus and stranded the popup; document-level Esc + a root in-popup handler) · `NodePills`' "+N" overflow popup and its Change-color swatch popup · `NodePill`'s right-click color row (Esc missing; backdrop click already closed) · `VerbPopover` (outside-click missing — Esc already cancelled) · `SelectionPropertyControl`'s options picker (Esc missing).
- **Findings recorded, not (yet) changed:** the app-level `components/modals/overlayHooks.ts` stack is dead code (its only import is `useCopiedState` from ExportPageModal — the live Escape stack is the kit's `ui/overlay-hooks.ts`); `NodeSelector`'s hand-rolled dismissal predates the seam and works — migrating it is a cleanup, not a bug fix; `ContextMenu`'s `.ep--popup`/`.color-btn-picker` outside-click exemption list is the pre-existing cross-popup guard, left as-is.

**Integration pattern (surgical, no redesigns):** each fixed popup adds the hook plus — only where its children don't already handle Escape — a root `onKeyDown` Escape. Several popups' ad-hoc `mousedown`-outside effects were replaced by the hook (same semantics, pointerdown). Backdrop-div popups (NodePills/NodePill) keep their backdrops — the hook's outside-pointer agrees with them — and gain only Escape.

**Verification.** Web typecheck clean. **Full web suite green: 93 files / 956 tests, exit 0** (+1 file `test/popup-dismissal.test.tsx`, +18 specs; baseline §34.66's 92/938). New suite: the **dismissal matrix** (Esc closes / outside-click closes / inside-press does NOT close) over the representative set the brief named — the kit color popover, a kit ContextMenu, the icon picker, the slash popup (editor harness), an anchored NodeSelector mention picker — plus **one regression per fixed family** (pickers CalendarPopup, DatePickerPopup, TemplateListPopup, NodePills overflow + color menu, NodePill color row, VerbPopover outside-click, SelectionPropertyControl). Test-harness note recorded: editor-harness specs explicitly unmount + settle before `afterEach` closes the sql.js client — teardown writes are fire-and-forget and reject "Database closed" otherwise (the file's afterEach runs before setup.ts's testing-library cleanup).
**Docs same pass** — `docs/ux.md` gains the "Popups — one dismissal convention" section (Esc + outside-click, inside presses never dismiss) · `AGENTS.md`'s primitives paragraph lists the new hook · this record.
**Deviations registered** — (1) The brief's "apply to EVERY popup" was scoped by the audit to the ones lacking honest dismissal; the already-working list above is the evidence, and unifying `NodeSelector`/the dead app-level stack onto the seam is recorded cleanup, not shipped. (2) The hook's Escape contract skips keydowns that originate inside the popup (per the brief) — popups whose interiors ignore Escape got a root-level handler instead of the hook closing blindly, so a consumed Escape (a future sub-state) can still stopPropagation. (3) No new npm deps. (4) `pickers/CalendarPopup` has no live caller left (App uses the kit `ui/CalendarPopup`) — fixed anyway as the shared grid surface, noted for a future cleanup pass.
**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.66's record (this wave does not reopen its rows; VerbPopover/LinkEditModal cross-refs stay accurate — the popover gained outside-click, its Esc is unchanged) · §34.19 capture gestures (the @/#/+ pickers' dismissal was already honest — no row change) · SCHEMA.md (no model/wire change — deliberately untouched) · lockstep law: zero wire change → no fixture gate, no three-client lockstep.
### 34.68 The 3.1.0 release — the owner-directive wave (2026-10-04)

Everything the owner ordered after 3.0.0: favorites/recents (§34.61, server-side per-user UI state — the law preserved), shares (§34.62, read-only public tokens + the no-app /s/:token view), plugins schema+registry (§34.59, runtime parked), export residue (§34.60: CSV/selection/JSON), recurrence (§34.63, compute-on-read per the ruling), global undo (§34.64, the op-inverse session journal), schema-at-capture (§34.66, PG1), the rough edges (§34.66: code editing, embed picker, F3, whiteboard, tables, mobile), one popup dismissal convention (§34.67, the usePopupDismissal kit hook + 10 fixes), covers as dedicated header chrome, the dead-carrier fix, the class-properties rename + default-mirror sweep (§34.65), the fleet empty-body Content-Type fix. The v3.1.0 tag was force-moved off a v1-era commit (same precedent as v3.0.0; the old commit stays reachable as a main ancestor).
