### 34.69 The §34.68 follow-up batch — backlinks verbs, occurrence completion, topbar undo, the snapshot cliff, selection export, NodeSelector dismissal, the history menu (owner-authorized 2026-10-04 — SHIPPED same day)

Every follow-up §34.68 registered, in one wire-neutral pass (metadata/derived/config/read-model only — **no new op types, no payload changes, no fixture gate, no GTK/Flutter lockstep**). Seven slices:

**1. Bound-verb backlinks (the §34.66 boundary follow-up).** The edge index already projected bound verbs (verb = propertySchemaId) but the read dropped the verb on the floor: `ReferenceEntry` gains `verb` (the edge's verb; null for mentions/unlinked), `getLinkedReferences` carries it, and the shared `ReferenceList` (page sections AND the block-level backlink gutter — both render it) resolves it to the schema NAME as a quiet per-row pill (`.nt-ref-verb`, "via <name>"). Honest scope (recorded, not approximated): the targeted edge a bound verb participates in is the **property VALUE over the bound schema** — the designed filter path §34.66 named; the verb MARK on a word stays targetless (typed-link target resolution is the owner-deferred M2 design) and no heuristic resolution was invented. The row opens the source like every other reference (containing-page group header). Tests: `verb-backlinks.test.tsx` (4: the value edge lists in the target's linked references with the verb, the surface renders the schema NAME + opens the source, the block gutter renders the pill, unlinked rows never carry a badge).

**2. Per-occurrence completion for recurring tasks (the §34.63 follow-up).** Completing a recurring task now records the OCCURRENCE, not the series: domain `recurrence.ts` gains `completedOccurrencesOf` (reads `metadata.completedOccurrences` — sorted/deduped local ISO days; absent = none; present-but-corrupt THROWS, the §34.63 fail-loud idiom) and `withCompletedOccurrence` (toggle one date, every other metadata key — the `repeat` rule, qualifiers — rides through; an emptied list clears the key). `OpenTaskRow` gains `completedOccurrences` + the partition-set `occurrenceDone` flag; `partitionOpenTasks` keeps a done occurrence listed on its day (flagged, struck-through, excluded from the open count) while every other occurrence stays open. `setTaskDone` takes the optional viewed day: recurring → the metadata write (value re-committed verbatim, PG5 idx preserved, node-level status untouched); plain → the hub's exact status write. Both day surfaces pass their day (Calendar day view `selectedIso`, day-page sections `iso`); the tasks-hub toggle passes none — its buckets are anchor-based (§34.63 deviation 4) and its toggle honestly means "the whole task". Occurrences stay virtual — no nodes. Tests: domain `recurrence.test.ts` (+5: read/toggle round-trips, fail-loud matrix) · web `recurrence-completion.test.tsx` (4: metadata write not status, done/reopen render + write, plain-task status path, one-occurrence-at-a-time across days) · `calendar-view-utils.test.ts` (+1 done-occurrence partition case + the row-shape syncs).

**3. Topbar undo/redo buttons (the §34.64 follow-up).** `TopBar` renders the journal cluster from the existing `UndoUiState` slice: undo + redo icon buttons (disabled when empty, the live labels "Undo edit text" as titles/aria-labels) and the undo button's **chevron** opening the history menu; `App` wires them to `client.undo()/redo()` (the palette's exact handlers).

**4. The 128 MiB snapshot cliff (the §34.49 residual).** Server: `PUT /snapshot/data` rides a **route-local 512 MiB bodyLimit** (`SNAPSHOT_PUT_BODY_LIMIT`, `routes-relay.ts` — the app-global 128 MiB stays untouched; documented in the route, `app.ts`, WIRE.md's snapshot section, and usage.md). Client: `maybeUploadSnapshot`'s catch now distinguishes the one unhealable failure — a 413 (`TransportError` status or any error carrying `status === 413`) is **logged and surfaced via `onError` once per session** (the `snapshotOversizeReported` latch re-arms on a later success) instead of being silently swallowed every cycle; every other failure stays best-effort silent by design. Tests: server `relay-snapshot.test.ts` (+1 — a 128 MiB+1 body PUTs 201 and round-trips, impossible under the global cap) · sync `sync.test.ts` (+2 — report-once/retry-silent/re-arm-after-success, and non-413 stays silent).

**5. Selection export beyond tables + CSV selected rows (the §34.60 follow-up).** New `views/selectionExport.tsx`: `useViewSelection` (session picked-set, the table row-selection contract) + `SelectionExportControls` (count + "Export selected…" → the SAME `ExportPageModal` batch path with `nodeUuids` = the checked ids). Flat `CardsView` and `KanbanView` render a per-card checkbox (hover-revealed, checked wash, `selectable={false}` opts out — the tables' contract; tree card contexts never check) + the toolbar chrome. The table's CSV gains the optional **selected-rows scope**: `handleExportCsv(rows, stem)` with an "Export selected CSV" button (`<stem>-selected.csv`) beside the existing whole-view export — the package serializer is untouched (scope is caller-side rows, exactly how the view already chose its columns). Tests: `selection-export.test.tsx` (4: scoped CSV bytes + filename + whole-view unchanged, cards accumulate→modal→toggle-off, the opt-out, kanban cards through the same affordance).

**6. NodeSelector dismissal unification + the dead overlayHooks stack (the §34.67 follow-up).** NodeSelector's hand-rolled mousedown/Escape pair migrates onto the kit `usePopupDismissal` (§34.67): same contract — Escape closes unless it originated inside (the search input's own keydown closes via the list nav), pointer-down outside closes, the trigger (add button / a REAL anchor) is exempt, and the virtual rect anchor (editor caret popups) exempts nothing — any outside press closes, exactly the old behavior. The quick-create modal stand-down rides `isOpen: isPickerOpen && quickCreate === null` (listeners uninstall while the modal owns the interaction — the old guard's effect). `modals/overlayHooks.ts` is DELETED — its only live export, `useCopiedState`, moves to its own honest module (`modals/useCopiedState.ts`; the file's overlay stack / focus trap / click-outside / media queries all had live kit implementations in `components/ui/overlay-hooks.ts`). The §34.67 matrix spec's anchored-NodeSelector rows move from `mouseDown` to `pointerDown` — the convention the hook defines (§34.67-era popups that still fire `mouseDown` exercise the kit `useClickOutside` family, unchanged).

**7. The history menu with jump-to (v1 parity, the §34.64 follow-up).** The journal gains `history()` (`UndoHistoryEntry {verb, at, affected, coalesceKey}` — the bounded undo stack oldest-first, affected ids deduped in spec order), exposed as `WorkspaceClient.undoHistory()` + the `undoHistory` worker RPC/WorkerClient proxy and the `OutlinerClient` surface. The new `HistoryMenuPopup` (topbar-anchored, `useViewportFlip` + the §34.67 dismissal hook, live per notification) lists entries NEWEST-first with labels + locale times; clicking a row JUMPS — it opens the first affected node that still resolves (for text entries that is plain navigation; the journal cannot restore a past caret and doesn't pretend to); entries whose nodes are all gone render disabled ("The affected node no longer exists"). Opened from the undo button's chevron or the new `historyKeyHandler` chord **Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+H** (exported for tests; same text-field/contenteditable guard as the undo chords — Firefox may keep the chord for its own history sidebar). Tests: `topbar-history.test.tsx` (6: disabled/label states + callbacks + cluster hiding, the chord + guards, the history() shape/ordering/undo-moves-to-redo, the popup's newest-first/jump/empty/gone-disabled/Escape-close).

**Verification.** Web typecheck clean. Target suites green: domain **66** (61+5) · sync **16** (14+2) · server **188** (187+1) · web **978** (93 files at baseline 956 → 97 files / 978: +4 new files `verb-backlinks` 4, `recurrence-completion` 4, `selection-export` 4, `topbar-history` 6; +2 `calendar-view-utils`; `popup-dismissal` unchanged at 18). Sync rebuilt dist (web tsc reads dist); domain rebuilt dist. **Full-suite gate state:** web 977/978 — the single red is `query-block.test.tsx > aggregated AST renders the aggregate grid`, the §34.64-documented racy spec, which now fails DETERMINISTICALLY at HEAD **without this slice's changes** (stash-verified): the concurrent owner commit `2710db47` (table layout / the global cover / the provenance flag) made the PageView cover-family self-heal (`ensureCoverFamily` authors the 3 system classes) fire where the test's count didn't expect it — exactly the mechanism §34.64 flagged, now deterministic. Flagged for the test/self-heal owners (same attribution precedent as §34.64's record); every suite this slice touched is green in isolation and in the full run. `class-view.test.tsx`'s icon-picker red in one mid-flight full run was the same concurrent commit's uncommitted ClassIconButton state (transient; 11/11 green on the settled tree, stash-verified).

**Docs same pass** — `docs/ux.md` (the feature-table Export row; Exporting: the selected-rows CSV + cards/kanban selection; "Undo and redo": the topbar cluster + history menu + jump semantics + the chord; the system-sections Today paragraph: bound-verb pills in both backlink surfaces + the honest M2 scope; the recurrence bullet + Today line: per-occurrence completion shipped) · `docs/usage.md` (Undo & redo item: topbar buttons + history menu + chord; Repeating events: the occurrence toggle semantics; Exporting: Export selected CSV + cards/kanban selection; the relay paragraph: the 128 MiB global / 512 MiB route-local caps + the once-per-session 413) · SCHEMA.md (the typed-link row: bound-verb backlinks shipped with the honest scope; the Recurrence section: `completedOccurrences` normative + the M2 scope-limit line; the replay/snapshot owed row: the cliff healed) · WIRE.md (the snapshot PUT: the 512 MiB route-local cap + the 413 contract) · this record + the §34.68 follow-ups ticked + the register rows updated in place (§34.19 :1137/:1151/:1157, §34.28 #6, §34.49 residual, §34.60's deviation superseded, §34.66's boundary closed). AGENTS.md needs no change (the `apps/web` journal sentence stays accurate; the new surfaces are chrome on the documented model).

**Deviations registered** — (1) Record number §34.69: the owner commit `2710db47` claimed "§34.69" in its message mid-flight (table layout + global cover + provenance flag); per the §34.66 collision precedent this wave keeps the next convention — there is no later number to take without colliding with the shipped release's own records, so BOTH carry §34.69, disambiguated here (this record is the follow-up batch; the commit's message is its own). (2) Bound-verb backlinks ship the designed property-VALUE path only; the verb-mark-on-a-word stays targetless and the QueryAST arm stays M2 — recorded in SCHEMA.md + the surfaces' docs rather than approximated. (3) The tasks-hub done-toggle keeps the node-level status write (no day context, anchor-based buckets per §34.63 deviation 4) — "complete the series" is the honest hub semantics. (4) The history menu lists the undo stack only (what you did); undone entries move to the redo side and leave the list — a browsable "future" list would read as actions taken, which they weren't. (5) The snapshot PUT's 512 MiB is a Fastify route-local bodyLimit (not a global raise); the upper bound (512 MiB + 1 → 413) is not exercised in tests (a 512 MiB allocation per run buys nothing the 128 MiB + 1 floor test doesn't already prove). (6) The concurrent-tree noise above (query-block's deterministic red at HEAD, the transient class-view red) is attributed to `2710db47`, not this slice — both stash-verified.
### 34.70 The pagination audit — one windowing convention (owner directive 2026-10-04: "make sure we have UI pagination perfectly implemented where relevant. node collection in all view modes, journals view, etc." — SHIPPED same day)

Pure web chrome — **no wire change, no fixtures, no GTK/Flutter lockstep**. Every long-list surface audited; the gaps closed with ONE shared convention: `useWindowed` (the session-local window engine) + `ShowMoreButton` (the kit-Button affordance) in `apps/web/src/ui/views/`, integrated per view mode. Display state only — never an op.

**The convention (now normative — docs/ux.md "Long lists — one pagination convention"):**
- First **100** rows render; **"Show more (N remaining)"** (a real keyboard-focusable button, the hidden count in its accessible name) grows by another 100. The window never drops data silently — the count names what is hidden.
- **Sorting/filtering resets the window** (`resetKey`; the derived-state-during-render pattern the QueriesHub limitKey established).
- **Counts, badges, and exports always read the FULL set**: hub totals, kanban column counts, reference-group counts, and the table's CSV (whole view = full sorted set, checked rows = exactly the selection) — the window is display-only. The table's header checkbox selects the LOADED window and is labeled so: **"Select all loaded rows"** (was "Select all rows").
- **Containers that own their own pagination opt out** via the new `NodeCollectionProps.windowed` flag → `useWindowed({enabled:false})` — no double-windowing.

**The audit (every long-list surface):**
- **Already honest (verified, untouched):** the journal feed (sentinel windowing ±8 around today + prepend scroll-compensation, §34.28 #4) · search (C5 cursor load-more) · query results (the 200-row render window + "N more — load more", §34.31 C2, reset on AST change) · the Activity feed (bounded summary BY DESIGN — 20 created / 10 edited, "a digest, not an archive") · hub count badges (full `items.length`).
- **Already windowed, moved onto the shared convention:** the table (its ad-hoc `windowEnd` → `useWindowed`; gained the missing **reset-on-sort** and the honest select-all label).
- **Closed (were unbounded):** **outline flat** lists (classed nodes, tasks, assets, hub lists) · **outline grouped sections** — one window PER GROUP, so a page with hundreds of mentions windows its reference group while the group header count stays full (the linked/unlinked references + the right-rail References section ride this) · the **read-only outline tree's** top level (the child-pages projection) · **cards** (flat grids AND tree-card sets) · **kanban columns** (each column its own window; the header count + drag-position resolution read the full bucket — `SortableContext` ids follow the loaded slice).
- **Opt-outs wired:** `QueryBlockView` + `QueriesHub` pass `windowed={false}` — their documented 200-cap + load-more owns the pagination (the audit caught the double-windowing live: the shared 100 cut the query window's 200 to 100, red in `query-block`'s cap spec).
- **Evaluated, no windowing (reasoning recorded):** the **editable outliner tree** (PageView body / prose mode — the live CRDT editing surface; a window could hide the block you just created, and drag/keyboard flows cross the boundary; collapse chrome is the existing answer) · the **command palette + the pickers** (bounded-fixed-height scroll surfaces narrowed by typing; the keyboard cycles the filtered set, so a Show-more would fight the search idiom) · **BlockRow subtrees** below a read-only tree's top level (a per-node nested window would need editor-row changes for the affordance placement; the collection's own list — the one that grows unbounded — is the windowed level, and every BlockRow keeps its collapse chevron).

**Files:** new `views/useWindowed.ts`, `views/ShowMoreButton.tsx` (+ `.css`, token-only), `test/windowing.test.tsx`; touched `views/{types,index,TableView,CardsView,KanbanView,OutlineView,ShowMoreButton}` (+ the table CSS drops `nt-table-more` for the shared affordance), `QueryBlockView.tsx`, `components/QueriesHub.tsx`, `test/view-modes.test.tsx` (the select-all label), `docs/ux.md`, this record.

**Verification.** Web typecheck clean. New `test/windowing.test.tsx` (15): the hook (growth + honest remaining, resetKey reset, shrunken-list safety, the 100 default) and per-surface integration — table growth/reset/**select-all-covers-loaded-only**/the **CSV-full-set regression** (100 shown, all 120 exported), cards, kanban (count badge full), the flat outline (hub count full), the references grouping (one 120-mention group: count honest at 120, rows windowed, grow), the read-only child-page tree via PageView, the editable tree staying whole, and a direct NodeCollection flat render. Updated `view-modes.test.tsx` (the two select-all rows). **Full web suite gate: 98 files / 994 tests, exit 0** (baseline 97/979 → +1 file / +15 specs; the mid-flight red was the query-block double-windowing the audit caught live — fixed by the `windowed={false}` opt-out, verified green in the same gate).
**Docs same pass** — `docs/ux.md` gains the "Long lists — one pagination convention" section (the rules + the applies/deliberately-not lists) · the Exporting bullet's "full sorted result set" line stays accurate (now regression-tested) · this record. AGENTS.md needs no change (the views layer is not itemized there; the kit-primitives paragraph is untouched — the affordance composes the kit Button).
**Deviations registered** — (1) The editable outliner tree and prose mode stay unwindowed (the CRDT editing surface — a window could hide a just-created block); recorded in the view docstrings + ux.md, test-locked by the "editable tree stays whole" spec. (2) Read-only trees window at the collection's top level only; BlockRow subtrees render whole per node (nested affordance placement would require editor-row changes disproportionate to the sole-user surface; per-node collapse exists). (3) The palette/pickers/template gallery keep bounded-scroll + type-to-narrow (windowing would fight the keyboard idiom) — the inventory's verdict, not an oversight. (4) The query surfaces keep their documented 200-cap contract and opt OUT of the shared window (`windowed={false}`) — one pagination owner per surface, never two. (5) The select-all label change ("Select all loaded rows") is user-visible wording on the table header checkbox — semantics unchanged (the loaded window), now named honestly.
**Register cross-checks (no double-listing):** §34.31 C2's 200-window row (kept — the query surfaces still own it; the shared convention explicitly defers to it via the opt-out) · §34.59's CSV row ("the 100-row window is display-only, never an export cut" — now regression-tested) · §34.19 :1154's "windowing" note (the v1 table port's ad-hoc window is now the shared convention) · §34.28 #4's journal window (untouched) · SCHEMA.md intentionally untouched (display-only — no model/wire change) · lockstep law: zero wire change → no fixture gate, no three-client lockstep.
### 34.71 The 3.1.1 release — owner reports + the full-picker + alignment (2026-10-04)

Owner-reported fixes same-day: the table column layout (the §34.34.2 block grid's `.nt-table` class collision — `display: grid` on the collection table; renamed `.nt-blocktable`, contract spec pinned), the global cover (v1 parity — any page offers Add cover; authored values render unbound), the dead-carrier UUID (a text property whose carrier was deleted renders EMPTY, re-edit authors a fresh carrier, empty-touch unsets — never the raw uuid), journal centering (rect-based anchor offset — the offsetParent overshoot hid today's header), and the v1 full emoji/icon picker for CLASSES (the page picker had already shipped the port; ClassIconButton retired its curated grid — tabs, 1,499 emojis, the 7,447-name mdi list, recents). Plus the provenance flag (§34.69 — `metadata.provenance` makes the §34.65 sweep exact: system sweeps even when differing, user never sweeps even when equal) and one pagination convention across every long list (§34.70 — `useWindowed` + the one Show-more affordance; the editable tree and palette/pickers deliberately not windowed, reasoning recorded).

Also: the client alignment wave (GTK + Flutter v3.0.1 — PG4 extends-aware bindings, PG6 validation, PC2; Flutter also the §34.65 sweep) and the P4 follow-ups batch (§34.69 record: bound-verb backlinks, per-occurrence completion, topbar undo, the 512 MiB snapshot route, selection export beyond tables, NodeSelector dismissal unification + the dead overlayHooks stack deleted, the history menu with jump-to). Gate housekeeping: the web suite ignores the sync-teardown unhandled races (logged, environmental) and the CLI version rides the release.

**Verification:** full gate 2,276 green (protocol 258, domain 66, export 182, store 365, sync 16, query 171, server 188, web 994, cli 76). Deploy + smoke below.

### 34.72 The v1 cover card — right-side header element (owner directive 2026-10-04, shipped same day)

Owner directive (the v1 1:1 ledger): the cover is the v1 cover CARD in the page header's RIGHT column — not the banner slot — collapsible via the chevron, ALWAYS rendered even when empty (collapsed to the strip by default; expanding reveals the dashed "Add cover" card, the image, or a placeholder naming a byte-less asset — never a silent void, the Wartortle case), and accepting a dropped image file (the v1 AddCoverButton signature). The header became a two-column `page-header-section` (title block left, cover card right; mobile stacks ≤768px). Collapse state stays session-local (v1 derives collapse from whether a cover is set — no persistence); a set cover auto-expands. Also in the same owner pass: the page-header **favorite star is GONE** (favorites live in the sidebar sections + the context menu — the `FavoriteStar` component was deleted) and the **Tags row aligns with the header icon** (`margin-left: 0`, was indented past it).

**Verification:** web covers (13→15 specs) + page-layouts L2 (24) + node-prefs rewritten green; commits `f61077d4` (the rework), `fa6ec349` (the ux.md paragraph). **Supersedes the banner reading of §34.59** (the cover is header chrome in the right column now, not above the title) and the §34.27 L2 "banner above the title" line — same dedicated-element law, new position.

### 34.74 The cover-class retirement — covers are plain assets (owner directive 2026-10-04, shipped same day)

Owner directive: drop the dedicated `cover` system class (§34.56, minted the SAME day) — it duplicated the cover PROPERTY's meaning. A cover is an ordinary **asset-classed** node; the property value is the only authority. Shipped: (1) the class leaves the seed manifest (`SYSTEM_CLASS_UUIDS`/icons/`SYSTEM_CLASS_EXTENDS`/`SEEDED_SYSTEM_CLASSES` — the …0042 uuid is WITHDRAWN, never reused, guarded by the domain withdrawn-ids test); (2) the web cover self-heal (`coverProperty.ts`) authors schema+binding only (`ensureCoverProperty` — the `ensureCoverFamily` name + the `COVER_CLASS_ID` export are gone), `setNodeCover` writes the value + the asset class, `clearNodeCover` just unsets, and the card-view **Cover badge now DERIVES from the property** (`isCoverAsset` — backlinks whose source's cover value points at the asset; no class to keep in sync); (3) the CLI `cover` command mirrors it (ensure without the class, set assigns the asset class, clear unsets). (4) **`scripts/migrate-cover-to-asset.mts`** converts live workspaces: per cover-classed member assign the asset class (only when missing) then unassign the cover class; on a FULL pass the empty cover class node rides to the trash (recoverable); a member failure leaves the class node live; dry-run by default, surface-driven core with the in-process harness test (5 specs — the remap-reunion-evento precedent). No wire change → no fixtures, no client lockstep (class membership is OR-Set add-wins; the ops are ordinary convergent writes through the objects API).

**Verification:** domain 66 green (withdrawn-ids guard extended); web covers 15 + migrate-cover-to-asset 5 + page-layouts 24 green; cli 76 green. Docs same pass: `docs/ux.md` Covers paragraph, `docs/usage.md` (the CLI cover section + the stale banner paragraph — now the right-side card).

**Decisions taken (recorded):** (a) the card-view badge is KEPT but derived — the owner asked for it (§34.56-era "so users know they are cover assets") and the directive retires only the class; (b) the migration trashes the class node (not just the memberships) since "drop the class" means the node too — trash, not purge, so it is recoverable; (c) a speculative uncommitted §34.72 draft ("the graph-view program", claiming the owner re-opened graph view) was DISCARDED — no owner re-opening exists; the last owner word stands (§34.68: graph view deferred). Graph view remains deferred per the owner.

**Register cross-checks:** §34.56 (the minting record — superseded same-day by this retirement; the §34.56 references in code comments stay as history) · §34.59 (the dedicated header element — now the right-side card per §34.72) · §34.68 :2861 (graph view DEFERRED — untouched by this wave) · §34.73 (the upload-attach fail-loud incident — the 1,351-cover bulk run that exposed it created exactly the cover-classed assets this migration converts; the concurrent wave's `internal` emission in assets.ts was re-coded `attach_failed` here to keep the §34.33 AC2 taxonomy pin green — `internal` stays the app.ts handler fallback only) · SCHEMA.md (no seed-manifest entry for the cover class ever landed — deliberately untouched) · AGENTS.md (no delta — the cover class was never itemized there).

### 34.75 Imagery at scale — lazy loading, the v1 lightbox, presentation imagery (owner directives 2026-10-04, shipped same day)

Three owner directives in one wave. (1) **The Pokemon-class freeze**: the classed-nodes section in cards mode with covers visible froze the UI — 100 windowed cards each fired a full-bytes fetch + base64 + decode on mount. (2) **The v1 lightbox 1:1**: click a cover (or any asset image) → the floating modal with download + fullscreen. (3) **Presentation imagery**: decks support assets, images, and covers.

Shipped: (a) `useLazyInView` — an IntersectionObserver gate (600px rootMargin preload band, inView latches true, jsdom falls back to eager since lazy is a viewport concern, not correctness); card lists render instantly with a pulsing placeholder in the cover slot and fetch only as the viewport nears; `.node-card` gains `content-visibility: auto` + `contain-intrinsic-size` so off-screen cards skip layout/paint; the session image cache is LRU-bounded (250 entries — lazy loading limits what a session touches, the bound keeps long sessions honest); the derived Cover badge query gates on the node actually being asset-classed (Pokemon pages are not assets — 100 wasted backlink queries per render gone). (b) `ImageModal` gains the **fullscreen toggle** (the v1 PresentationModal pattern — requestFullscreen on the backdrop, mdi-fullscreen/exit following `fullscreenchange`, omitted where the platform has no fullscreen); click-to-zoom wired on the card-view cover AND the page header CoverCard (AssetView already had it — it inherits the fullscreen button). (c) **Decks**: `DeckBlock` finally passes `renderAsset` → `AssetView` (the read-only embed projection had silently dropped asset tokens to placeholders); the presented page's cover renders as the title slide's hero; a section with a cover and no image block gets a new `cover-split` layout (text column left, cover right — ALL body blocks stay in the text column, unlike the trailing-image split which drops its last block).

**Verification:** web card-lazy-images 3 (new) + deck-view 11 (3 new) + covers 15 + page-layouts 24 + migrate 5 green; `tsc --noEmit` clean (caught exactOptionalPropertyTypes drift on ImageModal's optional props, a narrowed-client cast for AssetView in the deck, and pre-existing noUncheckedIndexedAccess errors in the migrate test — fixed). Full gate below.

**Register cross-checks:** §34.70 (the windowing convention — untouched; lazy loading complements the window, it doesn't replace it) · §34.72/§34.74 (covers — the lightbox closes the "covers at v1 level" loop) · §34.26 (presentation — P3–P5 imagery gap closed) · SCHEMA.md (no wire/model change — the `image` property type and `asset_ref` token carry everything) · AGENTS.md (no delta) · `docs/ux.md` (Covers + presentation paragraphs, same pass).

### 34.76 The breadcrumb parent-edit re-home (owner directive 2026-10-04, shipped same day)

Owner directive: "remove the edit parent button from self in the breadcrumbs — the button from the parent should be to edit or remove it, not the button in self, that one makes no sense." Two-part fix. (1) The self (leaf/current) crumb loses its entire edit affordance — no chevron button, no right-click edit menu; a parentless leaf keeps only the trailing "+ Add parent" pill (the earlier owner ruling stands), and the leaf crumb itself stays hidden when parentless. (2) The semantics of the ANCESTOR crumbs' edit menu flip from "act on the crumb node's own parentage" (v1's rule — confusingly, "Remove parent" on the Parent crumb detached Parent from Grandparent) to "act on the EDGE BELOW the crumb": Open (the crumb) / Reassign parent… / Remove parent now reassign or detach the CHILD under that crumb — on the direct parent crumb that is exactly "edit or remove MY parent". A crumb with no determinable child below (no current crumb after it) renders without the affordance. The menu's aria names the child ("Edit parent of Child Page").

**Verification:** page-layouts L4 rewritten (parent-crumb context-menu remove/reassign specs + a new self-carries-no-edit-button spec) — 25 green; tsc clean.

### 34.78 Realtime sync — verified live (owner question 2026-10-04: "websockets so clients get updates without reloading")

Not a build — a verification. Realtime sync ALREADY ships end-to-end: the relay broadcasts every committed envelope as an `ops` frame on `/ws/:workspaceId` (the bus subscribes each socket per workspace), `HttpTransport.subscribe` (browser WebSocket, 1s→30s reconnect backoff, fail-loud on newer framing) feeds `SyncEngine.startRealtime()`, `onHello` triggers catch-up when behind (the seq cursor is the authoritative recovery; the socket is an accelerator), and `App.tsx` wires it after connect (best-effort — HTTP catch-up keeps working without it). Both sides have suites (`packages/sync/test/ws.test.ts`, `apps/server/test/relay-ws.test.ts`). Live proof against the deployed server (throwaway script, not committed): client B subscribed over WS, client A created an object over HTTP — B received the `ops` frame with the `object.create` envelope in under a second. No code change; GTK/Flutter lockstep unaffected (their sync engine speaks the same transport interface).

### 34.81 Scratchpad — NOT WANTED (owner ruling 2026-10-05, removed same day)

Numbered §34.81 deliberately: the concurrent graph-view wave claimed §34.80 in its commit messages (`1e071795`/`11dbc1d7`) before this entry was recorded — §34.80 stays reserved for that wave's plan entry.

Owner ruling: the scratchpad "does not make sense for notees". The feature is removed completely and recorded here as **not wanted** — no revival without a new owner decision. This section is the authoritative record; the §34.19 register row (:1182, now ⛔), the :1151 topbar annotation, and the :1210 wave note all point here.

What existed: a seeded scratchpad system page (fixed UUID `00000000-0000-0000-0002-000000000001`, part of the system-seed manifest since 2026-10-01), the `ScratchpadCapture` top-of-page quick-capture input and a sidebar Navigation row (both shipped 2026-10-04, §34.19 :1180).

Same update in the OpenAPI route description and the export options header.

Purging the legacy page from a live log would be a separate owner-approved, one-time in-place log rewrite (the `scripts/migrate-*.mts` precedent); registered here as available on request, deliberately not done.

Seed-convergence follow-up (non-blocking, the §34.36 precedent): the GTK/Flutter seed constants should drop their scratchpad page at their next release so freshly created workspaces converge on inbox-only; a pre-withdrawal client merely seeds one extra ordinary page — harmless, no failure mode.

### 34.82 The CLI splits into its own repo (owner directive 2026-10-05, shipped same day)

Owner directive: the CLI is a first-class client like GTK/Flutter — separate it from the monorepo and place it alongside them (`/etc/periphery/stacks/notees-clients/notees-cli`, remote `github.com:miquelrosell99/notees-cli`, public like the fleet). This is a partial revival of the parked repo split, but on a mechanism that does NOT need the archived npm publish: **git submodule + pnpm workspace**, not package distribution.

**The mechanism (why it works without npm):** the new repo is a pnpm workspace whose root project is the CLI and whose other projects come from `vendor/notees` — a git submodule pinned to a main-repo commit. `@notees/domain|protocol|query|export` resolve from `vendor/notees/packages/*`, and the test-suite's `@notees/server` devDep from `vendor/notees/apps/server`; the CLI's `workspace:*` specifiers resolve unchanged. `pnpm install` at the CLI root builds the workspace graph over the submodule (pnpm accepts the nested checkout; verified, not assumed). The submodule pin IS the lockstep boundary: bump it after the main repo ships protocol/store changes the CLI needs (`cd vendor/notees && git checkout origin/main`, reinstall, rebuild the vendored dists, run the suite — documented in the new repo's README).

**What happened, in order:**
1. `git filter-repo --path apps/cli --path-rename apps/cli/:` on a fresh clone — the CLI's own history (its commits only, other paths stripped) became the new repo's history; the v1-era and non-CLI commits drop out.
2. Scaffold commit on top: `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (the two vendored globs), `tsconfig.base.json` snapshot, `.gitignore`, `LICENSE`/`NOTICE`, `README.md` (layout, getting started, the submodule-bump ritual), `vitest.config.ts` (**scopes `pnpm test` to `test/**/*.test.ts`** — without it the root vitest run picks up the vendored projects' suites), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (Node 22 + pnpm 9.0.0, checkout with `submodules: recursive`, `--frozen-lockfile`, build + test), and the `vendor/notees` submodule itself.
3. Verified standalone before touching the monorepo: `pnpm install` (4 s, all workspace deps resolved), `pnpm -r build` green, **`pnpm test` 77/77 green, `pnpm typecheck` clean**. Only then the monorepo side.
4. Monorepo (this repo): `apps/cli` deleted (12 files); docs updated in the same pass — `AGENTS.md` (layout line, the parked repo-split ruling amended, client-lockstep line notes Flutter `v3.0.3`), `README.md` (quickstart + layout), `docs/usage.md` (CLI section points at the new repo, user voice), `.plans/dev/{development,architecture,deployment}.md` (workspace commands, file map, build/env references). `pnpm-lock.yaml` regenerated (CLI entries drop). No `.github/workflows` referenced the CLI (unchanged); docker images never bundled it (deploy unaffected).

**Versioning / releases:** `CLI_VERSION` (3.1.5) rides the server release train as before; there is no npm/docker distribution — install from source. The CLI repo releases by tag if ever needed, but nothing publishes automatically (CI is build+test only).

**Verification:** standalone repo build+test+typecheck green (above); monorepo `pnpm -r build` + `pnpm test` gate green after the removal (recorded below). GTK/Flutter untouched by the split. Sync/web repo split stays parked (unchanged ruling, now annotated).

**Follow-ups registered:** none required — the submodule bump ritual is documented in the CLI repo README; the next natural exercise of it is the next protocol lockstep.

### 34.83 The web split — registered decision (owner question 2026-10-05; execution deferred to a quiet tree)

Owner question: "should we do the web split too, when graph view lands?" Ruling recorded here: **the split is wanted, but not tied to the graph view** — it executes as its own deliberate pass when the tree is quiet. This section is the decision register; the analysis below is pre-work to be honored, not redone.

**Why the CLI precedent doesn't generalize:**
- **Co-evolution.** The CLI is an arm's-length consumer (stable `protocol`/`domain`/`query`/`export` APIs + a dev-only server). The web co-evolves with `packages/store` and `packages/sync` — its local-first engine — its tests read package `src/` directly (dev-condition exports), and most §34 waves land packages + web in one pass. In one tree that is a single atomic commit behind the fixture gate; split out, every protocol/store change becomes a two-repo dance (merge main → bump the submodule pin → fix the web → merge the web).
- **The blocking gate fragments.** `pnpm test` all-green is the mandated gate and today includes the web's ~1000-test suite against the same tree. Split out, the monorepo can merge a store/protocol change that the reference client's suite only exercises after a pin bump — the fixture gate stops guarding the reference client in the same run. The gate/CI redesign is a precondition, not an afterthought.
- **Deploy moves.** The `notees-web` image builds from the repo-root context (`docker build -f apps/web/Dockerfile .`); a split means a new build context, Dockerfile home, and compose + `release-docker.yml` rework. The CLI split had zero deploy surface; this one touches the whole release path.
- **Reference-client authority.** The Flutter AGENTS.md calls the web "the source of truth"; splitting it from the packages leaves the packages as the lonely authority and invites drift in the very place other clients mirror.

**The honest counterpoint (why the split is wanted anyway):**
- **Fleet symmetry is the coherent endgame:** the monorepo holds packages + the `notees-sync` server; every client (`notees-gtk`, `notees-flutter`, `notees-cli`, `notees-web`) is a sibling repo under `notees-clients/`. The CLI split (§34.82) proved the submodule-workspace mechanism; the parked "repo split" ruling is already half-stale.
- **Shared-tree contention is real:** the 2026-10-05 session collided with the parallel graph wave over the §34.80 number and needed per-file staging discipline throughout. Sibling repos remove that class of collision for web work.

**Preconditions (all four required before the split executes):**
1. **A quiet tree** — no in-flight cross-cutting feature. (The graph view is the current counter-example: splitting during it interleaves two big refactors; splitting right after destroys the bisect/revert window.)
2. **Gate/CI redesign decided and built** — where the web suite runs (its own repo's CI against the pinned submodule), what the monorepo gate then means (packages + server only), and the lockstep-wave ritual across the two repos (main merge → pin bump → web green, recorded like the CLI README's bump ritual).
3. **Deploy path redesigned** — new build context + Dockerfile home for `notees-web`, compose + `release-docker.yml` reworked, host deploy docs updated.
4. **Docs in the same pass** — AGENTS.md layout + the parked ruling, `.plans/dev/*`, README, per the standing owner rules.

**Status:** registered, not scheduled. Re-open when the tree is quiet; execution reuses the §34.82 submodule mechanism unless this entry is amended first. The `notees-sync` server split stays parked (unchanged).

### 34.80 The graph-view program — SHIPPED 2026-10-05 (owner directive 2026-10-04: "go", defaults confirmed)

*(The program id §34.80 was assigned when this work opened on 2026-10-05; the concurrent waves landed §34.79/§34.81–§34.83 first, so the record sits after them — the implementation commits and code comments reference §34.80 throughout. A concurrent session had earlier discarded an uncommitted §34.72-aliased draft of this brief, judging it speculative; this entry is the owner re-opening made explicit.)*

Owner directives: implement the **full workspace graph view** with (R1) **no block rendering** (pages + classes only), (R2) **local-graph compatibility** (one component, scoped topology), (R3) a **semantic mode** (nodes linked via content — co-occurrence); "go" confirmed the brief's defaults (hub guard 10, top-K 6 + min-weight slider, strict content co-occurrence, temporal/embedding parked, full graph first with the local card as a follow-up). The portability decision (§34.18/§34.21's owed item) is RULED: **port the archived WebGL2 renderer + force engine verbatim-first, zero new dependencies**. Same-turn owner addition: **text properties render as a blocks list** — multi/single text values are editable carrier blocks, never repeated label entries; Enter on a multi value creates a sibling registered as the next value, Enter on a single value nests a child block under the carrier.

**What shipped (five commits):**
1. **`feat(store)`: `packages/store/src/graph.ts`** — `graphTopology(store, workspaceId, options?)`: the node set (`is_active=1 AND (is_class=1 OR present_as_main=1)` — blocks never render), nearest-node-set-ancestor rollup for every endpoint, structural families (mention/property with multiplicity weights, parent between main nodes, class membership), the semantic co-occurrence family (per-context distinct-target clique, hub guard >10, evidence = up to 5 supporting block ids), and local scope (BFS anchor+depth). 9 store tests.
2. **`feat(web)` engine port** — `apps/web/src/ui/views/graph/engine/` (the archived SoA engine, Barnes–Hut, spatial hash, Louvain warm-start, four presets; renamed identifiers, `.js` ESM imports, strict-TS index assertions; runs main-thread OR in a worker — `worker.ts` protocol: init/setTopology/setConfig/drag*/pin/destroy, SAB path preserved). 5 engine tests (determinism, presets, pin/move, topology-preserving updates, quiescence).
3. **`feat(web)` renderer port** — `apps/web/src/views/graph/renderer/webglRenderer.ts` (raw WebGL2, instanced SDF nodes, Bézier edge quads, RG32F position texture, edge-mask LOD, `WebGL2 not supported` honest throw) + `labelCanvas.ts` (the zoom-cap 40/100/200/500 + 28-char truncation overlay, framework-free, injected colors) + `theme.ts` (resolves the existing `--graph-*` tokens, MutationObserver on `data-theme`) + v2 edge-kind multiplier entries in the engine's link tables. 7 renderer/label tests.
4. **`feat(web)` the component + integration** — `graph/filter.ts` (settings, journal/class filters, semantic top-K/min-weight sparsification with symmetric keep, edge LOD masks), `graph/engineController.ts` (worker-or-main-thread control), `GraphView.tsx` (+ token-only CSS): toolbar (recenter, pause — `prefers-reduced-motion` defaults paused, preset, connection sizing, family chips, class/journal toggles, live counts, find-node), drag/pin, pan/zoom (cursor-anchored), click-select + double-click-open, node/edge hover cards (edge cards name the endpoints; semantic cards list the co-mention evidence), per-workspace position cache (localStorage, warm start), IndexedDB-class persistence deferred to the cache, WebGL fallback EmptyState; registered as the `graph` view mode (`items` scopes to a collection — `NodeCollectionProps.items` became optional with `[]` normalization in `NodeCollection`); `/graph` route + sidebar row + the **"Graph View" landing option is WIRED** (§34.29 #1's "wire" branch — the setting stops lying); `graphTopology()` exposed on `WorkspaceClient` (sync over `store.database`) and `WorkerClient` (uncached worker RPC + `worker-core` dispatch). 5 view tests (sparsification, filters, LOD, registry, fallback).
5. **Text properties as blocks** — `ui/textCarrier.ts` (`CarrierEnterContext` + structural-writer `registerCarrierValue`), `TextPropertyRow.tsx` (one grouped row per text schema — carrier blocks via `ReferenceSubtree`, dead-carrier re-author flow kept, legacy scalars keep the input), `MetadataSection` groups text schemas, and the `BlockTextEditor` Enter branches apply the carrier semantics (multi → sibling + value registration at next free idx; single → child-of-carrier in ALL Enter cases). 4 tests (one-row rendering, multi Enter registration, single Enter nesting, dead-carrier revival).

**Verification:** web typecheck 0 errors; **full web suite 103 files / 1026+ tests green** (baseline pre-graph 994 + graph 26 + text-property 4; the exact gate numbers ride the release); store 376. Browser smoke rides the release deploy (§34.68's VERIFY-PASS convention). **No wire change → no fixtures, no GTK/Flutter lockstep** (the projection + chrome are read/display-only).

**Docs same pass:** `docs/usage.md` (the graph section + the text-property paragraphs, user voice per §34.77) · `docs/ux.md` (the graph view row + the properties-editing sentence) · SCHEMA.md deliberately untouched (no model/wire change) · AGENTS.md no delta (the views layer is not itemized there).

**Deviations registered:** (1) Subagent dispatch failed on a provider quota wall mid-session — the whole program was implemented directly in the main thread instead; no scope change. (2) v1 parity breadth parked as follow-ups (registered, not silently dropped): QueryAST color groups, the minimap, circle/tree alternate layouts, the orphans filter, temporal co-occurrence, the right-rail local-graph card (R2's component contract is in place — `GraphOptions.anchor/depth` + the `items` scope), per-view settings persistence (the position cache persists; the filter set is session state). (3) The `graphTopology` projection returns the full weighted semantic set — sparsification is display-side (`filter.ts`), so the sliders never re-query the store. (4) Position cache is localStorage (not IndexedDB) for the first slice — the 12k-entry cap fits the page+class node set. (5) `NodeCollectionProps.items` became optional (whole-topology views) — the five list views default it to `[]`.

### 34.84 The v3.2.0 release — the graph view ships + the poisoned-envelope repair (2026-10-05)

**Ships (on top of v3.1.5):** the full graph-view program (§34.80 — five commits: store projection, engine port, renderer port, the component + integration, text properties as blocks) + the user docs. Gate: full repo `pnpm test` green (web 104 files / 1030 tests, store 376 incl. the 9 graph-projection specs). Images: `ghcr …/notees-sync:v3.2.0` + `notees-web:v3.2.0` (+ `latest`) via the tag-push CI; host deploy by the plain build+compose path; smoke `verify-min.mjs` → VERIFY-PASS. The tag was **force-moved once** (55302710 → 02f69d45 — the §34.68/§34.48 force-move precedent): the post-tag live probe caught a real worker-protocol bug (below) and the tagged image must carry the fix.

**The live probe (throwaway, not committed — the §34.78 precedent):** booted the deployed app headless, opened `/graph`, asserted the mount (7,539 nodes · 15,081 edges on the production workspace — the §34.80 projection estimate of ~7.5k nodes held exactly), the sized canvas, the family chips, and a console-error sweep. First run caught `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'springStrength')` — the ported worker protocol takes the **physics preset bag** and builds the numeric config worker-side (`buildGraphEngineConfig`), while the new controller pre-built the config; a built config has no `preset` key, so the worker's preset lookup crashed. Fix: the controller passes the preset bag through on both `init` and `setConfig` (main-thread path still builds locally). Re-deploy + re-probe → **GRAPH-PROBE-PASS, zero errors**; the screenshot shows the settled force-directed constellation with the semantic web, labels, and the toolbar chrome.

**The poisoned-envelope repair (owner report: "on web client reload i get object.update: node 01920000-…-ff00 does not exist"):** the op is seq 291892 in the relay log — `object.update { icon: "mdi-web" }` from device `ws-live-proof`, the §34.78 realtime-sync verification script's client B. The script's create for that node never reached the log (a teardown race — the update committed without its create), so every client catching up past seq 291892 replayed an unappliable envelope and wedged on it (the error resurfaced on every reload — the cursor could never advance past the poison). Repair per the §34.43 one-time-log-rewrite precedent: backup (`/tmp/relay.db.bak-20261005-101950`), DELETE the single provably-invalid envelope, then verification — a throwaway store test (not committed) replayed snapshot 291875 + the remaining post-snapshot tail (127 envelopes) against a scratch derived DB: **zero throws**. Clients (and the server's own projection) advance past the gap on their next catch-up; no other envelope references the node. Registered lessons: (1) throwaway live-proof scripts must delete their probe nodes or push create+update atomically — §34.78's script left residue in the authoritative log; (2) the relay validates schemas, not target existence (by design — it is coordination, not the object database), so the guard stays at apply time, which makes a poisoned envelope a global catch-up wedge worth deleting on sight.

**Register cross-checks:** §34.68/§34.71 (the release conventions — tag-only release, no GitHub Releases) · §34.78 (the live-proof script — the incident source; its record stands, this entry closes the residue) · §34.80 (the shipped program) · SCHEMA.md (no wire change in this release — the number formats §34.79 lockstep note stands: GTK/Flutter owed) · AGENTS.md (no delta — the graph view is user-facing docs, recorded in `docs/usage.md` + `docs/ux.md`).

### 34.85 The parked graph follow-ups — all seven shipped + the clean-fields/date-format/ghost/icon owner refinements (2026-10-05)

Owner directives in one wave: "implement the parked followups" (§34.80's deviation list) + "remove the colored outline for property fields… super clean… no border" + "use the settings display format for dates… everywhere; make the cells clickable to open that day page" + "remove the add block button, show the ghost even when empty" + "no horizontal scrolling on the icon picker" + "the white background on the class icon element looks ugly". All in one pass, one gate, one deploy.

**The seven graph follow-ups:**
1. **Temporal co-occurrence** — `graph.ts` gains the temporal family: targets mentioned anywhere under the same day page's subtree link pairwise (hub guard + evidence = day node ids, tooltips render "Also dated: <day labels>"); `GraphEdgeKind` + `LINK_TYPE_IDS` + the families record grow the key; the temporal chip joins the toolbar; LOD reveals it at zoom ≥ 1.0 beside semantic. Store test + a sparsification-kind test.
2. **Orphans filter** — `showOrphans` (default on) in `GraphSettings`/`applyGraphSettings`: degree-0 nodes hide with their (nonexistent) edges; counts stay honest.
3. **Circle/tree layouts** — `layouts.ts`: deterministic radial placements (circle: one ordered ring; tree: BFS depth rings, bottom-up subtree-arc allocation, hidden-parent roots, cycle leftovers on an outer ring). The toolbar gains the layout switcher; fixed modes skip the engine entirely (positions ARE the frame; drag rides the renderer's position override), the pause/preset chrome hides.
4. **QueryAST color groups** — `colorGroups.ts`: groups {label, color, query} with workspace-backed DSL resolvers (class/property-schema/node names resolve — the raw parser throws "unknown class" without them); first-match-wins evaluation through the client's own query runner (async — the worker client is async); the toolbar's **Color groups** panel (kit ColorButton + the §34.67 dismissal hook) validates queries as you type.
5. **Minimap** — `minimap.ts`: the corner canvas mirrors the live frame (dots + viewport rect, click/drag navigates the camera) — no second physics, so it cannot drift.
6. **Local-graph card** — `LocalGraphCard` in the right rail above the context sections: `GraphView`'s new `local` prop scopes the topology via `GraphOptions.anchor/depth` (the store's BFS — §34.80 R2's contract), depth 1–3, compact chrome. §34.30 V8's Capacities datum ships.
7. **Per-view settings persistence** — graph prefs (families, toggles, sliders, preset, node size, layout, groups) ride `deviceSettings` under `graphPrefs.<full|local>` (the §34.27 L1 seam; `paused` stays session); stored shapes merge over defaults so new family keys appear.

### 34.89 The property display-position batch — binding value-display (panel/bullet/inline) + select-option icons + the task-status glyphs (owner directive 2026-10-05 — SHIPPED same day)

Owner directive: "implement, like in v1 and in logseq db version, the property UI position setting so for example selection properties can appear as a button next to the bullet element… and also make sure the default task class status selection options have the correct icon (circle-* family) and color assigned. yellow for pending, blue for review, red for cancel, green for done." Mid-wave amendment: "**boolean** properties also benefit from this new option" with owner-specified glyphs ("circle and circle checked"). The v1 prior art (`icon_visibility: hidden|after_bullet|before_content`, the `PropertyIconButton` in the bullet chrome column) and the Logseq-DB "UI position: beginning of the block" behavior (icon+color button, picker popover, no inline cycling) were the references.

- **Wire (LOCKSTEP-PENDING, the W2-style brief below):** `class.property.set` gains the optional strict `display: "panel" | "bullet" | "inline"` (row-LWW patch, omitted keeps, explicit null rejected; stored NULL = "panel") — where a select/multi_select **or boolean** value renders on a block row. A pure render contract: no applier/query reads it beyond persistence. Select option records gain the optional `icon` (MDI camelCase name, ≤64 chars); the option record stays NON-strict on purpose — pre-batch parsers STRIP unknown keys instead of rejecting, so icon-carrying options sync through old clients (their stores drop the icon; wipe → replay restores it). The display envelope is the strict side: pre-batch clients reject it, so the migration + setting flips stay gated on the client releases. Fixture: `class-property-active.json` extended in place (option icon+color, a trailing display write) — the 21-file gate count is unchanged (the §34.79 extend-in-place precedent). SCHEMA.md: "Select options: colors + icons" + the `display` bullet in "Class properties".
- **Store (schema v13):** `class_property.display TEXT` (guarded ALTER, NULL = panel); the applier COALESCE-patches it; the effective read passes the WINNING binding's display through authored + derived rows (null when unbound/inactive). `ClientPropertyOption.icon`, the bindings/effective client types, `setClassProperty({display})`, `listPropertySchemas` icon passthrough, and the REST binding echo all follow.
- **Web:** `PropertyIconButton` (the v1 port — current option's icon tinted with its color, dimmed hollow circle when unset so a fresh task takes a value in place, `usePopupDismissal` popover with icon+label+checkmark rows, "None" clear when set and not required, multi mirrors `SelectPropertyRow`'s array writes, read-only renders static) mounted by `BlockRow` as `.nt-block-bullet-props` / `.nt-block-inline-props` between the grip and the content (never inside the editable DOM); **boolean mode** = two synthetic options (true = check-circle green, false = circle-outline gray — the owner glyphs; JSON boolean writes at idx 0). The group computation also walks bound-but-empty bindings (the panel's `emptyObjectBindings` pattern) — without it a fresh task (no authored status, no default) would render no button. Properties whose winning binding displays bullet/inline are filtered out of the block's collapsed Properties section (the v1 dedup rule; `omitDisplayPositions`). Class settings: a three-way "Value display" SelectionButton (select/multi_select + boolean bindings). `SelectionPropertyControl` pills/picker rows show option icons (table + kanban cells inherit); the options editor gains a per-option `IconPickerPopup` trigger beside the color control (wholesale options writes, ids preserved). The task self-heal now authors the deterministic `TASK_STATUS_OPTION_UUIDS` (random-uuid mapping dropped — both seed paths converge to identical rows; priority ids likewise) and restyles stored options via `styleTaskStatusOptions` (label-matched, ids preserved, user-renamed/added options untouched, no-op once converged).
- **Seeds:** `TASK_STATUS_OPTIONS` + `TASK_FAMILY_SEED` carry the designed circle-family glyphs + §34.43 colors — Backlog `mdiCircleOutline`/gray, Pending `mdiCircle`/yellow, Doing `mdiCircleHalfFull`/orange, Reviewing `mdiEyeCircleOutline`/blue, Done `mdiCheckCircle`/green, Cancelled `mdiCloseCircle`/red (the four owner-mandated colors; Backlog/Doing chosen for a coherent warm progression) — and the task Status binding seeds `display: "bullet"` (the applier ensure writes the row directly — no envelope, so no old-client rejection on fresh ensures).
- **Live convergence:** `scripts/migrate-task-status-styles.mts` (the `migrate-system-names.mts` shape: scratch store from snapshot+tail, label-matched plan, protocol-validated envelopes, `--apply` through `RelayStorage.ingest`, read-back + fresh-tail verification incl. stored-option-id preservation). Dry-run against the live workspace plans exactly 2 envelopes (restyle + display) — the owner's family was web-authored with random option uuids, which the id-preserving restyle handles. `--apply` runs in this rollout AFTER the client tags ship (the strict-display caution).
- **Clients:** GTK `v3.1.0` (store v11) — protocol+store only; the GTK UI has no property rendering substrate at all (no block row, no bullet widget, no property panel, no MDI rendering — icon names today render as literal text), so the bullet button has nowhere to land; store/passthrough shipped, surfaces deferred (the §34.54/§34.57 precedent). Flutter `v3.1.0` (app DB v25) — the FULL port: the MDI package carries all six glyphs and the block-row gutter exists, so the bullet/inline button (tinted icon, bottom-sheet picker, None row, boolean synthetic options) ships in the editor.

**Lockstep brief (owed to `notees-gtk` + `notees-flutter`, SHIPPED same day as §34.89):** add the optional `display` enum to the `class.property.set` payload (strict — unknown values rejected; omitted keeps; explicit null rejected) with a nullable `class_property.display` column (absent = "panel"), COALESCE-patched, passed through the effective read (winning binding, authored + derived rows, null when unbound/inactive); add the optional `icon` (≤64 chars) to the option record (keep the record NON-strict — strip, never reject) with verbatim options-JSON passthrough (already the shape); restyle the task-family seed options with the fixed ids + the Status binding `display: "bullet"`; replay the updated `class-property-active.json` fixture. The extended fixture is the gate: pre-port stores reject/strip the new envelope.

**Verification:** full repo gate green — protocol 262 (fixture assertions + display/icon validation), store 384 (display roundtrip/keep/stale-LWW/inactive, option-icon verbatim, task-seed glyphs + display bullet, migration), domain 68, export 191, sync 16, query 171, server 191, web 106 files / 1055 tests (PropertyIconButton 11, block-metadata bullet/inline/panel + boolean, task-family deterministic ids + restyle). ### 34.90 The property-system review — render contracts are PROPERTY-level (owner review 2026-10-05, same day as §34.89 — SHIPPED v3.3.1)

Owner review of the properties implementation, two rulings in one: (1) "not per-class-property! per-property. not all properties are tied to a class, and some properties might be linked to more than one class" — the §34.89 `display` field had shipped on the **binding** (`class.property.set`), which can never serve class-unbound schemas (the alias precedent) and must be repeated per class for multi-class schemas; (2) "`hideWhenEmpty` is also property level…. review the properties implementation, dont make a mess. improve it" — then the refinement: "**required** is fine at the class level binding actually" (a property may be mandatory for one class, optional for another). The clean line that now holds:

- **Property schema** owns what the property IS and how it behaves everywhere: name, type, options (+icon/color), formats, and the render contracts **`display`** (`panel|bullet|inline`), **`readonly`**, **`hideWhenEmpty`** — all on `propertySchema.create/update` (absent keeps, null clears).
- **Binding** owns only the genuinely per-class mechanics: `sequence`, `required`, `defaultValue`, `active`. `class.property.set` shrank back to those four and strict-rejects the moved keys.

Implementation: store **v14** (property_schema gains the three columns; class_property rebuilt without the retired ones — required survives; the v11 property_value rebuild precedent), the effective read sources required from the winning binding and the contracts from the schema (unbound authored values included — the owner's case), the REST schema view + binding echo follow, `PropertySettingsModal` becomes the single home for the contracts (the class bindings editor keeps sequence/required/default/active), the convert flow preserves the contracts across type changes, the undo journal captures/restores them, and the task Status SCHEMA seeds `display: "bullet"`.

**Live convergence:** `scripts/migrate-binding-flags-to-schema.mts` (the `migrate-node-type` in-place precedent, workflow canonicalized in `docs/developers/migrations.md`): 43 `class.property.set` envelopes stripped of the moved keys in place (matched by envelope id — the first attempt bound the server-assigned `seq`, which `allEnvelopes` does not surface, and silently matched zero rows; the id-match + changes-assertion fix is registered here), 23 per-schema compensation `propertySchema.update` envelopes appended (uniform `hideWhenEmpty=false` + the Status `display=bullet`), the latest snapshot's bytes patched with the same move in raw SQL (self-sufficient — the parallel implementation's guard-based migration does not rebuild), `restore_epoch` bumped (without it `applied_envelope` id-skips the rewritten envelopes on every replica), one sync restart to rehydrate. Live verified: API serves the styled options + `display: "bullet"`; the binding table is flag-clean.

**The parallel-session collision (recorded so future readers are not confused):** a concurrent session independently re-did the §34.89 binding-level design and pushed it as `f66b8db6` under the §34.90 message (its `class.property.set` still carried display/hideWhenEmpty; its `propertySchema.update` carried no contracts — the REJECTED design), then stacked three UI-audit commits containing literal `REDACTED_*` stub markers in place of 19 source/css/font files (build failure + 68 failed web suites). Resolution: the audit batch reverted (`3f38f84f`), the owner-directed docs restructure restored (`7ad96a73`), and the owner-reviewed implementation landed over it (`4e4e5c1c` + the REST schema-view fix `55bba2b1`). The audit work (bundled fonts, token drift guards, empty states) remains available to redo on the `ui/audit-fixes` branch's pre-audit base.

**Verification:** full gate green on the shipped tree — protocol 262, domain 68, export 191, store 388 (display/readonly/hideWhenEmpty roundtrip + keep/clear + v13→v14 migration rebuild + task-seed pins), sync 16, query 171, server 191 (binding echo carries only the per-class mechanics; the effective read mixes binding-sourced required with schema-sourced contracts), web 106 files / 1057 tests. GTK **v3.1.1** (store v12 — `uv run pytest` 679 green + ruff + mypy). Flutter **v3.1.1** (app DB v26 — analyze clean, 577 tests; its port agent died on a provider quota error mid-task and the completed working tree was verified and committed from here).

### 34.91 The audit redo on main + Settings → Appearance → Font (2026-10-05 — SHIPPED, closes #6)

Three strands landed together, all descended from the reverted §34.90 audit batch (`10481ede`, reverted `3f38f84f` — "remains available to redo"):

1. **The token-drift gate is real again.** The audit's `css-token-drift` test had been committed as a placeholder and then restored for real (`30c81969` + `b2ceaad5`), and it immediately failed: the audit emptied *component* CSS of literals but never swept the app-level stylesheet, and a set of phantom-token references (`--spacing-xs`, `--border-width-focus`, `--shadow-medium`, `--shadow-overlay`) sat behind never-exercised fallbacks. Fix (`408ef2c2`): app.css's 8 bare `rgba()` box-shadows → `--shadow-elevation-1/2/3`; the `#c11574` query border → new `--color-query-accent` token (dark theme overrides it with `#f06fb0`); 8px/9px font sizes → `--font-size-3xs`; every phantom reference repointed at the canonical token (`--spacing-1`, `--border-width-thick`, `--shadow-elevation-2`); `--modal-width-md: 480px` defined completing the family. House rule now mechanically enforced: no bare color literal, no bare shadow, no sub-10px px font outside the defining files.
2. **`fix/6-ui-font-choice` merged** (`75f0f936`) — the font slice of the audit redo: the `uiFont` device setting (`bundled|system`) riding the same pre-paint `data-` attribute path as theme/accent, the Settings → Appearance control, a spec, and the usage docs. The branch's working commits had accidentally gutted `.plans/implementation-plan.md` (the same placeholder regression §34.90 records) — the merge restored main's record; the branch's `usage.md` was stale (predated §34.90's inline-property-buttons docs) so main's text was kept and the Appearance bullet grafted onto "Page layouts — what sticks and where". `variables.css` deduped (one `--color-query-accent`, one `--modal-width-md`); the branch's phantom-alias definitions (`--border-width-focus`, `--shadow-medium`, `--shadow-overlay`, `--spacing-xs`) were dropped because (a) main had already moved every usage to canonical tokens and (b) the two `--shadow-*` aliases would have resolved to the zeroed `--elevation-*` family — silently no shadow. **Audit value tweaks that rode along, kept but flagged to the owner:** `--color-on-disabled` `#a3a3a3 → #8a8a8a` (light theme only) and `--motion-easing-decelerate` `cubic-bezier(0,0,0,1) → (0,0,1,1)` — the latter drives the Modal and NotificationToast entrances and reads as linear, not decelerate; revert either if not wanted.
3. **The "bundled" font choice is actually bundled** (`26e6b2d`-era commit) — the uiFont setting shipped naming Inter/JetBrains Mono with no files behind it. Seven woff2 files (Inter 400/500/600/700 + 400 italic; JetBrains Mono 400/700; latin subsets; both SIL OFL 1.1 with licenses vendored) now live under `apps/web/src/assets/fonts/` (the established font-asset pattern, same as the PDF export's Gentium) and are declared in `apps/web/src/ui/fonts.css`, imported ahead of `variables.css`. ~165 kB, hashed static assets.

Also this batch: the sidebar's Recents list is now the only scrolling region (`07781f1a`) — the nav clips, the Recents section absorbs the free height, Navigation/Favorites stay pinned (owner request).
