### 34.92 The main-thread SQL jank — profile-verified diagnosis (owner report 2026-10-05: "why is the app performance soooo laggy and bad" — DIAGNOSED; fixes 1–5 + the getBlockTree follow-up ALL SHIPPED same day)

Owner-shared **Firefox Profiler capture** (`Zen 2026-10-05 20.33 profile.json.gz`, Zen browser against the deployed `atlas:8378` web build, ~34.7 s window, 1 ms sampling, 34,649 main-thread samples) analyzed with the firefox-profiler skill. **Verdict: the page is not laggy, it is frozen by design accident — ~75% of the capture is synchronous sql.js (SQLite-in-WASM) queries running on the UI thread, triggered from React render paths.** Median input latency 2.1 s; the main thread is ~100% busy for 19 solid seconds after capture start.

**The numbers (evidence):**

- **Main thread utilization:** effectively zero idle for seconds 0–19 and 27–34 (largest sample gap anywhere in the capture: 2.5 ms).
- **eventDelay (input latency):** mean 2,229 ms · median 2,100 ms · p95 4,741 ms · max 5,405 ms — every input waits seconds.
- **LongTasks:** 11 `MainThreadLongTask` markers — six ≈5 s (5,097 / 5,035 / 5,029 / 4,930 / 4,927 / 4,797 ms) plus six 400–500 ms; ≈32.0 s of the 34.7 s capture sits inside LongTasks.
- **Where the time goes:** 26.0 s inside `sql.js Database.prototype.exec` (75% of the capture) · 23.1 s of that under the `listClasses` read · 23.0 s inside SQLite WASM stepping · native self-time: `__syscall_cancel_arch` 5.2 s (Emscripten per-row `read` syscalls) + `__memcpy_avx_unaligned_erms` 2.8 s (copying result rows into JS).
- **GC churn:** 254 `GCMinor` (~1.0 s) + 5 `GCMajor` (~1.5 s) — one minor GC every ~136 ms, driven by per-call row materialization (`mapNode` rebuilding every `ClientNode`).
- **Query-run shape** (contiguous `exec` samples split on >60 ms gaps): one 18.9 s stretch at capture start (startup replay + first renders), one 6.2 s burst at t≈27.8 s, and three ~327 ms runs at t≈22.6/24.6/26.7 s — the latter repeating at **exactly the 2 s `STATUS_POLL_MS` cadence**. Per-second exec share: 73–93% for seconds 0–19, then 4–28% in the "quiet" phase (main thread then idles in `PollWrapper`, so the quiet phase is genuine idle).
- **Trigger paths** (two representative stacks): `EventDispatcher message → React ($→NE→pE→J0→d2→xE→BC→_0→i0→Bs) → useMemo → rC → Bs/D< → listClasses → all → sql.js exec → step → wasm → read → memcpy` and the same ancestor chain's event-handler branch `Bs → uy → listClasses → …`; the 2 s-periodic runs go through a sibling component: `… → cZ → useMemo → rC → cZ/R< → listPages → all → sql.js exec`. The UI event that surfaces in the markers targeted `aside.nt-sidebar` with 3.3 s latency.
- **Capture environment fact:** the profile contains **no worker thread** (configuration requested `GeckoMain/Compositor/Renderer/DOM Worker`; only parent `GeckoMain`+`Renderer` and the tab `GeckoMain` exist) — the store ran **in-process on the main thread**, so every store read is a synchronous WASM query on the UI thread.

**Root causes, mapped to source (impact order):**

1. **In-process store on the UI thread.** `detectStoreMode()` (`apps/web/src/ui/App.tsx:124-130`) fell back to `WorkspaceClient` in this browser, where `listClasses()` is a synchronous `prepare().all()` over the whole `node` table (`apps/web/src/core/workspace-client.ts:1007-1016`), same pattern for `listPages()` (`:985-994`). `WorkerClient` (`apps/web/src/core/worker-client.ts:287-289`) exists precisely to move these reads off-thread; the capture shows it not engaged. Why Zen missed the `navigator.storage.getDirectory` gate is unconfirmed (worth a `console.warn` when the in-process branch is taken).
2. **Store reads from render, everywhere.** `Sidebar.tsx:206,209` calls `client.listClasses()` + `client.listPages()` directly in its render body; `BlockRow.tsx:165-169` runs `classIconMap(outlinerClient.listClasses())` **per row** in a `useMemo` keyed on `[node, node.classIds]` — and since the store hands out fresh object identities on every read, every client notification busts every visible row's memo → one full-table `SELECT * … ORDER BY COALESCE(name,id)` per row per refresh. Same render-time pattern: `CardsView.tsx:179`, `OutlineView.tsx:99`, `TableView.tsx:682`, `EmbedCardView.tsx:51`, `PageView.tsx:265`, `SearchBox.tsx:61,67`, `CommandPalette.tsx:239,448`, `App.tsx:1878`, `MetadataSection.tsx:148`, `QueriesHub.tsx:103`, `NodeSelector.tsx:346,517,547`, `PropertyView.tsx:76`, `CalendarView.tsx:306`, `ActivityLogSection.tsx:168,173`, `quickCreate.ts:117`, `DuplicatePageModal.tsx:63`, `WorkspaceSettingsModal.tsx:191`, `PropertyDefinitionsSection.tsx:81,94`, `QueryBuilderFields.tsx:35`, `PropertyConvertModal.tsx:294`, `views/graph/colorGroups.ts:32`, `presentation/DeckView.tsx:197,236`. The `iconFor.ts:13` doc comment already prescribes the fix ("build once per tree/render from listClasses()") — the call sites don't honor it.
3. **The 2 s re-render amplifier, unguarded.** `App.tsx:928-947` polls `client.status()` every `STATUS_POLL_MS = 2_000` and calls `setSyncStatus(snapshot)` **unconditionally**; `status()` returns a fresh object identity every call (`workspace-client.ts:2899-2912`) despite the comment claiming "fire React state only when the client changes". Whole-app re-render every 2 s → every render-time store read re-runs its ~330 ms query — exactly the three 327 ms `listPages` bursts at 2.0 s spacing in the profile.
4. **Per-query cost.** `SELECT *` (every column) + full `ORDER BY COALESCE(name,id), id` sort + `mapNode` over every row, prepared per call, single-stepped through WASM with per-row Emscripten `read`/memcpy. Startup replay stacking these back-to-back produces the 19 s freeze and the ~5 s LongTasks.

**Prioritized fixes (QUEUED — none shipped at record time):**

1. **One-line guard:** shallow-compare the `SyncStatusSnapshot` fields before `setSyncStatus` in `App.tsx:931-934` (kills the 2 s re-render storm; fix the stale comment in the same pass).
2. **Read once per store-version, not per component:** a subscription/context-level snapshot of `classes`/`pages` keyed by the client version counter (the `classIconMap` contract made real); `BlockRow`'s grip-icon memo re-keyed off a stable `classIds` string + shared map instead of `node` identity.
3. **Worker path mandatory or loudly warned:** diagnose why Zen took the in-process branch; surface a visible banner in in-process mode.
4. **Cheaper queries:** `SELECT id, icon` for icon maps instead of `SELECT *`; index on `(workspace_id, is_class, is_active)` (+ the `listPages` predicate columns); prepared-statement reuse.
5. **Stop re-allocating rows:** cache `mapNode` output per store version (also addresses the 254 minor GCs).

Note for implementers: even in worker mode the pattern still costs — `WorkerClient`'s `drainRefresh` re-fetches **every cached key** on each worker "changed" notification (`worker-client.ts:185-219`), so fixes 1–2 remain necessary; fixes 3–5 shrink the worker-side churn too.

**Fix batch 1 — SHIPPED 2026-10-05 (same day, owner-directed "proceed with implementation"):**

1. **The 2 s poll no longer re-renders the tree.** New exported `syncStatusEqual` (field-wise over the 8 `SyncStatusSnapshot` scalars) in `App.tsx`; the poll's `setSyncStatus` now keeps the previous state object when nothing changed (`prev => syncStatusEqual(prev, snapshot) ? prev : snapshot`), and the stale "only when the client changes" comment now describes the guarded reality. Spec: `test/sync-status-guard.test.ts` (identity-equal objects, per-field diffs, symmetry — 3 tests).
2. **Render-path list reads are revision-cached on `WorkspaceClient`.** A `listReadCache` map memoizes `listClasses`/`listPages`/`roots` per store revision; `notify()` — the verified single funnel for every local write (`enqueueLocal`), push/pull, remote batch, realtime start/stop, and bootstrap — clears it, so one full-table query per change burst instead of one per component call, and zero re-queries for renders that race no change (the 2 s poll case). Returned arrays are shared until the next notify; every existing consumer was verified non-mutating (functional filter/map/spread only — the one `roots.sort` in the tree is the graph layout's local array, unrelated). `BlockRow`'s grip-icon memo is re-keyed onto the stable cached read (`[node, classes]`, the exhaustive-deps disable dropped) — class-icon changes still bust it through the array reference. Specs: `test/workspace-client.test.ts` gains a "render-path list reads — the §34.92 revision cache" block (reference-identity within a revision; invalidation on local writes AND on the receiving side of a sync — 2 tests, 17 file-total).
3. **Docs same pass:** `docs/developers/architecture.md` §9 (Clients) now states the revision-cached read contract (one query result per version; read-only arrays).

**Deliberately deferred, registered here:** `getBlockTree` is called on EVERY `useOutlinerValue` render (`outliner-context.ts:273`) — the same per-render-read pattern, a bigger query; left for a follow-up slice so this batch stays surgical. Fixes 3–5 (worker-mode investigation + banner; cheaper column sets + index; row-mapping identity cache) remain queued.

**Fix batch 2 — SHIPPED 2026-10-05 (same day; fixes 3–5 + the registered getBlockTree follow-up):**

3. **In-process mode is loud now.** `detectStoreMode()` console.warns naming the exact missing capability (`Worker` vs OPFS `navigator.storage.getDirectory`) — the instrument for the still-open question of WHY the profiled Zen session fell back. New kit primitive `InProcessStoreBanner` (warning-token bar mirroring the BackendUnavailable banner, session-local dismiss, barrel-exported) renders from App whenever `storeMode === "in-process"`. Spec: `test/in-process-banner.test.tsx` (2).
4. **Cheaper reads, three parts.** (a) Derived schema **v15**: `idx_node_list_reads (workspace_id, is_class, is_active, name, id)` serving the list WHERE + ORDER BY. The first cut put it in the canonical DDL — the v7→v8 migration test caught the ordering bug (migrate's canonical exec runs BEFORE the rebuild ladder; pre-v8 node tables lack `is_class`) — final shape: `LIST_READS_INDEX_DDL` as a single source, a version-gated ladder step (`current < 15`, after the v8 rebuild block), the rebuild block recreating it for pre-v8 databases, AND an explicit re-assert on the snapshot-repair path (store.ts restore: migrate early-returns on a version-matching stamp, so the FTS-repair exec alone would lose it). (b) `classIcons()` — the narrow `SELECT id, icon …` read (no content blob, no sort) behind the icon maps, replacing `classIconMap(client.listClasses())` at all 9 call sites (BlockRow, Sidebar, Cards/Outline/Table views, PageView, EmbedCardView, DeckView ×2); full seam: WorkspaceClient (revision-cached) + WorkerClient `cachedRead` + worker-core `classIcons` case + `OutlinerReader`. (c) **Prepared-statement reuse DROPPED, registered deviation:** `Store.restore()` swaps the connection underneath cached statements (dangling "Database closed"), and the revision cache already amortizes prepare to once per version — not silently skipped, consciously rejected.
5. **Per-row `mapNode` identity cache.** `nodeRowStamp` hashes exactly the raw columns `mapNode` consumes (two FNV-1a-style 32-bit runs, ~64-bit space) — sound against the `recomputeClassIds` case (class_ids/tag_ids change WITHOUT an hlc/updated_at bump; a metadata stamp would have served stale membership); keyed by row id, no notify coupling, unchanged rows keep their `ClientNode` across revisions so one keystroke doesn't re-map the workspace. `mapNodeCached` backs every mapped read (the three list reads, `getNode`/`getNodeRaw`, `getChildren`/`getChildPages`, `getClassMembers`, `getBlockTree`) — single reads and list reads now return the same instance, which also stabilizes React memo deps. Specs pin identity reuse, the membership-bust soundness case, and classIcons tombstone behavior.
6. **The deferred follow-up shipped with it:** `getBlockTree` rides the revision cache (`cachedListRead` keyed by pageId + depth) — `useOutlinerValue` called it on EVERY view render; now once per revision, matching the worker path's cachedRead.

**Verification:** full gate green — store 392 (incl. the v15 fresh-create, v14→v15 migration, idempotent-second-migrate, and the corrected v8-rebuild index assertions), web 109 files / 1,072 tests (workspace-client 18, in-process-banner 2, the three mock-client suites fixed for the new seam method), workspace build green. The documented day-features teardown flake remains the only stderr (exit 0).

**Verification status:** full gate green on the shipped tree — web 108 files / 1,066 tests (the only stderr is the §34.91-documented intermittent sql.js-worker-teardown "Database closed" unhandled rejection in day-features, timing flake, exit 0); workspace build (`pnpm -r --workspace-concurrency=1 build`) green; `tsc --noEmit` on apps/web clean.

### 34.93 Group-by icons + the links-section tabs (2026-10-05 — SHIPPED, closes #1 + #5)

**#1 (bug):** group-by-page headers (query results / references grouping, `grouping.ts`) hard-coded `mdi-file-document-outline` — the daily-page case from the screenshot. The header now resolves the group-key node and paints `effectiveNodeIcon` (own → class-chain → render-state default). Row-level icons were deliberately left on the weaker `nodeIcon` resolver: routing rows through `effectiveNodeIcon` would add a worker-RPC cache key per visible row and `WorkerClient.drainRefresh` re-fetches every cached key on each "changed" notification — the exact churn class §34.92 just removed. The proper fix (chain-aware *synchronous* resolver, new client surface) is registered as follow-up.

**#5 (feature):** the page's reference area is now a tab strip — **Linked references / References / Unlinked references** — composing the `Tabs` kit primitive with a token-only `.nt-ref-tabs` skin (the ViewTabs visual precedent). Each tab hosts its `Section` inside a `Tabs.Panel`, so the lazy contract holds: an inactive tab never queries, a collapsed section inside the active tab neither. Empty tabs hide; an emptied active tab falls back to the first non-empty; all-empty ⇒ the strip disappears as before. The new **References** tab is the OUTGOING mirror of linked references: store `referencesWithRollup(nodeId)` (source-side subtree roll-up — recursive member skips trashed sources, mirroring the live-source filter; target = renders-with-document-chrome AND (top-level OR present-as-main child of the node), `mention` + node-typed `property` edges only, `typed_link` excluded as targetless, one row per target, direct-beats-containment). Client surface: `getReferences` + a roll-up-honest `getReferenceCount` (the linked-references badge stays direct-only — the pre-existing honest divergence, now documented in the method comment). Rows are a flat read-only outline of the target pages (source-oriented `ReferenceList` was the wrong shape for an outgoing list). `ClassView`'s shared mount is unchanged.

**Verification:** store 400 (+8: ordering/depth, per-target collapse, document-chrome filter incl. typed_link exclusion, trashed source/target), web 1076 (+24: tabs contract suite, group-by icon assertions incl. a 2-class extends chain, worker RPC mirror), whole workspace green post-rebase onto §34.92.

**Register cross-checks:** issues #1 + #5 closed · `docs/usage.md` (Reference tabs bullet; the outgoing tab vs the incoming right-rail References panel disambiguated) · `docs/ux.md` (section list + honest-badge note) · no wire change (read-side derived query only, no lockstep implication) · follow-up registered: chain-aware row-icon resolver.

### 34.94 Hover preview + pinned floating editor for node links (2026-10-05 — SHIPPED, closes #11)

Hovering a node-link mention (the `InlineTokens` registry pattern — zero caller wiring, so block rows/embeds/whiteboard cards/decks all get it) dwells 400 ms (suppressed mid-drag, 250 ms pointer-travel grace) and shows a bounded preview card — icon/title/excerpt/backlink count, honest broken-mention fallback with pin disabled. **Pin promotes to a floating editor**: sibling draggable windows (viewport-clamped, title bar with live title / open-in-main / close, re-pin raises instead of nesting) hosting the Revision-11 cascade — ClassView / FocusedBlockView / PageView `embedded` — each building its own `OutlinerContext`, so it is a fully independent editor instance; edits ride `client.updateObject`, undo/sync free. **Deliberately non-modal** (the brief's modal sketch was refined in implementation): no focus trap, no outside-press close — a modal would block the page and defeat "full editing like a floating editor instance"; focus moves to the title bar at pin time so typing is never hijacked, Close returns focus to the pre-open element. Known limits (documented): the floating editor edits the block tree fully but the title stays a navigation button (embedded mode's contract); page excerpt = title (title-is-content). Gate: `--nt-float-raise` allowlisted (owner-file rot-check) + the rot-check regex now also accepts React inline-style setters — literal/color/font checks untouched.

**Verification:** web 1094 (+18, fake-timer DOM specs over the in-process client), whole workspace green post-rebase onto §34.93.

**Register cross-checks:** issue #11 closed · `docs/usage.md` item "Node links — open, peek, pin" · no wire change.

### 34.95 Node aliases — alias pages rolling up to the main page (2026-10-05 — SHIPPED, closes #7)

The issue's "aliases is a system property of type node, restricted to pages" ships as the seeded **Alias of** schema (reserved `…000000000029`, single-value `object`, global scope, no `targetClassFilter` — "page" is a render state, not a class, so the filter cannot express it). The alias page points at its **main** (one-way); text aliases (`…0028`, §34.32) are untouched and both kinds coexist.

- **Roll-up**: `getLinkedReferences` unions the inbound edges of every alias page (`aliasPageIdsOf`, query-time over the derived edge index — no derived-schema change; own-subtree excluded relative to both main and alias); `ReferenceEntry.kind` gained `"alias"`. The alias's own view lists only its own edges — the "see only links related to that alias" half is free. `getBacklinkCount` stays materialized/direct (badge-vs-list divergence documented).
- **Navigation**: mention-open resolves alias→main in BlockRow, BlockTextEditor, DeckView, and the app-wide NodeLinkMenuHost (`resolveAliasOpen` — chain-collapsing, cycle-safe; §34.94's hover/floating hosts resolve at the same seam). Opening the alias *itself* keeps its own view + an **Alias of \<main\>** chip (Pill primitive).
- **Name equivalence**: alias titles join the main page's names in `resolveNodeByName` + unlinked references (`nameEquivalentsOf`).
- **Page restriction — client-enforced, documented honestly in SCHEMA.md** (PC1-style convention; the applier polices nothing): the properties row renders for document-chrome carriers only, the Add-property picker hides it for non-pages, the alias picker offers pages only, and `linkNode` rejects a non-page target with a visible error and no write. One deliberate product call: the unvalued Alias-of row is **synthesized for pages** — otherwise a global unbound object schema renders no row and a first alias is unauthorable.

**Known limits (flagged):** server `/api/resolve` folds text-alias values only (node-alias title equivalence is web-client-only — server/CLI parity is a follow-up); the graph's reserved `'alias'` link type still has no producer (follow-up).

**Verification:** domain 69 (+1), web 1104 (+10 `node-aliases.test.tsx`), whole workspace green post-rebase.

**Register cross-checks:** issue #7 closed · SCHEMA.md "Node aliases" normative section (semantics, enforcement level, coexistence) · `docs/usage.md` item 10 (two kinds, roll-up, the quiet alias view) · no wire change (object-typed property values are existing wire).

### 34.96 Excel table export + import with update-by-uuid (2026-10-05 — SHIPPED, closes #9)

**Export:** `packages/export/src/xlsx.ts` — a hand-rolled SpreadsheetML writer (one worksheet, inlineStr cells, XML by hand, `fflate.zipSync`) beside csv.ts, deliberately outside `EXPORT_FORMATS` (the csv.ts/bundleMarkdown precedent). A **leading uuid column** is always present (the issue's round-trip point); number columns emit typed `<v>` cells, everything else display strings; XML metacharacters escaped. Specs unzip the built xlsx via fflate (the docx-suite pattern) and round-trip through the package's own reader. Web: "Export Excel" beside "Export CSV" in the table toolbar.

**Import:** `packages/export/src/table-import.ts` holds BOTH parsers (csv: RFC-4180 quoting/BOM/CRLF; xlsx: unzipSync + sheet-XML walk over sharedStrings + inlineStr + cell refs) — one package, one suite, both directions; the web modal stays a thin mapping/write layer. `ImportTableModal` (Modal + FileDropZone): header mapping (uuid column case-insensitive = update rows; title column can create new nodes; headers matched to property schemas by display name; class column assigns by name), a preview with creates/updates counts, then per-row writes through the existing client op path — uuid present → `setProperty` per mapped cell (empty unsets); no uuid → `createObject` + `assignClass`. Multi-value columns write the **array-at-idx-0** shape (the pickers/Kanban precedent); select columns match option labels to stored ids; object columns resolve via `resolveNodeByName`; dates route through `ensureDateChain`. Per-cell failures collect into a final report — good cells still import, nothing is guessed. One beyond-the-letter call: a non-empty title cell on a uuid row renames (`updateObject`), since an exported sheet carries the Name column.

**Verification:** export 218 (+27), web 1114 (+10 — preview counts, update-by-uuid write, empty-cell unset, create+class flow, label→id, failure report with good-row survival, xlsx import), whole workspace green post-rebase.

**Register cross-checks:** issue #9 closed · `docs/usage.md` (Exporting section: the round trip + the rename behavior) · no wire change · no new deps (fflate promoted to runtime in @notees/export).

### 34.97 Workflow rules — when X on nodes matching Y, do Z (2026-10-05 — SHIPPED v1 slice, closes #13)

The first slice of custom workflows is a **server-side rules engine** in coordination state — deliberately NOT a wire change (the prefs/shares/plugins precedent): rules live in relay.db, their effects are ordinary existing ops written by a server actor, so no GTK/Flutter lockstep is implicated and the object log stays the sole authority.

- **Model**: `workflow_rule` (trigger op type, criteria as QueryAST JSON, actions JSON, enabled) + append-only `workflow_run` audit (rule, trigger envelope, matched node, actions written, outcome — `actions_written`/`actions_failed`/`skipped_loop`/`skipped_depth_cap`). Criteria are strict-parsed, aggregation-rejected, and trial-compiled at creation; composed action payloads are validated against the wire schema at creation.
- **Evaluation**: post-ingest on the freshly-saved envelopes only, batched (criteria compiled once per rule per ingest; envelopes checked only against trippable rules), criteria probed per affected node via the `@notees/query` compiler against the server's derived store.
- **Actions v1**: `property.set` and `class.assign` — the latter is NOT a wire op (class adds ride a re-issued `object.create` OR-Set carrier); the engine emits that carrier. Rules act as client `"rules-engine"` for auditability; action ingests are re-entrant at depth+1, bypass the relay rate budget (seed precedent), still broadcast, and are evaluated once more.
- **Loop policy (the correctness crux)**: the `firedRuleIds` chain set breaks same-rule re-trigger (`skipped_loop` — essential because a `class.assign` action re-trips its own trigger); depth-1 cap means **one-hop chains only** (depth-1 matches write nothing, `skipped_depth_cap`) — termination is structural. Corollary documented: rules evaluate in creation order and a later rule can match an earlier rule's fresh effect against the same trigger envelope.
- **Surface**: REST CRUD + runs (`GET/POST /api/workflows`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/workflows/:id`, `GET …/runs`), read = any authenticated principal, write = owner/admin (shares idiom), OpenAPI-documented. API-only in v1 — no web UI yet.
- **Known v1 warts (in the runbook)**: a failed action leaves an unapplied envelope in the relay log (hydration retries-and-skips it each boot); rules are live server code with a fixed action vocabulary, explicitly not plugin manifests for the parked runtime.

**Verification:** server 203 (+12 — match→action, non-match→nothing, both trigger types, loop cap, depth cap, disabled no-op, failure audited + ingest unaffected, authz matrix, 422/404 fail-loud, restart persistence), whole workspace green.

**Register cross-checks:** issue #13 closed · `docs/developers/workflows.md` (canonical runbook, indexed from README + architecture) · `docs/usage.md` "Workflows" (honest v1 limits) + API-table row · AGENTS.md server line · follow-ups registered there: scoped-key admin enforcement if rules grow a UI; plugin-provided actions as a future extension point.

### 34.98 Task chord, v1 ghost block, class chips in crumbs + edit mode (2026-10-05 — SHIPPED, closes #10, #3, #2)

**#10 — Cmd/Ctrl+Enter toggles task states.** `taskCycle.ts`: not-a-task → assign the task class + set Status to the first non-Backlog option (Pending) → **Done** → clear (unset status at the row's idx + unassign) — the v1 `cycleTaskStatus` shape. Stored option ids resolved by label (never the designed UUIDs — old workspaces have random option ids); `ensureTaskFamily` before the first status write; the live class named "task" preferred over the seed id via `deriveDisplayName` (the old `/checkbox` probe `cls.name === "task"` was latently dead under title-is-content — fixed in both paths). Documented semantics: Enter with an open slash popup commits the popup; `Cmd+Shift+Enter` keeps the hard-break newline.

**#3 — v1 ghost block.** Every expanded editable block trails a muted "+ Add block" row (`__ghost-<parentId>`, aria-label "Add block") at the next depth, plus one at the page root; realizing one writes a single `object.create` after the parent's last real child and focuses it. Ghost ids never enter any dnd-kit `SortableContext`; none render in readOnly/embedded/prose/cards/table surfaces (an explicit `ghost` flag through `BlockRow` + `NodeCollectionProps`, forwarded through recursion — a prose-grandchild leak a new test caught motivated the forwarding). The §34.85 ruling holds: the ghost is the sole add affordance and shows on empty bodies; the old page-level italic button is gone.

**#2 — class chips in breadcrumbs + edit mode.** Chips are `class_chip` content tokens. Edit mode: they render as **atomic `contenteditable=false` pills** labeled by a `resolveClassName` callback plumbed from `BlockTextEditor` (re-resolved at build time; the wire token is never mutated — no log writes on edit entry). The label rides the prose projection (`el.textContent === proseFromAst(ast)`); the resolver threads through prose/proseSpans/applyTextEdit/spliceTokens/editableAtoms/signature/marks, optional everywhere so non-editor consumers keep the old projection. Atomicity: boundaries expand to whole chips and covered chips ride through both splice paths (labels excluded from flattened prose) — straddling edits no longer silently destroy chips, including zero-prose dead chips; select-all+delete preserves a chip (delete via the atom gesture). Breadcrumbs: `plainTextExcerpt` gained a `class_chip` case + a resolved-class-name crumb fallback for name-only chip titles; the lead-clipped popup now uses `effectiveNodeIcon` like the main crumbs.

**Verification:** web 1140 (+26 across `task-cycle`, editor-atoms, outliner-editor, system-sections, block-selection), domain 70 (+1), whole workspace green post-rebase onto §34.97.

**Register cross-checks:** issues #10 + #3 + #2 closed · `docs/usage.md` (Cmd+Enter sentence on the Slash item) · `docs/ux.md` (ghost + chip rendering) · no wire change · follow-up flagged: `withCandidateSpans` stays resolver-free on purpose (stored metadata must compute identically on every writer — documented in capture.ts).

### 34.99 Palette Random + focus mode + the class creation modal (2026-10-05 — SHIPPED, closes #8, #12, #14)

**#8 — palette Random section + refresh.** The command palette gains a **Random** section: 5 pages picked by Fisher–Yates over the once-per-open cached `listPages()` (asset-classed excluded), empty-query only, sitting between Recent and Commands and exempt from the seen-ids dedupe so it always shows its five. The group label carries a ghost refresh `Button` (mdiRefresh, "Refresh random pages") that re-picks **without re-running the sync-sections memo or re-querying the worker** (the v1 starvation lesson, now spec-pinned: the test spies `listPages` and asserts the call count is unchanged across refresh).

**#12 — focus mode.** "Only the editor interface, simplified": the `focusMode` device setting rides the pre-paint `data-focus` attribute path; **Ctrl/Cmd+Alt+F** or the palette command toggles, **Esc** exits (both yielding to text fields). Under it the shell dims (TopBar/Sidebar/right rail, token-only opacity, reduced-motion honored) and the page sheds its chrome — classes corner, header icon + picker, tags, cover, DayPageDateBar, PropertiesSection, SystemSections, PageFooter, block backlink toggle+panel, §34.90 property icon buttons — keeping the title row and all text editing. Scope call (flagged): the block bullet's class-derived icon and the row-end class pills stay visible; easy to extend if "no icons" is meant literally there.

**#14 — class creation modal + system-class deployment.** The Classes hub **New class** button (and the palette's **New class…**) open a kit-modal with two tabs: **Create blank class** (name, IconPickerPopup, color) and **Deploy system class** — the catalog (`DEPLOYABLE_SYSTEM_CLASSES` in `features.ts`, structural classes excluded) filtered to the seeded classes the workspace doesn't have yet. `deploySystemClass` authors the class at its **designed fixed uuid**, its extends edges (ancestors first), and its `SYSTEM_PROPERTY_SPECS` property family in one idempotent step (every step existence-checked; `task` delegates to `ensureTaskFamily`) — the same shape a server-seeded workspace starts with. The NodePills classes-picker create row routes through the modal with the query prefilled, resolving the created id back into the assign flow. Minting NEW seeds (definition/idea/place/project/trip from the issue's list) is a deliberate follow-up — seed vocabulary needs the convergence discipline, so v1 ships the 21 existing seeds.

### 34.102 Tailscale HTTPS serving on the fleet host (2026-10-06 — SHIPPED, ops-only)

notees on atlas is served over TLS inside the tailnet via `tailscale serve` (tailscaled terminates TLS; the per-hostname Let's Encrypt cert is issued and renewed by Tailscale itself — no reverse-proxy container, no cert files, no image change): `https://atlas.taila48da.ts.net` (443 → 127.0.0.1:8378, web) and `https://atlas.taila48da.ts.net:8443` (→ 127.0.0.1:8377, sync API). The sync API rides its own HTTPS port by design: its routes are root-level (no `/api` prefix), so path-muxing under the web origin would collide with SPA paths; and an https page may not call an http API/WS (mixed content), so both browser-facing ends must be TLS.

Host config: `.env` gained `NOTEES_SERVER_URL=https://atlas.taila48da.ts.net:8443`; `notees-web` recreated so the entrypoint re-baked `/config.js` (a plain `restart` does not re-run it). Prerequisite was enabling Serve/HTTPS certificates on the tailnet (admin console) — before that, `tailscale cert` failed with "account does not support getting TLS certs" and `tailscale serve` printed the `login.tailscale.com/f/serve` enablement URL.

**Verification:** `tailscale serve status` lists both listeners; over the tailnet name (via `--resolve` — atlas runs `accept-dns=false` and cannot resolve ts.net names locally): web `/` → 200; sync `/healthz` → `{"ok":true}`, `/api/version` → 3.2.0/protocol v3, `/ws/<uuid>` reaches the app (404 = workspace-gated, not a proxy failure); CORS `access-control-allow-origin: *` on the https origin; TLS chain issuer LE YE2, CN=atlas.taila48da.ts.net, exp 2027-01-04. LAN/plain-HTTP paths unchanged (`/config.js` + web 200 on 8378); `verify-min.mjs` smoke → VERIFY-PASS post-recreate (boot 9.1s, no console errors).

**Register cross-checks:** deployment.md §10 (recipe + caveats) · `.env.example` comment · no wire/code change — no lockstep implicated.

### 34.103 Tailnet port consolidation: the owner's :8378 habit, loopback publishes (2026-10-06 — SHIPPED, ops-only)

Owner requirement: notees must answer over HTTPS on the habitual port (`https://atlas:8378/`). Feasibility finding recorded here: the bare name can never hold a browser-trusted cert (public CAs don't issue single-label names; Tailscale only issues `<host>.<tailnet>.ts.net`), and tailscaled selects serve certs by SNI — for SNI `atlas` it **aborts the handshake**, so the bare-URL path is a hard connection failure, not a click-through warning. Shipped the closest faithful state instead: **the ts.net name on the owner's ports** — `https://atlas.taila48da.ts.net:8378` (web, canonical) and `:8377` (sync), with `:443`/`:8443` from §34.102 kept.

Mechanics: container host ports republished loopback-only (`NOTEES_SYNC_PORT=127.0.0.1:8377`, `NOTEES_WEB_PORT=127.0.0.1:8378` in `.env`) so tailscaled can bind the tailnet IPs on 8377/8378 alongside; `NOTEES_SERVER_URL=https://atlas.taila48da.ts.net:8377` — port-symmetric with the web URL, which also makes the web client's same-host `:8377` guess correct out of the box. Trade registered: LAN http on 8377/8378 is gone — tailnet URLs are the access path (loopback http remains for the serve proxies + smoke). Owner confirmed the green `:8378` URL working from a client.

**Verification:** `tailscale serve status` lists 443/8377/8378/8443; green matrix via `--resolve` (atlas runs `accept-dns=false`): web `/` 200 on :8378 + :443; sync `/healthz` ok + `/api/version` 3.2.0 on :8377 + :8443; bare-`atlas` SNI confirmed aborting at TLS (documented impossibility, not a config bug); loopback http://localhost:8377/8378 200; `verify-min.mjs` → VERIFY-PASS post-change.

**Register cross-checks:** deployment.md §10 rewritten to the loopback+serve reality (bare-name law, LAN trade, undo) · `.env.example` port + URL comments · owner preference recorded: habitual-port HTTPS via the ts.net name; a self-signed in-container cert vault for the bare name is the documented fallback if ever wanted · no wire/code change.
